+#[test]
+fn hidden_query_count_is_dev_console_only() {
+ let project=Project::new();fs::write(project.0.join("snapback/schema.q"),format!("{SOURCE}\nquery page(c: cursor ?):\n  return rows first 10 by .id after c\n")).unwrap();
+ let dev=Host::start(project,true,false);
+ let seed=dev.post("/seed",json!([{"table":"rows","row":{"id":"hidden","owner":"dev:bob"}}]));assert_eq!(seed["seeded"],1);
+ let reply=dev.post("/q/page",json!({"args":{"c":null}}));assert_eq!(reply["data"],json!([]));assert!(!reply.to_string().contains("hidden"));
+ let until=Instant::now()+Duration::from_secs(5);
+ loop {let logs=fs::read_to_string(&dev.stderr).unwrap();if logs.contains("dev query: page hidden: 1") {break;}assert!(Instant::now()<until,"{logs}");std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(10));}
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn bare_persona_arguments_refuse_only_on_the_dev_door() {
+ let dev=Host::start(Project::new(),true,false);
+ let bare=dev.request("add","alice","x-snapback-persona: alice");assert_eq!(bare["why"]["code"],"E_INPUT","{bare}");assert!(bare["why"]["message"].as_str().unwrap().contains("dev:alice"));
+ assert_eq!(dev.request("add","dev:alice","x-snapback-persona: alice")["state"],"sent");
+ let prod=Host::start(Project::new(),false,false);
+ let minted=Command::new(BIN).current_dir(&prod.project.0).args(["token","--service","mp-test","--release","release"]).output().unwrap();assert!(minted.status.success());
+ let token=String::from_utf8(minted.stdout).unwrap();
+ let raw=prod.request("add","alice",&format!("Authorization: Bearer {}",token.trim()));assert_eq!(raw["why"]["code"],"E_RULE","production must apply its table rule, not dev persona validation: {raw}");
+}
+#[test]
+fn fixture_queries_print_hidden_counts_and_persona_arguments_have_the_same_guard() {
+ let project=Project::new();fs::write(project.0.join("snapback/schema.q"),format!("{SOURCE}query point(id: rows):\n  return rows[id]\n")).unwrap();
+ let fixture=project.0.join("fixture");fs::create_dir(&fixture).unwrap();
+ fs::write(fixture.join("rows.jsonl"),"{\"id\":\"hidden\",\"owner\":\"dev:bob\"}\n").unwrap();
+ fs::write(fixture.join("test.cases.jsonl"),"{\"as\":\"alice\",\"op\":\"point\",\"args\":{\"id\":\"hidden\"},\"expect\":\"complete\",\"data\":null}\n{\"as\":\"alice\",\"op\":\"add\",\"args\":{\"owner\":\"alice\"},\"expect\":\"E_INPUT\"}\n").unwrap();
+ let out=Command::new(BIN).current_dir(&project.0).args(["test","--fixture","fixture"]).output().unwrap();assert!(out.status.success(),"{out:?}");let text=String::from_utf8(out.stdout).unwrap();assert!(text.contains("hidden: 1"),"{text}");assert!(text.contains("E_INPUT"),"{text}");
+}

fn empty_read(table: &str, site: &Site) -> ReadExpr {
    ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: BTreeMap::new(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: table.to_owned(), keys: Vec::new(), seeks: Vec::new(), filter: None, index: None, range: None, matching: None, site: site.clone() }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#4 — program guards observe the caller's reads, never global absence.
fn unreadable_guards(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, programs: &[&ProgramDecl], notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    fn expand_shorthand(expr: &mut Expr) {
        if let Expr::Object { fields, .. } | Expr::Write { fields, .. } = expr {
            for field in fields {
                if field.value.is_none() && !field.spread { field.value = Some(Expr::Name { name: field.name.clone(), site: field.site.clone() }); }
            }
        }
    }
    fn analysis_expr(value: &Expr) -> Expr {
        let mut copy = value.clone(); super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut expand_shorthand); copy
    }
    fn analysis_body(body: &[Stmt]) -> Vec<Stmt> {
        let mut copy = body.to_vec(); super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy, &mut expand_shorthand); copy
    }
    fn fact_text(value: &Expr) -> String {
        let mut value = analysis_expr(value);
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut value, &mut |expr| {
            if is_viewer(expr) { *expr = Expr::Name { name: "viewer".into(), site: expr.site().clone() }; }
        });
        crate::print::expression(&value)
    }
    fn facts(condition: &Expr, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = condition && op == "and" { facts(left, out); facts(right, out); }
        else { out.insert(fact_text(condition)); }
    }
    fn readable(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, read: &ReadExpr, known: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
        let Some(table) = schema.table(&read.table) else { return true };
        if super::owner_equality::readable(catalog, schema, read, known) { return true; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable(); };
        let mut pinned = BTreeMap::new();
        // Unlike older advisory lints, proof cannot guess a non-id key: typed ids
        // may select byId even beside one same-width principal/composite key.
        let selected = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|index| &index.name == name));
        let inferred: Vec<_> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len()).collect();
        let positional_index = if read.index.is_some() { selected.filter(|index| index.is_plain()) }
            else { match inferred.as_slice() { [index] => Some(*index), _ => None } };
        if let Some(index) = positional_index {
            for (column, key) in index.columns.iter().zip(&read.keys) {
                pinned.insert(key.column.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| column.clone()), key.value.clone());
            }
        }
        // Extra named tests pin fields even when the positional prefix is unresolved.
        if selected.is_none_or(|index| index.computed.is_empty()) {
            for part in &read.keys { if let Some(column) = &part.column { pinned.insert(column.clone(), part.value.clone()); } }
        }
        if read.index.is_none() && read.keys.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_none()).count() == 1 && read.keys.first().is_some_and(|part| part.column.is_none() && is_viewer(&part.value)) {
            let principal_keys: Vec<_> = table.indexes.values().filter(|index| index.unique && index.columns().len() == 1
                && table.columns.get(&index.columns()[0]) == Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)).collect();
            if let [index] = principal_keys.as_slice() { pinned.insert(index.columns()[0].clone(), read.keys[0].value.clone()); }
        }
        let mut filters = Vec::new();
        if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { super::intent::split((**filter).clone(), &mut filters); }
        for filter in filters {
            if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = &filter && op == "="
                && let Some(column) = super::intent::column(left) { pinned.entry(column.to_owned()).or_insert_with(|| (**right).clone()); }
        }
        // A total self-membership key is witnessed by the probed row itself.
        if table.rules.read.disjuncts().iter().any(|rule| {
            let RuleExpr::Exists { table: target, index, key, equalities, .. } = rule else { return false };
            target == &read.table && equalities.is_empty() && table.index(index).is_some_and(|index|
                index.unique && index.columns().len() == key.len() && index.columns().iter().zip(key).all(|(column, value)| {
                    let required = column == "id" || table.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|kind| !matches!(kind, snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Optional(_)));
                    required && (matches!(value, RuleTerm::Field(field) if field == column)
                        || matches!(value, RuleTerm::Viewer) && pinned.get(column).is_some_and(is_viewer))
                }))
        }) { return true; }
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm| -> Option<String> {
            match term {
                RuleTerm::Viewer => Some("viewer".into()),
                RuleTerm::Literal(value) => Some(RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(value.clone())).quarry().trim_start_matches("viewer = ").to_owned()),
                RuleTerm::Field(column) => pinned.get(column).map(fact_text),
                _ => None,
            }
        };
        fn holds(rule: &RuleExpr, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> Option<String>, schema: &Schema, known: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
            match rule {
                RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => true,
                RuleExpr::Eq(a, b) => match (term(a), term(b)) {
                    (Some(a), Some(b)) => a == b || known.contains(&format!("({a} = {b})")) || known.contains(&format!("({b} = {a})")),
                    _ => false,
                },
                RuleExpr::And(items) => items.iter().all(|rule| holds(rule, term, schema, known)),
                RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|rule| holds(rule, term, schema, known)),
                RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } => {
                    let Some(target) = schema.table(table).and_then(|t| t.index(index)) else { return false };
                    let Some(key) = key.iter().map(term).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>() else { return false };
                    let plain = format!("exists {table}[{}]", key.join(", "));
                    let Some(extra) = equalities.iter().map(|(c, t)| term(t).map(|v| format!("{c} = {v}"))).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>() else { return false };
                    let named = target.columns().iter().zip(&key).map(|(c, v)| format!("{c} = {v}"))
                        .chain(extra).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                    // Positional spellings with multiple compatible index widths
                    // are not evidence for this particular raw-rule key.
                    let source = schema.table(table).unwrap();
                    let unambiguous = source.indexes.values().filter(|candidate| candidate.unique && candidate.columns().len() == key.len()).count() == 1
                        || key.len() == 1 && key[0] == "viewer" && target.columns().len() == 1
                            && source.columns.get(&target.columns()[0]) == Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)
                            && source.indexes.values().filter(|candidate| candidate.unique && candidate.columns().len() == 1
                                && source.columns.get(&candidate.columns()[0]) == Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)).count() == 1;
                    (equalities.is_empty() && unambiguous && known.contains(&plain)) || known.contains(&format!("exists {table}[{named}]"))
                },
                _ => false,
            }
        }
        holds(&table.rules.read, &term, schema, known)
    }
    fn guard(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, condition: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        if system { return; }
        let mut reads = Vec::new();
        let mut copy = analysis_expr(condition);
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
            if let Expr::Exists { read, .. } = expr { reads.push(read.clone()); }
            if let Expr::Binary { op,left,right,.. } = expr && op == "=" {
                if let (Expr::Read(read),Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Null,.. }) | (Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Null,.. },Expr::Read(read)) = (left.as_ref(),right.as_ref()) { reads.push(read.clone()); }
            }
        });
        for read in reads {
            let Some(table) = schema.table(&read.table) else { continue };
            if readable(catalog, schema, &read, known) { continue; }
            if notes.iter().any(|note| note.site == read.site && note.code == "N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN" || note.site.file == read.site.file && note.site.line == read.site.line && note.code == "N_UNREADABLE_PROBE" && note.message.starts_with(&format!("{program}: this probe reads {} under", read.table))) { continue; }
            let rule_site = rule_site(catalog, &read.table, Effect::Read).map(|site| format!(" ({site})")).unwrap_or_default();
            notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "N_UNREADABLE_PROBE",
                format!("{program}: this probe reads {} under its read rule `{}`{rule_site}; rows hidden from the caller do not satisfy a program exists, so using this value as a negative guard cannot enforce absence across those rows (table-rule probes see all rows)", read.table, table.rules.read.quarry()),
                "use a unique/cap/after insert constraint for an invariant across hidden rows, or authorize this caller to read the rows the guard must observe; keep this probe only when caller-visible existence is intended").guide("rules"));
        }
    }
    fn absence(condition: &Expr, inverted: bool) -> Vec<ReadExpr> {
        match condition {
            Expr::Exists { read, .. } => {
                let mut out = if inverted { vec![read.clone()] } else { vec![] };
                out.extend(absence(&Expr::Read(read.clone()), false)); out
            },
            Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "not" => absence(value, !inverted),
            Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "=" || op == "!=" => {
                let pair = match (left.as_ref(), right.as_ref()) {
                    (value, Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(b), .. }) | (Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(b), .. }, value) => Some((value, *b)),
                    _ => None,
                };
                pair.map(|(value, b)| absence(value, inverted ^ (op == "=" && !b || op == "!=" && b))).unwrap_or_else(|| absence(left, inverted).into_iter().chain(absence(right, inverted)).collect())
            }
            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => absence(left, inverted).into_iter().chain(absence(right, inverted)).collect(),
            Expr::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => {
                let mut all = Vec::new(); let mut copy = (**condition).clone();
                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if let Expr::Exists { read, .. } = expr { all.push(read.clone()); } });
                all.extend(absence(then, inverted)); all.extend(absence(otherwise, inverted)); all
            }
            Expr::Unary { value, .. } | Expr::Member { base: value, .. } | Expr::Assert { value, .. }
                | Expr::ShapeUse { base: value, .. } | Expr::Walk { seed: value, .. } => absence(value, inverted),
            Expr::Array { items, .. } | Expr::Call { args: items, .. } => items.iter().flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted)).collect(),
            Expr::Object { fields, .. } => fields.iter().filter_map(|field| field.value.as_ref()).flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted)).collect(),
            Expr::Shape { base, fields, .. } => absence(base, inverted).into_iter().chain(fields.iter().filter_map(|field| match field {
                ShapeField::Field { value, .. } => Some(value), _ => None,
            }).flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted))).collect(),
            Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Created { read, .. } => {
            let mut substitutions = Vec::new();
            for (param, arg) in helper.args.iter().zip(args) {
                if is_viewer(arg) || (!readonly && !matches!(arg, Expr::Name { .. })) { continue; }
                let mut marker = "diagSnapshot".to_owned();
                while used.contains(&marker) { marker.push('_'); }
                used.insert(marker.clone());
                substitutions.push((fact_text(arg), marker, param.name.clone()));
            }
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| {
                let text = fact_text(expr);
                if let Some((_, marker, _)) = substitutions.iter().find(|(arg, _, _)| *arg == text) {
                    *expr = Expr::Name { name: marker.clone(), site: expr.site().clone() };
                }
            });
            let mut valid = true;
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| {
                if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr && name != "viewer" && !substitutions.iter().any(|(_, marker, _)| marker == name) { valid = false; }
                if matches!(expr, Expr::Focal { .. }) { valid = false; }
            });
            if valid {
                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| {
                    if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr && let Some((_, _, param)) = substitutions.iter().find(|(_, marker, _)| marker == name) { *name = param.clone(); }
                });
                out.insert(fact_text(&condition));
            }
        }
        for (param, arg) in helper.args.iter().zip(args) {
            if is_viewer(arg) { out.insert(format!("({} = viewer)", param.name)); }
        }
        out
    }
    fn rebound_names(statements: &[Stmt], out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        for statement in statements {
            match statement {
                Stmt::Bind { names, .. } => out.extend(names.iter().cloned()),
                Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => { rebound_names(then_body, out); rebound_names(else_body, out); }
                Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => rebound_names(body, out),
                _ => {}
            }
        }
    }
    fn lookup_fact(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, names: &[String], value: &Expr, known: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        let mut shadowed = false;
        let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if matches!(expr, Expr::Name { name, .. } if names.contains(name)) { shadowed = true; } });
        if shadowed { return; }
        let Expr::Assert { value, .. } = value else { return };
        let Expr::Read(read) = value.as_ref() else { return };
        if schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some() || !point_probe(catalog, read) { return; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        let mut indexes: Vec<_> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) } else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() }).collect();
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && read.keys.len() == 1 && is_viewer(&read.keys[0].value) {
            indexes.retain(|index| info.columns.get(&index.columns[0]).is_some_and(|kind| kind.kind == TypeKind::Principal));
        }
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && super::owner_equality::typed_id(catalog, read) { indexes.retain(|index| index.name == "byId"); }
        let [index] = indexes.as_slice() else { return };
        let keys = index.columns.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(at, column)| {
            let key = if named { read.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)) } else { read.keys.get(at) }?;
            Some(format!("{column} = {}", fact_text(&key.value)))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        // A successful ruled row lookup proves the indexed row exists. Extra
        // projected-field tests are not raw rule evidence and are deliberately omitted.
        known.insert(format!("exists {}[{keys}]", read.table));
    }
    fn point_probe(catalog: &Catalog, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
        if read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() { return false; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return false };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        info.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) }
                else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() })
    }
    fn expression_guard_node(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, expr: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, observe_prefix: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        if !observe_prefix {
            for input in guarded_inputs(catalog, expr) {
                input_guards(catalog, schema, program, &input, known, global, system, visiting, notes);
            }
        }
        if let Expr::Exists { read, site } = expr && (observe_prefix || point_probe(catalog, read)) {
            guard(catalog, schema, program, &Expr::Exists { read: read.clone(), site: site.clone() }, known, system, notes);
        }
        if let Expr::If { condition, .. } = expr {
            guard(catalog, schema, program, condition, known, system, notes);
            expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, condition, known, global, system, true, visiting, notes);
        }
        for (name, shape) in definitions(catalog, expr) {
            let key = format!("{shape}:{name}");
            if !visiting.insert(key.clone()) { continue; }
            if let Some(inner) = helper_body(catalog, &name, shape) {
                // Parameters and focal rows belong to the definition's scope. Only
                // viewer/literal facts about the same world cross that boundary.
                let entry = if let Expr::Call { args, .. } = expr && !shape { call_facts(catalog, &name, args, known, global) } else { known.intersection(global).cloned().collect() };
                body(catalog, schema, program, &inner, entry, global, &guard_bindings(catalog, &inner), system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
            }
            visiting.remove(&key);
        }
    }
    // Immutable input-value traversal: candidate predicates determine a
    // returned row/page, not a boolean field received by a guard. Never erase
//! Shared type-directed positional point selection for programs and rules.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — identity wins; otherwise ordered compatible declared keys only.
use super::catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo};
use crate::{ast::{IndexDecl, ReadExpr}, diag::Diagnostic, types::Ty};

pub(super) fn candidates<'a>(info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, types: &[(Ty, bool)], synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>) -> Vec<&'a IndexDecl> {
    let identity = read.index.is_none() && types.len() == 1 &&
        (types[0].0.required() == Ty::Id || types[0].0.required() == Ty::Ref(read.table.clone())
            || (!types[0].0.is_optional() && matches!(types[0].0.required(),Ty::Row(t)|Ty::WriteRow(t) if t==read.table)));
    info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
        .filter(|index| match &read.index {
            Some(name) => &index.name == name,
            None if identity => index.name == "byId",
            None => index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len(),
        })
        // Explicit indexes keep their existing mismatch diagnostics and range semantics.
        .filter(|index| read.index.is_some() || types.iter().zip(&index.columns).all(|((ty, literal), column)| {
            let expected = if index.computed.contains_key(column) { Ty::Int }
                else if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(read.table.clone()) }
                else { info.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown) };
            ty.comparable(&expected) || (!ty.is_optional() && matches!((ty.required(),expected.required()),(Ty::Row(a)|Ty::WriteRow(a),Ty::Ref(b)) if a==b)) || (*literal && expected.is_identity())
        })).collect()
}

pub(super) fn refusal(info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, candidates: &[&IndexDecl], catalog: &Catalog) -> Diagnostic {
    let alternatives = if candidates.is_empty() && read.index.is_some() {
        info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>()
    } else { candidates.to_vec() };
    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.7 — `budgets['general']` (lane 020): a text value names the declared
    // text key far more often than a row id, so that named form leads (and is --fix's choice).
    let mut alternatives = alternatives;
    alternatives.sort_by_key(|index| index.columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id"));
    let mut changes = Vec::new();
    for index in &alternatives {
        // Same-column opposite-order indexes need the explicit name as well.
        let duplicate = info.indexes.iter().any(|other| other.name != index.name && other.unique
            && other.columns.len() == index.columns.len() && other.columns.iter().all(|c| index.columns.contains(c)));
        if read.index.is_none() { changes.push(crate::repair::ReadChange::NamedKey { columns: index.columns.clone(), index: duplicate.then(|| index.name.clone()) }); }
        // Unknown explicit indexes and computed keys retain verified legacy alternatives.
        changes.push(crate::repair::ReadChange::Index(index.name.clone()));
    }
    let message = if alternatives.is_empty() {
        format!("{} has no declared unique key accepting these positional argument types in order; name the key columns, or use where for a population", read.table)
    } else {
        format!("{} positional argument types leave {} candidate keys; name the intended key columns or choose a compatible unique index explicitly", read.table, candidates.len())
    };
    let diagnostic = Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN", message, "").guide("reads").repair_read(&read.site, changes);
    // The user explicitly opts into one context-verified choice with --fix.
    if read.index.is_none() && candidates.len() > 1 { diagnostic.mechanical() } else { diagnostic }
}
    // ----- reads -----

    fn read(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        if read.matching.is_some() && self.binding(&read.table).is_some_and(|b| matches!(b.ty.required(), Ty::Text(_) | Ty::Enum(_))) {
            return Err(type_error(&read.site, format!("`{}` is text; `matching` searches a table's word index", read.table),
                format!("for text compare `{0} = q` or `{0} != q`; for whole-token membership use `q in tokens({0})`; for word-prefix row search write `<table> matching q first 50 by .id`", read.table)).guide("types"));
        }
        if let Some(timeline) = self.schema.timelines.iter().find(|timeline| timeline.name == read.table) {
            let expanded = if let Some(plan) = self.catalog.feed_views.get(&read.table) {
                super::feed_view::read(timeline, plan, read)?
            } else { super::timeline::read(timeline, read)? };
            // @ref LLP 3000.013#14.4 — the scale certificate trusts only the scan this expansion generates.
            let outer = self.expanding_feed.replace((timeline.table("Feed"), read.site.clone()));
            let bound = self.expr(&expanded, out);
            self.expanding_feed = outer;
            return bound;
        }
        if let Some(shaped) = self.page_shape(read) { return self.expr(&shaped, out); }
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — a bound row of this table addresses itself, as in `update t[row]`.
        if let Some(by_id) = self.row_as_key(read) { return self.read(&by_id, out); }
        if self.is_page(read, ReadMode::FilteredPoint)? {
            let (scan, take) = self.scan(read, out)?;
            return Ok((Expr::Scan(scan), Ty::Page(read.table.clone(), take)));
        }
        let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
        if let Some(maintain) = &info.maintained {
            if read.keys.len() != maintain.by.len() || read.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| key.column.as_ref()).any(|column| !maintain.by.contains(column)) {
                return Err(super::maintains::refusal(&read.site, format!("{} scalar lookup needs the complete group key: {}", read.table, maintain.by.join(", ")), format!("supply every group key, or page the view: {} first 20 by .id after c", read.table)));
            }
        }
        let value = self.read_value(read, ReadMode::FilteredPoint, out)?;
        if let Some(maintain) = &info.maintained {
            // A maintained point read is the typed value: zero when absent.
            let row = self.temp("countRow");
            let tmp = self.temp("total");
            let zero = Expr::Literal(Value::Int(0));
            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: row.clone(), value });
            out.push(Stmt::If {
                condition: Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left: Box::new(ir::var(&row)), right: Box::new(Expr::Literal(Value::Null)) },
                then_body: vec![Stmt::Let { name: tmp.clone(), value: zero }],
                else_body: vec![Stmt::Let { name: tmp.clone(), value: ir::field(ir::var(&row), "value") }],
            });
            let ty = info.columns.get("value").map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Int);
            let ty = self.maintained_result_type(maintain, read, ty);
            return Ok((ir::var(&tmp), ty));
        }
        Ok((value, Ty::Row(read.table.clone()).optional()))
    }

    fn scan(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Scan, u32)> {
        self.scan_at(read, out, prefix::Position::Page)
    }

    fn scan_at(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, out: &mut setup::Body, position: prefix::Position) -> Fallible<(Scan, u32)> {
        let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
        let (keys, index, selection) = self.select_index(info, read, ReadMode::Range, out)?;
        let prefix = self.order_keys(info, &index, keys)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5.1 — one pinned parent authorizes all of its aggregate groups.
        if let Some(m) = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table).filter(|m| m.groups_by_parent(self.schema)) {
            let parent = self.schema.tables[&m.source].through.as_ref().unwrap();
            if m.parent_key.is_none() || !index.columns.iter().take(prefix.len()).any(|f| f == &parent.reference) {
                let fields = m.by.iter().filter(|f| *f != &parent.reference).map(|f| format!(".{f}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                let order = if fields.is_empty() { ".id" } else { &fields };
                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_READ_THROUGH_SCAN",
                    format!("{} must pin {} to one readable {} row before enumerating groups", read.table, parent.reference, parent.parent),
                    format!("inside a page of {}, read `{}[{} = .id] by {order}` in its shape: `{{ *, totals: {}[{} = .id] by {order} }}`; keep the parent page's audience key, order and cursor. For a scalar supply every group key: {}[{}]. Omit an explicit aggregate read rule to inherit parent authorization", parent.parent, read.table, parent.reference, read.table, parent.reference, read.table, m.by.join(", "))).guide("maintains"));
            }
        }
        let (range, bound) = self.prefix_bound(read, &index, position)?;
        if info.maintained.is_some() && let Some(position) = index.columns.iter().position(|field| field == "value") {
            if prefix.len() != position {
                return Err(super::maintains::refusal(&range.site, format!("{} by {} needs every ranking scope field pinned: {}", read.table, index.name, index.columns[..position].join(", ")),
                    format!("pin {} with where equalities, then read {} last {} by .value after c; omit the scope predicate for a global ranking", index.columns[..position].join(", "), read.table, range.take)));
            }
        }
        if range.take == 0 {
            return Err(type_error(&range.site, "a page holds at least one row", print::read_expr(&ReadExpr { range: Some(ast::RangeSpec { take: 50, ..range.clone() }), ..read.clone() })));
        }
        let mut page_source = read.clone();
        page_source.computed.extend(index.computed.iter().map(|(key, value)| {
            let mut value = value.clone();
            if let A::Call { args, .. } = &mut value
                && let Some(A::Name { name, site }) = args.first().cloned() {
                args[0] = A::Member { base: Box::new(A::Focal { site: site.clone() }), name, site };
            }
            (key.clone(), value)
        }));
        for (key, column) in page_source.keys.iter_mut().zip(&index.columns) {
            key.column.get_or_insert_with(|| column.clone());
        }
        let filter_text = read.filter.as_ref().map(|expr| self.catalog.source.expression(expr));
        let (read, filter) = self.scan_filter(read, &index)?;
        let read = &read;
        // Key expressions precede the seek bounds and cursor, including their setup.
        let prefix: Vec<Expr> = prefix.into_iter().map(|value| self.snapshot(value, out)).collect();
        let seek = self.seek(info, read, &index, out)?;
        let mut after = None;
        if let Some(cursor) = &range.cursor {
            let (e, t) = self.expr(cursor, out)?;
            if !matches!(t.required(), Ty::Cursor) {
                return Err(type_error(cursor.site(), format!("after expects a cursor, got {}", t.name()), "declare c: cursor ? and pass after c"));
            }
            if !info.total_cursor(&index) {
                let columns = index.columns.iter().map(|column| index.computed.get(column)
                    .map(print::expression).unwrap_or_else(|| column.clone())).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                if index.unique {
                    // Do not add id to the unique constraint: that would
                    // weaken it. Plan a separate pageable index from the read.
                    let mut planned = read.clone();
                    let rewrite = if index.computed.is_empty() {
                        planned.index = None;
                        for (key, column) in planned.keys.iter_mut().zip(&index.columns) { key.column.get_or_insert_with(|| column.clone()); }
                        planned.order_keys = index.columns.iter().skip(prefix.len()).cloned().collect();
                        print::read_expr(&planned)
                    } else {
                        let mut name = format!("{}Page", index.name);
                        while info.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.name == name) { name.push('_'); }
                        planned.index = Some(name.clone());
                        format!("add `by {name}: {columns}, id` to {}; read `{}`", read.table, print::read_expr(&planned))
                    };
                    return Err(Diagnostic::new(&range.site, "E_CURSOR_INDEX", format!("index {}.{} cannot carry a cursor: its nullable unique key allows tied null rows without an id tiebreak", read.table, index.name), rewrite).guide("indexes"));
                }
                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&range.site, "E_CURSOR_INDEX", format!("index {}.{} cannot carry a cursor: it has no declared id or total unique key", read.table, index.name), format!("by {}: {columns}, id", index.name)).guide("indexes").mechanical());
            }
            after = Some(Box::new(e));
        }
        let mut prefix: Vec<IndexKeyExpr> = prefix.into_iter().map(IndexKeyExpr::Scalar).collect();
        if let Some(matching) = &read.matching {
            // The search text follows the keys as the word term; the index

                return Err(type_error(matching.site(), format!("matching takes present text, got {}", t.name()), rewrite));
            }
            prefix.push(IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(e));
        }
        let scan_site = self.site();
        self.page_sources.insert(scan_site.clone(), page_source);
        let pins = read.keys.iter().enumerate().map(|(position, key)| {
            let column = key.column.as_ref().unwrap_or(&index.columns[position]);
            format!(".{column} = {}", self.catalog.source.operand(&key.value))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
        let domain = if pins.is_empty() { read.table.clone() } else { format!("{} where {pins}", read.table) };
        self.sources.insert(format!("domain/{scan_site}"), (read.site.clone(), domain));
        if filter.is_some() && let Some(text) = filter_text { self.filters.insert(scan_site.clone(), text); }

        self.record_selection(&scan_site, read, &index, if position == prefix::Position::Exists { "exists" } else { "page" }, &selection);
        // Authored spelling serves diagnostics; inferred-index residency sites
        // keep canonical bytes. Explicit-index search alternatives retain their
        // authored origin even when presence planning synthesizes a browse index.
        if !read.order_keys.is_empty() && let Some(text) = self.catalog.canonical_read_origins.get(&read.site.to_string()) {
            self.canonical_sources.insert(scan_site.clone(), text.clone());
        }
        let spelling = if !read.order_keys.is_empty() { self.catalog.read_origins.get(&read.site.to_string()).cloned() } else { None };
        self.sources.insert(scan_site.clone(), (read.site.clone(), spelling.unwrap_or_else(|| print::read_expr(&ReadExpr { index: None, range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), ..read.clone() }))));
        let mut scan = Scan { cursor_pins: read.cursor_prefix.map_or(0, |old| (read.keys.len() - old) as u32),
            variant: self.variant(),
            site: scan_site,
            table: read.table.clone(),
            index: index.name,
            prefix,
            order: if range.order == ast::Order::Asc { Order::Asc } else { Order::Desc },
            take: bound,
            existence: position == prefix::Position::Exists,
            after,
            before: None,
            range: seek,
            filter,
            opaque_cursor: range.cursor.is_some(),
            merge_site: None,
            provenance: KeyProvenance::Shape,
        };

            scan.take = Bound::Meter;
        }
        if matches!(position, prefix::Position::Exists | prefix::Position::Aggregate) {
            self.constraint_domains.insert(scan.site.clone());
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.028#4-put-the-rules-where-the-builder-meets-them — job/preview advice belongs only to a helper with a query-ineligible meter read.
        if self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Job && self.call_chain.len() > 1
            && scan.take == Bound::Meter && !snapback4_core::engine::rules::scan_visibility(self.schema, &scan).uniform {

            // gives a query a literal bound, bypassing its non-uniform meter refusal.
            let cap = self.schema.group_bound(&scan.table, &index.columns[..read.keys.len()]);
            if cap.is_none_or(|cap| u64::from(cap) > snapback4_core::Ceilings::default().examined) {
                self.job_bound_helpers.insert(self.call_chain.last().unwrap().0.clone());

//! Development argument checks shared by the host and fixture runner.
use serde_json::Value as Json;
#[cfg(test)] use serde_json::json;
use snapback4_core::Refusal;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
pub(crate) fn arguments(personas:&[String],kinds:&BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::ir::ArgType>,args:&Json)->Option<Refusal> {
    fn bare(kind:&snapback4_core::ir::ArgType,value:&Json,personas:&[String])->Option<String> {
        use snapback4_core::ir::ArgType;
        match kind {
            ArgType::Principal => value.as_str().filter(|v| personas.iter().any(|p| p==v)).map(String::from),
            ArgType::Optional(inner)=>bare(inner,value,personas),
            ArgType::List { of:Some(inner),.. }|ArgType::ListRange { of:Some(inner),.. }=>value.as_array()?.iter().find_map(|v|bare(inner,v,personas)),
            _=>None
        }
    }
    let fields=args.as_object()?;
    kinds.iter().find_map(|(name,kind)|bare(kind,fields.get(name)?,personas).map(|p|Refusal::new("E_INPUT","input",format!("argument {name}: '{p}' is a persona name, not a principal; write 'dev:{p}' (or use the authenticated account principal in production)")).guide("personas")))
}
#[cfg(test)]
mod qdiag_tests {
 use super::*;
 use snapback4_core::ir::ArgType;
 #[test] fn declared_persona_principals_are_checked_without_rewriting_identity(){
  let personas=vec!["alice".into()];let kinds=BTreeMap::from([("p".into(),ArgType::Principal)]);
  let value=json!({"p":"alice"});
  let why=arguments(&personas,&kinds,&value).unwrap();assert_eq!(why.code,"E_INPUT");assert!(why.message.contains("dev:alice"));assert_eq!(value,json!({"p":"alice"}));
  for p in ["dev:alice","aliceOther","user:alice","service:alice"] {assert!(arguments(&personas,&kinds,&json!({"p":p})).is_none());}
  assert!(arguments(&[],&kinds,&value).is_none());
  assert!(arguments(&personas,&BTreeMap::from([("p".into(),ArgType::Id)]),&value).is_none());
  let lists=BTreeMap::from([("p".into(),ArgType::Optional(Box::new(ArgType::List {of:Some(Box::new(ArgType::Principal)),max:5})))]);
  assert!(arguments(&personas,&lists,&json!({"p":["dev:alice","alice"]})).is_some());
 }
}

Mint `snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment`, then call the mutation with the real account principal and its bearer token. For synced rosters use the independent seat-or-member branch; snapback4 guide deploy --section first admin")
+}

//! A request scope plus a response-layer backstop, never a caller channel.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — missing instrumentation must supersede older evidence.
use super::{Recent, Sites};
use serde_json::{Value, json};
use snapback4_core::{Context, engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace};
use std::{cell::RefCell, sync::{Arc, atomic::Ordering}};

thread_local! { static CURRENT: RefCell<Option<Evidence>> = const { RefCell::new(None) }; }
struct Evidence {
    recent: Recent,
    operation: String,
    request: u64,
    query: bool,
    trace: RefusalTrace,
    sites: Arc<Sites>,
    generation: Option<u64>,
}
pub(in crate::serve) struct RequestScope;
impl Drop for RequestScope { fn drop(&mut self) { CURRENT.with(|c| c.borrow_mut().take()); } }
pub(in crate::serve) fn begin(shared: &crate::serve::Shared, path: &str) -> Option<RequestScope> {
    if !shared.personas { return None; }
    let recent = shared.refusals.clone();
    let request = recent.requests.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed) + 1;
    CURRENT.with(|c| *c.borrow_mut() = Some(Evidence { recent,
        operation: path.strip_prefix("/q/").or_else(|| path.strip_prefix("/m/")).unwrap_or(path).into(),
        request, query: path.starts_with("/q/"), trace: Default::default(), sites: Default::default(), generation: None }));
    Some(RequestScope)
}
pub(in crate::serve) fn context(context: Context, sites: Arc<Sites>, generation: u64) -> Context {
    CURRENT.with(|c| {
        let mut c = c.borrow_mut();
        let Some(evidence) = c.as_mut() else { return context };
        evidence.sites = sites;
        evidence.generation = Some(generation);
        context.with_refusal_trace(evidence.trace.clone())
    })
}
pub(in crate::serve) struct Pending { recent: Recent, record: Value }
impl Drop for Pending {
    fn drop(&mut self) { self.recent.submit(std::mem::take(&mut self.record)); }
}
// Inspect the actual encoded envelope, after overflow substitution. This covers
// every responder using the shared JSON response layer, including receipt replay,
// static admission and future paths with no attached collector. No user data is
// copied into the record. Unknown empty answers enter only the opt-in local
// trace; successful visibility filtering is never a refusal.
pub(in crate::serve) fn response(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<Pending> {
    CURRENT.with(|c| {
        let mut c = c.borrow_mut();
        let evidence = c.as_mut()?;
        let body: Value = serde_json::from_slice(bytes).ok()?;
        if evidence.query && evidence.trace.hidden() > 0 {
            eprintln!("dev query: {} hidden: {} (encountered row images filtered by read rules)", evidence.operation, evidence.trace.hidden());
        }
        let code = body.get("denied").or_else(|| body.get("why")).and_then(|v| v.get("code")).and_then(Value::as_str);
        let traced = evidence.trace.take();
        let why = body.get("denied").or_else(|| body.get("why"));
        // The one filtered read that is evidence about a program refusal: the
        // refusing `t[x] ! else CODE` lookup itself, whose read rule hid the row.
        let hidden = traced.as_ref().filter(|cause| !cause.terminal && cause.phase == "read rule").and_then(|cause| {
            let site = why?.get("site")?.as_str()?;
            evidence.sites.contains_key(&(cause.table.clone(), format!("lookup {site}"))).then(|| (cause.table.clone(), evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "read rule".into())).cloned()))
        });
        let cause = traced.filter(|cause| {
            // A filtered read earlier in a program is not evidence about a
            // later uninstrumented terminal refusal in that same request.
            code.is_none() || cause.terminal
        });
        let empty_answer = code.is_none();
        let code = match code {
            Some(code) => code,
            None if evidence.query && body.get("data").is_some() && body.get("loading") != Some(&Value::Bool(true)) => {
                // Visibility filtering is a successful read, not a refusal.
                if cause.as_ref().is_some_and(|cause| !cause.terminal && !cause.no_candidate) { return None; }
                if evidence.recent.trace_empty && empty(&body["data"]) { "EMPTY_READ" }
                else { return None; }
            }
            None => return None,
        };
        let preflight = code == "E_AUTH" && evidence.generation.is_none() && why.and_then(|v| v.get("site")).is_none();
        let mut record = json!({"preflight":preflight,"operation":evidence.operation, "code":code, "requestId":evidence.request,
            "generation":evidence.generation, "clause":"cause not instrumented", "at":snapback4_core::now_ms()});
        // A program's own `require … else CODE` (or `! else CODE`) carries
        // its source site in the refusal itself: the response's own
        // evidence, not an older trace, so the log can name the line.
        if cause.is_none() && let Some(why) = why
            && why.get("rule").and_then(Value::as_str) == Some(code) && let Some(site) = why.get("site").and_then(Value::as_str) {
            record["clause"] = json!("program refusal");
            record["site"] = json!(site);
            record["siteKind"] = json!("program");
            if let Some((table, rule)) = &hidden {
                record["hiddenBy"] = json!({"table": table, "readRuleSite": rule});
            }
        }
        if empty_answer {
            record["emptyRead"] = json!(true);
            record["cause"] = json!(if cause.as_ref().is_some_and(|cause| cause.no_candidate) { "no-candidate" } else { "unrecorded" });
            record["clause"] = json!("no probe miss and no rule cause were recorded");
        }
        if let Some(cause) = cause {
            let clause = evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), cause.phase.clone()))
                .or_else(|| evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), cause.clause.clone())));
            let site = clause.or_else(|| evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "table".into())));
            // Whether the site is the clause's own line or the table's (an omitted rule).
            record["siteKind"] = json!(if clause.is_some() { "clause" } else if site.is_some() { "table" } else { "" });
            record["table"] = json!(cause.table);
            record["clause"] = json!(cause.clause);
            record["phase"] = json!(cause.phase);
            record["site"] = json!(site);
            record["executionSite"] = json!(cause.site);
            record["generation"] = json!(cause.generation);
            record["readRuleSite"] = json!(evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table, "read rule".into())));
        }
        // Published before sending: why refuses an old disk snapshot even when
        // the writer is blocked, this handoff drops, or responses finish out of order.
        Some(Pending { recent: evidence.recent.clone(), record: evidence.recent.sequence(record) })
    })
}
fn empty(value: &Value) -> bool {
    match value {
        Value::Null => true,
        Value::Array(values) => values.is_empty(),
        _ => false,
    }
}
use super::*;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleTerm {
    /// One authorized point read through a row-local typed reference.
    Reference { table: String, column: String, field: String, old: bool },
    /// Bounded live reads; a distinct variant makes old decoders refuse.
    ReferenceChain { column: String, hops: Vec<RuleHop>, old: bool },
    Field(String),
    OldField(String),
    NextField(String),
    Viewer,
    Literal(Value),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RuleHop { pub table: String, pub field: String }

pub const MAX_RULE_HOPS: usize = 3;

impl RuleTerm {
    pub fn reference_source(&self) -> Option<(&str, bool)> {
        match self {
            Self::Reference { column, old, .. } | Self::ReferenceChain { column, old, .. } => Some((column, *old)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    pub fn references(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&str, &str)> {
        let (one, many) = match self {
            Self::Reference { table, field, .. } => (Some((table.as_str(), field.as_str())), &[][..]),
            Self::ReferenceChain { hops, .. } => (None, hops.as_slice()),
            _ => (None, &[][..]),
        };
        one.into_iter().chain(many.iter().map(|hop| (hop.table.as_str(), hop.field.as_str())))
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn has_reference(&self) -> bool {
        self.any_term(&|t| t.reference_source().is_some())
    }
    pub fn any_term(&self, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> bool) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) | Self::Compare(_,a,b) => term(a) || term(b),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(term),
            Self::And(rs) | Self::Or(rs) => rs.iter().any(|r| r.any_term(term)),
            Self::Not(r) => r.any_term(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, source: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    let Some((column, old)) = term.reference_source() else { return; };
    let depth = term.references().count();
    let mut valid = !path.ends_with(".read") && (!old || update) && (1..=MAX_RULE_HOPS).contains(&depth)
        && !matches!(term, RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } if depth < 2);
    let (mut owner, mut column) = (source, column);
    for (table, field) in term.references() {
        valid &= schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()
            && matches!(owner.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: t }) if t == table)
            && !owner.maintained.contains_key(column) && !owner.written_by.get(column).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { valid = false; break; };
        valid &= target.ephemeral.is_none() && target.columns.contains_key(field)
            && !target.maintained.contains_key(field) && !target.written_by.get(field).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        owner = target; column = field;
    }
    if !valid { issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message: "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP: write rules allow up to three ordinary live references with audience-readable fields; use authorized mutation reads for other paths".into() }); }
}

pub(super) fn validate_term(schema: &Schema, table: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) => {
            if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => {
            if !update {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: "old and next images exist only in update rules".into() });
            } else if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::Reference { .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } => validate(schema, table, term, update, path, issues),
        RuleTerm::Viewer | RuleTerm::Literal(_) => {}
    }
}

llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:2598:**2026-09-17 — lane MQ implementation.** A binder-proven, cursor-free query that is not declared `held` and returns a record of independent fields, each holding at most one read, with no reading `require`, can compose retained and authoritative reads. A found point read of a delivered left row counts as retained. Composition is entered only when at least one field routes to the server and at least one is retained. Every field that would stay local as its own query comes from a second local observation, and only the server-routed fields come from the original query's one server reply. The result's `complete` is the conjunction of the server word and every locally kept field's word; it reports `fresh: false`, no `since`, and no continuation. Composition is never entered while a predictable write is unobserved; the whole local answer stands until it settles. Offline or on request failure, the whole local answer stands. The server and the wire are unchanged.

llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7715:| L16 | W15a's brief is corrected, not the ruling: **a held poll under a proven absence guard charges the server exactly what a graced recipient charges**, field by field; absent, graced and every unchanged form charge what they did; device prediction is unchanged; and no server case may exceed what the prediction reserves (the lane stops if one does). The lane continues as W15a-2 | The first session used its stop clause on a real conflict: the brief asked for the emit to execute *and* for server meters identical to a base that skipped it (3 steps and 202 heap bytes for the minimal program). Its measurements price ruling 32 for the author: a 50-member send with all 49 recipients connected goes from 817 steps / 43,297 heap / 0 effect rows to the graced case's 1,356 / 69,400 / 49 effect rows + 49 dependency rows, every one satisfied with zero worker attempts when the clients re-poll. It also makes the server's charges independent of whether a recipient is held, graced or absent, which ruling 18 wanted and the base did not have (6 steps against 9) |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7716:| L17 | After W15a's first blind review (GPT NOT READY, Grok READY WITH CHANGES): **"held never charges more than graced" is withdrawn**. The contract is: prediction, absent, graced and unchanged forms charge exactly what they did; for the paved form — presence values feeding only proven absence guards — held equals graced field by field; and in *every* composition the held case charges at most what device prediction reserves (prediction evaluates the connected path and charges every safe untaken arm, which includes the projected absence arms), the ticketed six-byte divergence excepted. Hedging stays: with `if not online: emit A` and `if online: emit B`, an unconfirmed recipient gets A deferred (cancelled if they return) **and** B. A fix session applies this with the review's other findings — an exponential, unmetered setup walk on alias chains; the server-only projection compiled into the wasm device; "never dropped" restated as durable admission — and the fixed commit goes back to both families | GPT showed L16's closing claim — that the change makes the server's charges independent of the recipient's connection state — is true only of the paved form: a payload that keeps its ordinary value makes a held recipient run work a graced one skips. The alternative that would make it true everywhere is for the **server to charge every safe presence arm as prediction does**, so that presence never changes a meter on any host. That changes what absent and graced mutations are charged today, reaches every effects-only mutation and the cards' cost statements, and is therefore **put to the author rather than built here** |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7727:| L28 | **L26 amended: cardinality is provenance of its own.** W15b's fourth round — both families, independently — showed L26's clause "how many rows a loop sums" was too broad: `count(xs { … })` over an argument list stores `count(xs)`, and `for i in range(0, amount): delta = delta + 1` stores `amount`; in both the caller supplies the number, and can set 999 in one call. A collection carries a cardinality provenance beside its items' data: READ for a read page, ARGUMENT for an argument list or an argument-bounded range, AMBIENT for a literal; shaping, filtering, slicing and sorting preserve it; `count`/`len` return it as data; a loop accumulation over a domain takes the domain's. A finite choice among ambient constants stays selection. Under L27 the round-4 finding stops the landing; the fix is one more session and a fifth round, confirmatory | Two families reaching the same rule from two constructions is the strongest evidence a review loop produces; the rule is now stated in the terms the pass computes (data, control, cardinality), which is what makes the next round able to confirm rather than construct |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7728:| L29 | **Loops prefer silence.** W15b's fifth round (one family READY; the other one Medium: a conditional reset inside an argument-bounded loop let the join drop the domain's cardinality) is the fifth loop or join shape the pass had modelled optimistically. The loop rule is made shape-independent: every binding assigned anywhere inside a loop body takes the domain's cardinality into its data — ARGUMENT for an argument list or argument-bounded range, UNKNOWN for an unknown domain, READ for a read domain — regardless of resets, joins, breaks or position. The accepted cost: an unconditional reset inside an argument-bounded loop (which stores 1) is silent — a false negative on a program no builder writes. **The sixth round is the last**: a wording finding is applied and the lane lands; a behavioural finding is met by extending the same conservatism (the construct goes UNKNOWN) and the lane lands without a further round | A note's only unacceptable outcome is a note on a caller-supplied value; precision inside loops bought nothing but review rounds. The bound on rounds is stated before the round, as L22 and L27 were |

use super::*;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleTerm {
    /// One authorized point read through a row-local typed reference.
    Reference { table: String, column: String, field: String, old: bool },
    /// Bounded live reads; a distinct variant makes old decoders refuse.
    ReferenceChain { column: String, hops: Vec<RuleHop>, old: bool },
    Field(String),
    OldField(String),
    NextField(String),
    Viewer,
    Literal(Value),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RuleHop { pub table: String, pub field: String }

pub const MAX_RULE_HOPS: usize = 3;

impl RuleTerm {
    pub fn reference_source(&self) -> Option<(&str, bool)> {
        match self {
            Self::Reference { column, old, .. } | Self::ReferenceChain { column, old, .. } => Some((column, *old)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    pub fn references(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&str, &str)> {
        let (one, many) = match self {
            Self::Reference { table, field, .. } => (Some((table.as_str(), field.as_str())), &[][..]),
            Self::ReferenceChain { hops, .. } => (None, hops.as_slice()),
            _ => (None, &[][..]),
        };
        one.into_iter().chain(many.iter().map(|hop| (hop.table.as_str(), hop.field.as_str())))
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn has_reference(&self) -> bool {
        self.any_term(&|t| t.reference_source().is_some())
    }
    pub fn any_term(&self, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> bool) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) | Self::Compare(_,a,b) => term(a) || term(b),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(term),
            Self::And(rs) | Self::Or(rs) => rs.iter().any(|r| r.any_term(term)),
            Self::Not(r) => r.any_term(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, source: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    let Some((column, old)) = term.reference_source() else { return; };
    let depth = term.references().count();
    let mut valid = !path.ends_with(".read") && (!old || update) && (1..=MAX_RULE_HOPS).contains(&depth)
        && !matches!(term, RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } if depth < 2);
    let (mut owner, mut column) = (source, column);
    for (table, field) in term.references() {
        valid &= schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()
            && matches!(owner.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: t }) if t == table)
            && !owner.maintained.contains_key(column) && !owner.written_by.get(column).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { valid = false; break; };
        valid &= target.ephemeral.is_none() && target.columns.contains_key(field)
            && !target.maintained.contains_key(field) && !target.written_by.get(field).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        owner = target; column = field;
    }
    if !valid { issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message: "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP: write rules allow up to three ordinary live references with audience-readable fields; use authorized mutation reads for other paths".into() }); }
}

pub(super) fn validate_term(schema: &Schema, table: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) => {
            if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => {
            if !update {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: "old and next images exist only in update rules".into() });
            } else if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::Reference { .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } => validate(schema, table, term, update, path, issues),
        RuleTerm::Viewer | RuleTerm::Literal(_) => {}
    }
}

### 5.2 Composition: helpers with bodies, jobs with preludes

**8. `fn` takes a body.** Today `fn` is one expression and `proc` is a
block that returns nothing, so the 0.1.0 builder wrote the receipt
fan-out three times (`send`, `sendMedia`, `sendPoll`) and the 0.2.6
builder wrote its receipt state as three nested one-line `fn`s. LLP
2000.000 §6 named the split as pricing residue. Proposed: one `fn`,
declared with an expression or with a block ending in `return`, callable
in expression position anywhere; expanded inline as helpers are today
(`bind/program/control.rs`), so a write inside a body is admitted exactly
when the caller may write. **Loop placement also inherits the caller's
enclosing loops:** a helper inside a job's recipient sweep performs ordinary
per-recipient loops; a helper at the job's top level still receives the
automatic-sweep and stable-domain checks. `break` and `continue` remain
lexically scoped to loops inside the helper. A query calling a writing `fn`
is a placement error at the call site, with the site named. `proc` is deleted with the
rewrite `fn` (rules: delete, don't deprecate).

```quarry
fn receiptState(cid: conversations, sender: principal, seq: int):
  unread = 0
  undelivered = 0
  for m in memberships[cid] first 50 by byConversationPrincipal:
    if m.principal = sender or m.joinedSequence >= seq:
      continue
    if m.readSequence < seq:
      unread = unread + 1
    if m.deliveredSequence < seq:
      undelivered = undelivered + 1
  return 'read' if unread = 0 else ('delivered' if undelivered = 0 else 'sent')
```

**2026-09-27 — repeated pure calls (0.4.5 lane QE, calibration #22).**
A null test narrows a repeated `fn` call like a field path of a lexical
snapshot when the expanded helper reads no table, writes nothing and reads
neither `now` nor another changing input (the commit/read clock, a minted id
or connection state). Every argument must be a lexical name, a field path
of a lexical snapshot, a literal, or another such pure call. For example,
`now + windows.ttl(c.disappearing) if windows.ttl(c.disappearing) != null else null`
compiles. A database read used directly as an argument does not qualify;
binding its result once creates a lexical snapshot.

The null fact identifies the helper and ordered arguments, including each
snapshot's lexical identity. It is scoped to the guarded branch, short circuit
or surviving guard and invalidated when any argument root is reassigned or
carried through a changing loop. The binder checks the expanded IR, including
transitive helpers and named shapes, for effects; it never removes a read,
write or clock access to make a call pure. Both calls still execute in source
## 8. The client, dev and rule seams the runs hit

Each item names the loop it removes (§2.2).

1. **A mutation's result reaches the client.** `Write` gains
   `result` on `sent` (the server already answers with it,
   `crates/snapback4/src/serve.rs`); `useMutation().run()` resolves with
   the `Write`. Ruled (2026-09-13): `result` on `sent` only — no
   `predicted` on `pending`; a prediction is visible as `pending` rows,
   not as an answer.
   Fresh replies remove Grok's DM-reuse lookup. **2026-09-14 correction
   under LLP 3000.008's delegated program:** §17 deliberately withholds
   historical values on replay. `Write<R>` therefore distinguishes fresh
   `sent` with `result: R` (including null) from `sent` with
   `replayed: true` and no result. Both clients and hooks preserve this
   distinction; a lost acknowledgement never grants historical read
   authority or justifies resubmission with a new ID. An application may
   make a newly authorized query when it receives execution identity only.
2. **A shape over a device row keeps `pending`.** `*` spreads device
   metadata through as it spreads columns; `api.ts` types a shaped
   object with `pending?: boolean` when its base is a row. Removes the
   0.2.6 M6 loop and the `historyRows` second query.
3. **`webDevice()` persists the wasm.** The opener keeps the module's
   bytes in IndexedDB by digest and opens on them with no network, so an
   offline reload does not depend on a URL. Removes the M3 loop the
   builder solved by hand and makes the cold-offline promise the
   opener's, not the app's.
4. **The `{ id }` asset argument predicts.** The device resolves the
   uploader's own upload facts onto the argument as the server does
   (`issues/20260913-snapback4-video-id-only-prediction-refuses.md`).
5. **Identity admission under `dev`.** Ruled (2026-09-13): on loopback,
   `snapback4 dev` lifts the per-peer and per-email minute counters to
   what the host can carry — the Argon2 slots and the connection caps,
   which are the physical bounds, remain — and prints the fact once;
   `serve` is unchanged. Removes two loops in each of the Astra and
   Grok lanes and the harness's backoff scaffolding.
   **2026-10-06, HR:** password seed scripts and replay adapters use the
   existing `signup`/`login` exports from `snapback4/auth`, passing the returned
   session to one `createClient` per account. Signup already creates a live
   session. No new auth verb, credential store or development bypass is needed.
   The auth card and starter READMEs teach this; the social starter emits a
   Node script for N accounts and each account's authorized profile write.
   Reruns choose login explicitly; arbitrary signup refusals never trigger an
   automatic fallback. Tests execute the published guide and installed script,
