//! Developer-owned, in-memory refusal context. Never part of Refusal or receipts.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — only a host opt-in can collect the private refusing clause.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{RuntimeError, schema::Effect};

#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
#[derive(Clone, Debug, serde::Serialize)]
pub struct RefusingClause {
    pub table: String,
    pub viewer: String,
    pub clause: String,
    pub phase: String,
    pub terminal: bool,
    pub no_candidate: bool,
    pub site: String,
    pub generation: u64,
}
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
pub struct RefusalTrace(std::sync::Arc<std::sync::Mutex<Option<RefusingClause>>>, std::sync::Arc<std::sync::atomic::AtomicU64>);
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
impl RefusalTrace {
    /// Physical row images encountered and filtered by read authority in this request.
    pub fn hidden(&self) -> u64 { self.1.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed) }
    pub fn take(&self) -> Option<RefusingClause> { self.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).take() }
}
impl Runner<'_> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.031#1.2 — only a real, uncapped storage miss; never replace a clause.
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub(super) fn no_candidate(&self, table: &str, site: &str) {
        if let Some(trace) = &self.context.refusal_trace {
            trace.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).get_or_insert_with(|| RefusingClause {
                table: table.into(), viewer: self.context.viewer.clone(), clause: "no-candidate".into(), phase: "storage probe".into(),
                terminal: false, no_candidate: true, site: site.into(), generation: self.context.generation,
            });
        }
    }
    pub(super) fn refusing_clause(&self, table: &str, clause: &str) {
        self.refusing_phase(table, clause, clause);
    }
    pub(super) fn filtered_clause(&self, table: &str, clause: &str) {
        self.filtered_phase(table, clause, clause);
    }
    pub(super) fn filtered_phase(&self, table: &str, clause: &str, phase: &str) {
        self.record_clause(table, clause, phase, false);
    }
    pub(super) fn refusing_phase(&self, table: &str, clause: &str, phase: &str) {
        self.record_clause(table, clause, phase, true);
    }
    fn record_clause(&self, table: &str, clause: &str, phase: &str, terminal: bool) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if let Some(trace) = &self.context.refusal_trace {
            *trace.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()) = Some(RefusingClause {
                table: table.into(), viewer: self.context.viewer.clone(), clause: clause.into(), phase: phase.into(), terminal, no_candidate: false, site: self.statement_site.clone(), generation: self.context.generation,
            });
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (table, clause, phase, terminal);
    }
    pub(super) fn trace_rule_result(&self, result: &Result<bool, RuntimeError>, table: &str, effect: Effect) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if self.context.refusal_trace.is_some() {
            let phase = format!("{effect} rule");
            if matches!(result, Ok(false)) { self.record_clause(table, &phase, &phase, effect != Effect::Read); }
            self.trace_authorization(result, table, effect, &phase);
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (result, table, effect);
    }
    /// Preserve the phase before enclosing writes remap the public error effect.
    pub(super) fn trace_authorization<T>(&self, result: &Result<T, RuntimeError>, table: &str, effect: Effect, phase: &str) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if self.context.refusal_trace.is_some() && matches!(result, Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { .. })) {
            self.refusing_phase(table, &format!("authorization allowance ({effect})"), phase);
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (result, table, effect, phase);
    }
    pub(super) fn trace_unlocated_budget(&self, table: &str, effect: Effect) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if self.context.refusal_trace.as_ref().is_some_and(|trace| trace.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e|e.into_inner()).as_ref().is_none_or(|cause| !cause.clause.starts_with("authorization allowance"))) {
            self.refusing_phase(table, &format!("authorization allowance ({effect})"), "authorization access");
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (table, effect);
    }
    pub(super) fn hidden_row(&self) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if let Some(trace) = &self.context.refusal_trace { trace.1.fetch_add(1, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed); }
    }
    pub(super) fn rule_refusal(&self, table: &str, effect: Effect, clause: &str) -> RuntimeError {
        self.refusing_clause(table, clause);
        RuntimeError::Rule { table: table.into(), effect }
    }
}
//! Column and parameter types, including precise unsupported-default repairs.
use super::Parser;
use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Fallible, Site, syntax, syntax_with}, lexer::Kind, print};
impl Parser {
    pub(super) fn parse_args(&mut self) -> Fallible<Vec<ArgDecl>> {
        self.take("(")?;
        let mut args = Vec::new();
        while !self.at(")") {
            let name = self.peek().clone();
            let binding = self.local_name()?;
            self.take(":")?;
            let type_site = self.peek().site.clone();
            let type_ = self.parse_type(false).map_err(|mut d| {
                if d.mechanical && d.message.starts_with("once is a column modifier") {
                    // Nested element types need a whole-list rewrite, not an argument fragment.
                    if d.site == type_site { d.site = name.site.clone(); d.rewrite = format!("{binding}: {}", d.rewrite); }
                    else { d.mechanical = false; }
                }
                d
            })?;
            if self.at("=") || self.at("default") {
                let at = self.peek().site.clone();
                self.position += 1;
                let fallback = self.expression(0, &[",", ")"])?;
                let mut optional = type_.clone(); optional.optional = true;
                return Err(syntax_with(&at, "operation parameters have no literal defaults; omission is null for an optional parameter",
                    format!("declare `{binding}: {}` and bind `{binding}Value = {binding} ?? {}` at the start of the body", print::scalar_type(&optional), print::expression(&fallback))).guide("types"));
            }
            args.push(ArgDecl { name: binding, type_, site: name.site });
            if !self.maybe(",") {
                break;
            }
        }
        self.take(")")?;
        Ok(args)
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_type(&mut self, column: bool) -> Fallible<ScalarType> {
        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
        let mut kind;
        if self.maybe("[") {
            let item = self.parse_type(column)?;
            self.take("]")?;
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — list ranges share text's inclusive spelling.
            let bounds_error = |site: &Site| syntax_with(site, "list bounds require <=N or an inclusive a..b range", "write [T] <=N or [T] a..b, for example [int] 1..8").guide("types");
            let number = |parser: &mut Self| -> Fallible<u32> {
                if parser.at("-") {
                    return Err(syntax(&parser.peek().site, "list bounds must be nonnegative integers"));
                }
                if !parser.at_kind(Kind::Number) { return Err(bounds_error(&parser.peek().site)); }
                let token = parser.take_kind(Kind::Number)?;
                if token.value.contains('.') {
                    return Err(syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers; fractional bounds are not allowed"));
                }
                token.value.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers from 0 through 4294967295"))
            };
            let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
                (0, number(self)?)
            } else {
                let min = number(self)?;
                if !self.maybe(".") || !self.maybe(".") { return Err(bounds_error(&self.peek().site)); }
                (min, number(self)?)
            };
            if min > max {
                return Err(syntax(&site, "list minimum must not exceed its maximum"));
            }
            if item.optional {
                return Err(syntax(&site, "list element types cannot be optional"));
            }
            kind = TypeKind::List { item: Box::new(item), min, max };
        } else {
            let name = self.word()?;
            kind = match name.value.as_str() {
                "text" => {
                    let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
                        (None, self.number()?)
                    } else if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) {
                        let min = self.number()?;
                        self.take(".")?;
                        self.take(".")?;
                        (Some(min), self.number()?)
                    } else {
                        // The default is normalized here; every downstream
                        // validator and resource calculation sees the same bound.
                        (None, 10_000)
                    };
                    let mut format = None;
                    if self.maybe("format") {
                        let value = self.word()?;
                        if value.value != "handle" && value.value != "url" {
                            let bound = match min {
                                Some(min) => format!("{min}..{max}"),
                                None => format!("<={max}"),
                            };
                            return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("unknown text format {}", value.value), format!("use text {bound} format handle or text {bound} format url")));
                        }
                        format = Some(value.value);
                    }
                    TypeKind::Text { min, max, format }
                }
                "decimal" => TypeKind::Decimal { scale: self.scale("decimal 12")? },
                "money" => {
                    let currency = self.word()?;
                    if currency.value.len() != 3 || !currency.value.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_uppercase()) {
                        return Err(syntax_with(&currency.site, "money currency must be exactly three uppercase letters", "use money USD or money USD 2"));
                    }
                    let scale = if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) { self.scale(&format!("money {} 12", currency.value))? } else { 2 };
                    TypeKind::Money { currency: currency.value, scale }
                }
                "json" => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    TypeKind::Json { max: self.number()? }
                }
                "bytes" => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    TypeKind::Bytes { max: self.number()? }
                }
                "image" => {
                    if !self.maybe("<=") { return Err(syntax_with(&site, "image needs a maximum upload size in bytes", "write `image <=5MB` (5,000,000 bytes), optionally followed by once or ?").guide("types")); }
                    TypeKind::Image { max: self.media_size()?, once: false }
                }
                "video" => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    TypeKind::Video { max: self.media_size()? }
                }
                // `file` is a type only before `<=`: a table named `file` stays a reference.
                // @ref LLP 3000.038#1 — the allow-list names only types the server can verify against the bytes.
                "file" if self.at("<=") => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    let max = self.number()?;
                    let mut types = Vec::new();
                    if self.maybe("(") {
                        while !self.at(")") {
                            let value = self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?;
                            if let Some(why) = snapback4_core::assets::file_type_refusal(&value.value) {
                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, why, format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf', 'text/plain')")).guide("assets"));
                            }
                            if types.contains(&value.value) {
                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("'{}' is listed twice", value.value), "list each media type once"));
                            }
                            types.push(value.value);
                            if !self.maybe(",") { break; }
                        }
                        self.take(")")?;
                        if types.is_empty() {
                            return Err(syntax_with(&site, "a file allow-list needs at least one media type", format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf'), or file <={max} for every verifiable type")).guide("assets"));
                        }
                    }
                    TypeKind::File { max, types }
                }
                "cursor" => TypeKind::Cursor,
                "id" => TypeKind::Id,
                "principal" => TypeKind::Principal,
                "int" if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) || matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "<=" | ">=" | "<" | ">" | "-" | ".") => {
                    let (min, max) = self.int_range(&name.site)?;
                    TypeKind::IntRange { min, max }
                }
                "int" => TypeKind::Int,
                "time" => TypeKind::Time,
                "bool" => TypeKind::Bool,

    /// One fetched candidate's expiry, rule and field verdict, inside an
    /// internal scope. Unadmitted rule work is an unknown fact on a device.
    fn candidate_verdict(&mut self, scope: &super::authorized_scan::Authorization, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], mut row: Value, trusted: bool, rule_prepaid: bool) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,table);
        let provisional = self.is_provisional(name,&row)?;
        let needs_rule = !self.context.is_system() && (!trusted || provisional);
        if needs_rule && !rule_prepaid && !self.device_work_active && !scope.admit_probes(super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read)) {
            // @ref LLP 3000.020#3.2 — unresolved authority is unknown, not an absent contribution.
            if self.device { self.unknown_fact(name, index, prefix, "source rule admission"); }
            return Ok(None);
        }
        if trusted && !provisional && self.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some_and(|m| m.parent_key.is_some()) {
            return Ok(Some(self.authorized_synthetic(table, row)));
        }
        self.authorize_image(name,row)
    }

    /// Cold certification has already admitted the member and entire source
    /// group, including a decoder page reserved before entering prepaid work. Every row
    /// still receives the ordinary expiry, rule and field verdict below.
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#3 — live count certification shares its transaction and authority.
    pub(super) fn authorized_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.reserved_scratch(bytes, allowance, true, work)
    }

    /// Count certification follows an admitted member and source-group verdict.
    /// Nested authorization still shares any enclosing physical allowance.
    pub(super) fn disclosed_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.reserved_scratch(bytes, allowance, false, work)
    }

    pub(super) fn reserved_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], physical: bool, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device_work_active || self.context.is_system() { return self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work); }
        let mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
        let result = self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work);
        self.meter.release_row_heap(mark, bytes);
        result
    }

    pub(super) fn rule_scratch(&self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> u64 {
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        super::rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        sites.iter().map(|(table, _)| self.witness_scratch(table)).max().unwrap_or(0)
    }

    pub(super) fn witness_scratch(&self, table: &str) -> u64 {
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let own = crate::rows::disclosed::admission(definition);
        let source = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            .and_then(|m| self.schema.table(&m.source)).filter(|source| source.is_expiring());
        own.saturating_add(source.map_or(0, crate::rows::disclosed::admission))
    }


    /// Ordinary data reads keep their existing meter and cut semantics. This
    /// scope grants storage access without disabling or restoring any charge.
    /// Hidden authorization must use authorized_scan's prepaid reservation.
    pub(crate) fn metered_scan<T>(&mut self, scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        scan(self, &Authorization::internal())
    }

    /// A rule whose logical allowance the caller has already charged, possibly
    /// to a cut, still evaluates its witnesses under the physical allowance;
    /// the caller decides what its cut means for the answer (LLP 3000.014 §5).
    pub(super) fn prepaid_witnesses<T>(&mut self, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        let active = self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || !self.context.is_system());
        let result = work(self, &Authorization::internal());
        self.tx.authorizing.set(active);
        result
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — admission depends on declared bounds, never on
    // occupants. Private scratch cannot cut the caller's prepaid scan.
    pub(crate) fn authorized_scan<T: Default>(&mut self, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.prepaid_scan(allowance, true, scan)
    }

    /// Public or already-authorized data reads still reserve their logical
    /// meter; they do no authorization traversal. A nested scope cannot opt
    /// out of its enclosing authorization's physical allowance.
    pub(super) fn prepaid_scan<T: Default>(&mut self, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], physical: bool, scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        // This meter is the device's separate physical-work budget. Its
        // caller maps exhaustion to uncertainty, never a logical E_BOUND.
        if self.device_work_active {
            let active = self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || (physical && !self.context.is_system()));
            let result = scan(self, &Authorization::internal());
            self.tx.authorizing.set(active);
            return result;
        }
        for &(kind, amount) in allowance {
            if !self.read_charge(kind, amount)? { return Ok(T::default()); }
        }
        if self.read_capped() { return Ok(T::default()); }
        let scope = Authorization { probes: Cell::new(0), remaining_probes: self.meter.ceilings.rule_probes.saturating_sub(self.meter.used.rule_probes) };
        let state = self.enter_prepaid(physical);
        let result = scan(self, &scope);
        self.leave_prepaid(state, result.as_ref().err());
        if scope.probes.get() > 0 { self.read_charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, scope.probes.get())?; }
        result
//! Read authorization of provisional images, over raw combined-state witnesses.
use super::authorized_scan::Authorization;
use super::{rules::{self, Probe}, runner::Runner};
use crate::{error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, schema::{Audience}, value::Value};

use super::rules::Verdict;

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn is_provisional(&self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        Ok(self.device && (self.provisional_rows.contains(&(table.into(), row.field("id")?.as_str()?.into())) || self.tx.provisional_row(table, row)?))
    }

    pub(super) fn provisional_readable(&mut self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if self.context.is_system() || !self.is_provisional(table, row)? { return Ok(true); }
        self.authorize_read(table, row, false)
    }

    /// F3 calls this on an image before insertion; provenance cannot bypass it.
    pub(super) fn evaluate_read_rule(&mut self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.authorize_read(table, row, false)
    }

    pub(super) fn authorize_read(&mut self, table: &str, row: &Value, prepaid: bool) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let result = self.device_work(|runner| {
            let probes = if prepaid { 0 } else { rules::rule_probes(&runner.schema.tables[table].rules.read) };
            runner.authorized_scratch(runner.rule_scratch(&runner.schema.tables[table].rules.read), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, probes)], |runner, allowance| {
                let scratch = runner.meter.mark();
                let result = runner.authorize_read_inner(allowance, table, row, prepaid || !runner.device_work_active);
                if runner.device_work_active { runner.meter.release_heap(scratch); }
                result
            })
        });
        self.trace_authorization(&result, table, crate::schema::Effect::Read, "read rule");
        if matches!(result, Ok(false)) { self.filtered_clause(table, "read rule"); }
        result
    }

    fn authorize_read_inner(&mut self, allowance: &Authorization, table: &str, row: &Value, prepaid: bool) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let verdict = self.read_authority_inner(allowance, table, row, prepaid)?;
        if verdict == Verdict::Unknown {
            self.unknown_fact(table, "byId", &[row.field("id")?.clone()], "provisional read authority");
            self.components.note(|frame| frame.speculative = true);
        }
        Ok(verdict == Verdict::Readable)
    }

    /// 0.4.2's three-valued verdict for a parent row, under the same prepaid,
    /// restoring authorization scope as a provisional read.
    pub(super) fn read_authority(&mut self, table: &str, row: &Value, prepaid: bool) -> Result<Verdict, RuntimeError> {
        let result = self.device_work(|runner| {
            let probes = if prepaid { 0 } else { rules::rule_probes(&runner.schema.tables[table].rules.read) };
            runner.authorized_scratch(runner.rule_scratch(&runner.schema.tables[table].rules.read), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, probes)], |runner, allowance| {
                let scratch = runner.meter.mark();
                let result = runner.read_authority_inner(allowance, table, row, prepaid || !runner.device_work_active);
                if runner.device_work_active { runner.meter.release_heap(scratch); }
                result
            })
        });
        self.trace_authorization(&result, table, crate::schema::Effect::Read, "read rule");
        if matches!(result, Ok(Verdict::Denied)) { self.filtered_clause(table, "read rule"); }
        result
    }

    fn read_authority_inner(&mut self, allowance: &Authorization, table: &str, row: &Value, prepaid: bool) -> Result<Verdict, RuntimeError> {
        let provisional = self.is_provisional(table, row)?;
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let rule = definition.rules.read.clone();
        if self.device_work_active { self.authorized_scratch(0,&[],|runner, scope| { let image = runner.disclosed_row(definition,row.clone()); super::authorized_scan::InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut runner.meter, |meter,_| image.charge_materialization_internal(meter,scope)) })?; }
        let row = self.disclosed_row(definition, row.clone()).into_value();
        self.rule_dependencies(&rule);
        let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
        let verdict = rules::evaluate_verdict(&rule, &viewer, None, &row, false, &mut ReadProbe { runner: self, allowance, table, provisional, prepaid, unknown: false })?;
        Ok(if self.read_capped() { Verdict::Unknown } else { verdict })
    }

    pub(super) fn witness_covered(&self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        // Own pending writes are facts in the combined state, even in a Left
        // group. Settlement removes their dependents if the server refuses.
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — unknown coverage is not current authority.
        // Until-revoked delivered images prove their own current membership;
        // retained/subset images need affirmative scope evidence, not merely
        // the absence of a Left marker (Unknown is distinct from Retained).
        let tracked = self.schema.tables[table].leave == crate::schema::LeavePolicy::DeliveredHistory
            || self.schema.maintain_for(table).is_some_and(|m| m.own_keys.is_some());
        Ok(!self.device || self.is_provisional(table, row)? ||
            (self.schema.tables[table].sync.is_some() && (!tracked || self.tx.current_row(table, row)?)
                && !self.tx.retained_row(table, row)? && !self.tx.retained_group(table, index, key)?))
    }

    pub(super) fn authorization_absence_covered(&mut self, allowance: &Authorization, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let Some(sync) = &definition.sync else { return Ok(false) };
        if definition.leave == crate::schema::LeavePolicy::DeliveredHistory && !self.tx.current_group(table, index, key)? { return Ok(false); }
        if sync.horizon.is_some() || self.tx.retained_group(table, index, key)? { return Ok(false); }
        let pinned: std::collections::BTreeMap<_, _> = crate::small_map::collect(definition.index(index).expect("validated index").columns().into_iter().zip(key));
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.000.000#5.3 — a union's absence is known through any one arm whose pinned group the viewer wholly holds.
        let schema = self.schema;
        let sync = schema.tables[table].sync.as_ref().expect("synced");
        for (audience, _) in sync.arms() {
            let covered = match audience {
                Audience::Public => true,
                Audience::Column(column) => pinned.get(column).is_some_and(|value| value.as_str() == Ok(self.context.viewer.as_str())),
                Audience::Target { table: target, prefix, .. } => {
                    let Some(mut group) = prefix.iter().map(|column| pinned.get(column).map(|value| (*value).clone())).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>() else { continue };
                    let binding = crate::schema::audience_binding(self.schema, table, definition, sync).map_err(RuntimeError::Schema)?;
                    group.push(Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone()));
                    let target_def = &self.schema.tables[target];
                    self.read_tables.insert(target.clone());
                    // This is a raw audience membership, not the witness's ACL.
                    let found = self.live_lookup(allowance, target, &binding.group_index, target_def.index(&binding.group_index).expect("audience index"), &group, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] None)?;
                    found.map(|row| self.witness_covered(target, &binding.group_index, &group, &row)).transpose()?.unwrap_or(false)
                }
            };
            if covered { return Ok(true); }
        }
        Ok(false)
    }
}

struct ReadProbe<'a, 'b> { runner: &'a mut Runner<'b>, allowance: &'a Authorization, table: &'a str, provisional: bool, prepaid: bool, unknown: bool }
impl Probe for ReadProbe<'_, '_> {
    fn take_unknown(&mut self) -> bool { std::mem::take(&mut self.unknown) }
    fn take_changes(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<(i64, super::rules::Verdict)>> { self.runner.witness_changes.take() }
    fn replace_expiry(&mut self, expiry: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> { self.runner.next_expiry.replace(expiry) }
    fn field(&mut self, field: &str, _old: bool) {
        self.runner.provisional_sequence_read |= self.provisional && self.runner.schema.tables[self.table].sequences.contains_key(field);
    }
    fn exists(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], equalities: &[(String, Value)]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if !self.prepaid && !self.runner.read_charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, 1)? { return Ok(false); }
        let (present, known) = self.runner.probe_witness(self.allowance, table, index, key, equalities, true)?;
        self.unknown |= !known;
        Ok(present)
    }
    fn created(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool { self.runner.created(table, index, key) }
}

#[cfg(all(test, feature = "sqlite"))]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{Context, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{MemoryRows, Rows}, value::object};

    #[test]
    fn provisional_authorization_scopes_expiry_to_succeeded_predicates() {
        let witness = "exists grants[parent = .parent, who = viewer, kind = 'admin']";
        for kind in ["guest", "admin"] {
            for rule in [format!("{witness} or .owner = viewer"), format!("({witness} and .owner != viewer) or .owner = viewer"), witness.into()] {
                let source = format!("table grants:\n  parent: text <=10\n  who: principal\n  kind: text <=10\n  until: time ?\n  expire at .until\n  unique parent, who\n  public\n  sync public\ntable notes:\n  parent: text <=10\n  owner: principal\n  read <- {rule}\n  online only\n");
                let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
                assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
                let schema: crate::schema::Schema = crate::json_codec::from_text(&crate::json_codec::to_text(&bound.schema).unwrap()).unwrap();
                for until in [2000, 3000] {
                    let rows = MemoryRows::default();
                    let grant = object([("id", Value::Id("g".into())), ("parent", Value::String("p".into())), ("who", Value::Id("alice".into())), ("kind", Value::String(kind.into())), ("until", Value::Int(until))]);
                    rows.put("grants", &schema.tables["grants"], &grant, None).unwrap();
                    let candidate = object([("id", Value::Id("own".into())), ("parent", Value::String("p".into())), ("owner", Value::Id("alice".into()))]);
                    let mut runner = Runner::new(&rows, &schema, Context::new("alice", Default::default()).at(1000), Meter::new(Ceilings::default(), Cut::Cap), 0);
                    runner.device = true;
                    runner.provisional_rows.insert(("notes".into(), "own".into()));
                    runner.next_expiry = crate::engine::expiry::Expiry::from(Some(9000)); // A separate earlier read's dependency survives.
                    let scope = runner.next_expiry.scope(); // Deadlines are provisional until a read emits them.
                    assert_eq!(runner.authorize_read("notes", &candidate, false).unwrap(), rule != witness || kind == "admin");
                    scope.emit();
                    assert_eq!(runner.next_expiry.get(), Some(if rule == witness && kind == "admin" { until } else { 9000 }), "{kind}/{rule}");
                }
            }
        }
    }
    #[test]
    fn provisional_authority_uses_complete_existential_and_correlated_timelines() {
        let a = "exists grants[parent = .parent, who = viewer]";
        for rule in [a.to_string(), format!("{a} or not {a}"), format!("not ({a} and not {a})"), format!("({a} and not {a}) or .owner = viewer")] {
            for permanent in [false, true] {
                let source = format!("table grants:\n  parent: text <=10\n  who: principal\n  until: time ?\n  expire at .until\n  unique byParentWho: parent, who\n  public\n  sync public\ntable notes:\n  parent: text <=10\n  owner: principal\n  read <- {rule}\n  online only\n");
                let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
                assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
                let schema: crate::schema::Schema = crate::json_codec::from_text(&crate::json_codec::to_text(&bound.schema).unwrap()).unwrap();
                for early in [2000, 3000] {
                    // Inject legacy physical ties directly to retain the
                    // low-level expiry defense; unique Quarry writes cannot.
                    let rows = MemoryRows::default();
                    for (id, until) in [("early", Value::Int(early)), ("later", if permanent { Value::Null } else { Value::Int(8000) })] {
                        rows.put("grants", &schema.tables["grants"], &object([("id", Value::Id(id.into())), ("parent", Value::String("p".into())), ("who", Value::Id("alice".into())), ("until", until)]), None).unwrap();
                    }
                    let candidate = object([("id", Value::Id("own".into())), ("parent", Value::String("p".into())), ("owner", Value::Id("alice".into()))]);
                    for now in [1000, 2000, 3000, 8000] {
                        let mut runner = Runner::new(&rows, &schema, Context::new("alice", Default::default()).at(now), Meter::new(Ceilings::default(), Cut::Cap), 0);
                        runner.device = true;
                        runner.provisional_rows.insert(("notes".into(), "own".into()));
                        runner.next_expiry = crate::engine::expiry::Expiry::from(Some(9000));
                        let scope = runner.next_expiry.scope();
                        assert_eq!(runner.authorize_read("notes", &candidate, false).unwrap(), rule != a || permanent || now < 8000);
                        scope.emit();
                        assert_eq!(runner.next_expiry.get(), Some(if rule == a && !permanent && now < 8000 { 8000 } else { 9000 }), "{rule}/{now}/{permanent}");
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn witness_answer_cache_is_invalidated_by_every_row_write() {
        let source = "table grants:\n  parent: text <=10\n  who: principal\n  unique parent, who\n  public\n  sync public\ntable notes:\n  parent: text <=10\n  read <- exists grants[.parent, viewer]\n  online only\n";
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let schema: crate::schema::Schema = crate::json_codec::from_text(&crate::json_codec::to_text(&bound.schema).unwrap()).unwrap();
        let rows = MemoryRows::default();
        let note = object([("id", Value::Id("note".into())), ("parent", Value::String("p".into()))]);
        let grant = object([("id", Value::Id("g".into())), ("parent", Value::String("p".into())), ("who", Value::Id("alice".into()))]);
        let mut runner = Runner::new(&rows, &schema, Context::new("alice", Default::default()).at(1000), Meter::new(Ceilings::default(), Cut::Abort), 0);
        assert!(!runner.authorize_read("notes", &note, false).unwrap());
        runner.tx.put("grants", &schema.tables["grants"], &grant, None).unwrap();
        assert!(runner.authorize_read("notes", &note, false).unwrap());
        runner.tx.delete("grants", &schema.tables["grants"], &grant).unwrap();
        assert!(!runner.authorize_read("notes", &note, false).unwrap());
    }

}

#[cfg(all(test, feature="sqlite"))]
mod row_view_tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{Context, Schema, meter::{Ceilings,Cut,Meter}, rows::{MemoryRows,Rows}, value::object};
    #[test]
    fn provisional_row_view_witness_uses_complete_rule_deadline() {
        let witness="exists unread[member = .member]";
        for rule in [format!("{witness} or viewer = 'alice'"),format!("{witness} or not {witness}"),format!("{witness} or exists unread[member = .backup]"),witness.into()] {
            let source=include_str!("../../../snapback4-lang/tests/fixtures/row-keyed-view.q").replace("  joinedAt: int default 0","  joinedAt: int default 0\n  deadline: time ?\n  expire at .deadline")
                + &format!("\ntable gates:\n  member: members\n  backup: members\n  read <- {rule}\n  online only\n");
            let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source)]);
            assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
            let schema:Schema=crate::json_codec::from_text(&crate::json_codec::to_text(&b.schema).unwrap()).unwrap();
            for until in [1001,1100] {
                let rows=MemoryRows::default();
                for (id,expiry) in [("b",until),("c",1200)] {
                    let member=object([("id",Value::Id(id.into())),("conversationId",Value::Id("chat".into())),("principal",Value::Id(id.into())),("readUpTo",Value::Int(0)),("joinedAt",Value::Int(0)),("deadline",Value::Int(expiry))]);
                    rows.put("members",&schema.tables["members"],&member,None).unwrap();
                    let view=object([("id",Value::Id(id.into())),("member",Value::Id(id.into())),("value",Value::Int(0)),("recountPending",Value::Bool(false))]);
                    rows.put("unread",&schema.tables["unread"],&view,None).unwrap();
                }
                let gate=object([("id",Value::Id("gate".into())),("member",Value::Id("b".into())),("backup",Value::Id("c".into()))]);
                let mut runner=Runner::new(&rows,&schema,Context::new("alice",Default::default()).at(1000),Meter::new(Ceilings::default(),Cut::Cap),0);
                runner.device=true;
                runner.provisional_rows.insert(("gates".into(),"gate".into()));
                runner.next_expiry=crate::engine::expiry::Expiry::from(Some(9000));
                let scope=runner.next_expiry.scope();
                assert!(runner.authorize_read("gates",&gate,false).unwrap());
                scope.emit();
                assert_eq!(runner.next_expiry.get(),Some(if rule==witness {until} else if rule.contains(".backup") {1200} else {9000}),"{rule}");
            }
        }
    }
}

    pub(crate) fn check_rule(&mut self, table_name: &str, effect: Effect, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if effect != Effect::Read && self.timeline_may_write(table_name, effect) { return Ok(true); }
        if self.context.is_system() {
            return Ok(true);
        }
        if effect == Effect::Read && self.is_provisional(table_name, next)? { return self.provisional_readable(table_name, next); }
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        // A device holds a synced table's rows because the server delivered
        // each under the read rule and withdraws what it revokes; what
        // `retain delivered-history` kept stays readable here (LLP 3000 §7).
        if effect == Effect::Read && self.trust_synced_read(table) {
            return Ok(true);
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — item 7(a): an unnamed update rule judges old and next.
        let mut rule = table.rules.for_effect(effect).clone();
        if effect == Effect::Update && old.is_some() && table.judges_old_image() { rule = rule.old_and_next(); }
        if effect == Effect::Update && let Some(old) = old {
            for (field, authority) in &table.written_by {
                if !authority.audience && old.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone())) {
                    rule.fold_untouched_field(field, &table.columns[field]);
                }
            }
        }
        if effect == Effect::Read { self.rule_dependencies(&rule); }
        let bytes = self.rule_scratch(&rule);
        let result = self.authorized_scratch(bytes, &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&rule))], |runner, allowance| {
            let mut next = next.clone();
            materialize_optional_fields(&mut next, table);
            let old = old.map(|old| {
                let mut old = old.clone();
                materialize_optional_fields(&mut old, table);
                old
            });
            runner.evaluate_sequence_rule(allowance, table_name, &rule, effect, old.as_ref(), &next)
        }).map_err(|error| error.authorization_context(table_name, effect));
        self.trace_rule_result(&result, table_name, effect);
        result
    }

    /// A row by id, materialized, without a rule check.
    pub(crate) fn fetch(&mut self, _allowance: &Authorization, table_name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        self.schema.refuse_private(table_name)?;
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        let found = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(table) {
            self.live_lookup(_allowance, table_name, "byId", &table.indexes["byId"], &[Value::Id(id.into())], #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] None)?
        } else { self.authorized_scratch(crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table), &[], |runner, authority| {
                if runner.device_work_active { runner.meter.release_heap(scratch); }
                result
            })
        });
        self.trace_authorization(&result, table, crate::schema::Effect::Read, "read rule");
        if matches!(result, Ok(Verdict::Denied)) { self.filtered_clause(table, "read rule"); }
        result
    }

    fn read_authority_inner(&mut self, allowance: &Authorization, table: &str, row: &Value, prepaid: bool) -> Result<Verdict, RuntimeError> {
        let provisional = self.is_provisional(table, row)?;
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let rule = definition.rules.read.clone();
        if self.device_work_active { self.authorized_scratch(0,&[],|runner, scope| { let image = runner.disclosed_row(definition,row.clone()); super::authorized_scan::InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut runner.meter, |meter,_| image.charge_materialization_internal(meter,scope)) })?; }
        let row = self.disclosed_row(definition, row.clone()).into_value();
        self.rule_dependencies(&rule);
        let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
        let verdict = rules::evaluate_verdict(&rule, &viewer, None, &row, false, &mut ReadProbe { runner: self, allowance, table, provisional, prepaid, unknown: false })?;
        Ok(if self.read_capped() { Verdict::Unknown } else { verdict })
    }

    pub(super) fn witness_covered(&self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        // Own pending writes are facts in the combined state, even in a Left
        // group. Settlement removes their dependents if the server refuses.
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — unknown coverage is not current authority.
        // Until-revoked delivered images prove their own current membership;
        // retained/subset images need affirmative scope evidence, not merely
        // the absence of a Left marker (Unknown is distinct from Retained).
        let tracked = self.schema.tables[table].leave == crate::schema::LeavePolicy::DeliveredHistory
            || self.schema.maintain_for(table).is_some_and(|m| m.own_keys.is_some());
        Ok(!self.device || self.is_provisional(table, row)? ||
            (self.schema.tables[table].sync.is_some() && (!tracked || self.tx.current_row(table, row)?)
                && !self.tx.retained_row(table, row)? && !self.tx.retained_group(table, index, key)?))
    }

    pub(super) fn authorization_absence_covered(&mut self, allowance: &Authorization, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let Some(sync) = &definition.sync else { return Ok(false) };
        if definition.leave == crate::schema::LeavePolicy::DeliveredHistory && !self.tx.current_group(table, index, key)? { return Ok(false); }
        if sync.horizon.is_some() || self.tx.retained_group(table, index, key)? { return Ok(false); }
        let pinned: std::collections::BTreeMap<_, _> = crate::small_map::collect(definition.index(index).expect("validated index").columns().into_iter().zip(key));
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.000.000#5.3 — a union's absence is known through any one arm whose pinned group the viewer wholly holds.
        let schema = self.schema;
        let sync = schema.tables[table].sync.as_ref().expect("synced");
        for (audience, _) in sync.arms() {
            let covered = match audience {
                Audience::Public => true,
                Audience::Column(column) => pinned.get(column).is_some_and(|value| value.as_str() == Ok(self.context.viewer.as_str())),
                Audience::Target { table: target, prefix, .. } => {
                    let Some(mut group) = prefix.iter().map(|column| pinned.get(column).map(|value| (*value).clone())).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>() else { continue };
                    let binding = crate::schema::audience_binding(self.schema, table, definition, sync).map_err(RuntimeError::Schema)?;
                    group.push(Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone()));
        let probes = rule_probes(&table.rules.read).saturating_mul(2).saturating_add(rule_probes(&table.rules.update).saturating_mul(1 + u64::from(table.judges_old_image())))
            .saturating_add(if upsert || table.ephemeral.is_some() { rule_probes(&table.rules.insert).saturating_mul(2) } else { 0 });
        // Covers the largest cloned row allowed by the schema, the caller's patch
        // (including ill-typed fields), and the miss arm's image/default materialization.
        // Retained maps/assets share their Arc; the merge charges only memory it copies.
        let witness = self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read).max(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.update))
            .max(if upsert || table.ephemeral.is_some() { self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.insert) } else { 0 });
        let copy = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(table) { 0 } else { row_copy_ceiling(table).saturating_mul(2) };
        let heap = copy.saturating_add(patch.heap_bytes().saturating_mul(2))
            .max(crate::rows::disclosed::admission(table)).saturating_add(witness);
        for (kind, amount, used, ceiling) in [(MeterKind::RuleProbes, probes, self.meter.used.rule_probes, self.meter.ceilings.rule_probes),
            (MeterKind::HeapBytes, heap, self.meter.used.heap_bytes, self.meter.ceilings.heap_bytes),
            (MeterKind::IndexEntries, if upsert { table.sequences.len() as u64 } else { 0 }, self.meter.used.index_entries, self.meter.ceilings.index_entries)] {
            if !self.meter.room(kind, amount) { return Err(RuntimeError::Bound { kind, actual: used.saturating_add(amount), ceiling, site: self.meter.site().into() }); }
        }
        self.reserve_maintains(table_name, table)?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// The merged image and its lawful copy price (LLP 3000.015 §1), which a
    /// later engine copy of the same image charges again.
    pub(in crate::engine) fn merge_patch(&mut self, table: &str, old: &Value, patch: &crate::value::Supplied, _readable: bool) -> Result<(Value, u64), RuntimeError> {
        let public = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(&self.schema.tables[table]) {
            self.authorize_image(table, old.clone())?
        } else { None };
        let mut bytes = 40_u64;
        for (key, _) in old.as_object()?.iter().filter(|(key, _)| !patch.as_object().expect("patch checked").contains_key(*key)).chain(patch.as_object()?.iter()) {
            // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — a retained field's actual size is disclosed
            // only after both row and field read authority succeed.
            let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
            let copy = if patch.as_object()?.contains_key(key) { patch.field_copy(key) } else if let Some(row) = &public { row.scalar_heap(key) } else { definition.columns.get(key).map_or(0,column_copy_ceiling) };
            bytes = bytes.saturating_add(80 + key.len() as u64 + copy);
        }
        self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
        let fields = old.as_object()?.iter().filter(|(key, _)| !patch.as_object().expect("patch checked").contains_key(*key)).chain(patch.as_object()?.iter())
            .map(|(key, value)| (key.clone(), value.clone())).collect::<std::collections::BTreeMap<_, _>>();
        Ok((Value::Row(fields.into()), bytes))
    }

    pub(in crate::engine) fn no_write_target(&self) -> RuntimeError {
        RuntimeError::Require { site: Some(self.statement_site.clone()), code: "NOT_FOUND".into(), message: None }
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — item 7: an unheld write target without an absence proof queues.
    pub(in crate::engine) fn write_miss(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> RuntimeError {
        if self.device {
            self.unknown_fact(table, index, key, &self.statement_site.clone());
            if let Some(message) = &mut self.missing_fact { *message = message.replace("the row or its absence is past the acquired horizon or online only", "the write target is not held on this device; only the server can resolve it"); }
            RuntimeError::Prediction(self.missing_fact.clone().expect("missing site recorded"))
        } else { self.no_write_target() }
    }

mod common;
use common::*;
use snapback4_core::{Context, WriteState};
use snapback4_device::{device::Device,store::DeviceStore};
use serde_json::json;
const SOURCE:&str="table media:\n  photo: image <=5MB\n  public\ntable parents:\n  label: text <=20\n  unique label\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  unique parent\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\nfn label(p: parents): p.label\nmutation add(id: id, child: id, title: text <=20):\n  p = insert parents { id, label: title }\n  insert kids { id: child, parent: p }\n  return label(p)\nmutation clash(id: id, parent: parents):\n  insert parents { id: 'rolledback', label: 'temporary' }\n  insert kids { id, parent }\nquery parents(c: cursor ?):\n  return parents first 20 by .id after c\n";
fn run<H:Host>(host:&mut H){
 let (mut e,b)=compile(SOURCE);
 let mut d=host.open("qdiag",Some(b)).unwrap();
 for (i,page) in snapshot_pages(&mut e,"alice",10).iter().enumerate(){d.apply(page,i==0).unwrap();}
 let args=json!({"id":"p","child":"k","title":"label"});
 d.predict("add","alice",&args,100,vec![],1).unwrap();
 let actual=e.mutate("add",None,Context::new("alice",common::args(&[("id",id("p")),("child",id("k")),("title",snapback4_core::Value::String("label".into()))])).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(actual.state,WriteState::Sent { result:snapback4_core::Value::String(ref s),.. } if s=="label"),"{actual:?}");
 let row=e.store().rows("kids").unwrap().pop().unwrap();
 assert_eq!(row.field("parent").unwrap(),&id("p"));
 let predicted=d.predict("clash","alice",&json!({"id":"other","parent":"p"}),100,vec![],2).unwrap_err();
 assert_eq!(predicted.code(),"E_UNIQUE","{predicted:?}");
 let conflict=e.mutate("clash",None,Context::new("alice",common::args(&[("id",id("other")),("parent",id("p"))])).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(conflict.state,WriteState::Failed { ref why,.. } if why.code=="E_UNIQUE" && why.message.contains("unique parent") && !why.message.contains("$intent")),"{conflict:?}");
 assert_eq!(e.store().rows("parents").unwrap().len(),1,"the earlier write rolled back");
 assert_eq!(query(&mut d),json!([{"id":"p","label":"label","pending":true}]),"device refusal rolled back");
 host.close("qdiag",d);
}
fn query<S:DeviceStore>(d:&mut Device<S>)->serde_json::Value{snapback4_device::call::call(d,json!({"op":"query","name":"parents","viewer":"alice","args":{"c":null},"now":100}))["ok"]["data"].clone()}
#[test]fn qdiag_memory(){run(&mut Memory::default());}
#[test]fn qdiag_sqlite(){run(&mut Sqlite::new("wave11-qdiag"));}

#[path="common/write_observation.rs"] mod observation;
use snapback4_core::Engine;
use serde_json::json;
fn engine(source:&str)->Engine{let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap()}
#[test]
fn contextual_projection_preserves_every_published_observation(){
 let source="table media:\n  photo: image <=5MB\n  public\ntable parents:\n  label: text <=20\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\nmutation create(id: id, kid: id):\n  parent = insert parents { id, label:'parent' }\n  insert kids { id:kid, parent }\n  return parent.id\nquery list(c: cursor ?):\n  return kids first 10 by .id after c\n";
 let mut a=engine(source);let mut b=engine(&source.replace("id:kid, parent","id:kid, parent:parent.id").replace("5MB","5000000"));
 let (_,a)=observation::observe(&mut a,"create","alice",json!({"id":"p","kid":"k"}),"qdiag-op",("list",json!({"c":null})));
 let (_,b)=observation::observe(&mut b,"create","alice",json!({"id":"p","kid":"k"}),"qdiag-op",("list",json!({"c":null})));
 a.assert_same(&b);
}

 succeeded in 0ms:
  partitions are tagged so no principal's name collides with `public`; a
  commit is delivered whole however large; a grant that ends inside its
  own commit delivers nothing; a delivered aggregate's audience is its
  source's. Horizons bound acquisition, not holdings: a device holds what
  was delivered, and a local read past the horizon says `complete: false`.
- **2026-09-12 — completeness is a class, not a count (lead, after the
  meter review).** As Snapback 2 (LLP 1001 §3): a query with a scan the
  prefix does not decide is `complete: false` whatever the meter found,
  and a capped page's `next` continues from its last visible row or ends
  on an empty page; the meter's numbers, its cut site and a refusal's
  count stay off the viewer wire; a hidden row costs what a miss costs and
  a hoisted-false range what an empty one costs. The device runs the same
  hoist; its `complete` speaks of the horizon and the meter alone, since a
  device holds only its partition.

  **2026-09-15 — W7c development inspection (author's lane brief).**
  Loopback `dev` may return the existing engine counters on successful reads
  explicitly requesting `x-snapback-meter: 1`; the online client exposes this
  session for its principal, and expired sessions are pruned. Change polls and
  effect claims reauthenticate after waiting before observing or claiming new
  work. The web client
  keeps credentials in memory by default, exposes localStorage only as an
  explicit compatibility store, and refreshes a near-expiry session before it
  becomes unusable.

- **2026-09-13 — hidden rows stay hidden at write and replay boundaries
  (security review).** A unique conflict with a row the caller cannot read is
  the generic rule refusal. Audience insertion is admitted only when the
  caller already has the matching grant; audience keys cannot be changed to
  acquire one. The private mutation log may retain old operator data, but sync
  catch-up re-evaluates the current row and emits only its current readable
  image, or a deletion for a row that disappeared or expired. Previously
  delivered device data remains a property of that device.

- **2026-10-12 — a third asset kind, `file`, under the same contract
  (author, approving LLP 3000.038).** A document column (`file <=N`, with an
  optional allow-list of media types Snapback can verify) keeps one home,
  serving by the home row's read rule and the release/sweep lifetime above.
  The upload declares its type and filename (`?as=file&name=`); the server
  checks the declaration against the bytes and never stores a script-bearing
  type. A file is only ever served as an attachment (`nosniff`, a
  sandboxing CSP); `once` does not apply; nothing is transcoded.

- **2026-09-13 — assets are staged, bounded and viewer-private (security
  review).** Server IDs use operating-system or Web Crypto entropy. Upload
  bytes and asset deletion run outside the engine mutex, with bounded slots
  and a generation/session check before commit. A once asset is consumable by
  its placed recipient, while its unplaced uploader retains preview access;
  ranged reads cannot bypass consumption. Image dimensions, total pixels and
  video duration are capped. Unsatisfiable ranges return 416 and every asset
  response is `private, no-store`, so a browser cache cannot cross principals.

- **2026-09-13 — optimize the production server for speed.**

# LLP 3000.035: Differential testing of Quarry, then a small Lean core

**Type:** Plan
**Status:** Draft
**Systems:** Quarry (`snapback4-lang`: parser, binder, emitters), the program IR and interpreter (`snapback4-core`), the device interpreter (`snapback4-device`), the SQLite and Postgres stores, a new differential-testing crate, a later Lean 4 project, scheduled verification
**Author:** Tudor (the original prompt, §1); Claude Opus 5.5 for Charlie Cheever (the record, the Quarry adaptation, the assessment and the staged plan, §2–§6)
**Date:** 2026-10-07
**Revised:** 2026-10-13 (FLAKE: serialize cold oracle refreshes across threads/processes, §5.2); 2026-10-13 (PAGE2 pinned-clock lifetime and exact membership context pointers); 2026-10-12 (LANG2 post-merge: refused commutations compare semantics, with counted meter omissions, in §5.1); 2026-10-12 (revision 15: parent-pinned page guards in the engine and the model, in §5.2.); 2026-10-12 (revision 14: the model realigned with LLP 3000.036 §12.1, §15.4 and §15.12, in §5.2.); 2026-10-12 (lane EX integration: records the live-row meter ruling and the unproved-insert refusal fix, in §5.1.); 2026-10-08 (revision 13: records the generator widening (lane GW), the prefetch control and their findings in §5.1.); 2026-10-08 (revision 12: R2 was an engine defect, a security fix: an unnamed `update <- R` now judges old and next, in §5.2.); 2026-10-08 (revision 11: records the heap fix in §5.1.); 2026-10-08 (revision 10: records the Lean emitter and oracle, its run, its negative controls and the classification of its disagreements, in §5.2.); 2026-10-08 (revision 9: records the supplied-id prediction ruling in §5.1 slice C.); 2026-10-08 (revision 8: records Stage 2's model and proofs as built, in §5.2.); 2026-10-08 (revision 7: records slice C, the server against the device, in §5.1.); 2026-10-08 (revision 6: the nightly schedule is installed on expo-build-450a.); 2026-10-08 (revision 5: records Charlie's heap-accounting ruling in §5.1.); 2026-10-07 (revision 4: records the first 3-hour store run in §5.1.); 2026-10-07 (revision 3: records Stage 1 slice B (stores, prefetch, scheduled runner) as landed, with its first finding, in §5.1.); 2026-10-07 (revision 2: records Stage 1 slice A as landed, with its run and negative controls, in §5.1.); 2026-10-07 (revision 1: adds the assessment (§4) and replaces the full-scope adaptation with a two-stage plan (§5), at Charlie's direction. Stage 1 is a random program generator and Rust-against-Rust differential testing. Stage 2 is a small Lean core for the security claims. §3 is kept as the record of what was not adopted.); 2026-10-07 (draft)
**Related:** [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md), [LLP 3000.003](./3000.003-one-semantics-and-credible-verification.research.md) (one semantics; the reproduced disagreements), [LLP 3000.009 §11](./3000.009-general-purpose-backend.rfc.md#11-verification-obligations) (verification obligations), [LLP 3000.007](./3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md) (the current Quarry), [LLP 0189](./0189-verification-cadence-and-claim-gating.decision.md) (cadence), [LLP 4001](./4001-snapback5-sql-front-end.rfc.md) (a second front end over the same engine)

## 0. What this records

Tudor wrote a prompt for Opus 5.5 that asks for a formal semantics of the
Contract language and a differential test of Contract's Rust interpreter
against it. Charlie asked for the prompt to be recorded and then adapted to
verify Quarry instead of Contract.

§1 is the prompt verbatim. §2 lists what has to change for Quarry, with the
reason for each change. §3 is that adaptation in full. §4 assesses whether it
is worth doing, and concludes that the full scope is not. §5 is the plan
adopted instead. §6 lists what is left open.

Nothing here has been run yet. This is a plan, not a result.

## 1. The original prompt (verbatim)

Recorded 2026-10-07 as Charlie relayed it. Contract lives
outside this repository.

> Here's the prompt for Opus 5.5
>
> Write an axiomatic and operational semantics for the Contract language.
> Then write a code generator backend for the compiler that emits an
> embedding in those semantics of any user code. Then write a differential
> random testing library that can do large-scale testing of the generated
> code (using the operational semantics) against the Rust interpreter
> version. Then add a large number of test scripts to the repo and update CI
> to do differential random testing between the lean and rust versions

The prompt has four steps, and the adaptation keeps all four:

1. axiomatic and operational semantics (in Lean, per the last sentence);
2. a compiler backend that embeds any user program in those semantics;
3. a differential random tester, Lean against Rust;
4. a large test corpus and CI that runs the differential test.

## 2. What changes for Quarry, and why

**Which program the semantics describes.** Quarry has two candidate
objects: the checked surface program the binder receives, and the IR it
emits (`snapback4-core/src/ir.rs`). The server and device interpreters
already share that IR. A semantics of the IR alone would compare one IR in
two interpreters, so the binder would never be tested. Two of the
disagreements LLP 3000.003 reproduced were binder defects:
`identity(1) + identity(2)` returned 4, and a null assertion ran in the
skipped right side of `true or …`. The adapted prompt therefore defines the
meaning of the checked surface program. An IR-level embedding is optional
and serves to localize failures. It is not the reference.

**What an outcome is.** The original prompt does not say what an outcome
is. A Quarry read or operation returns more: data, completeness, cut sites,
cursors, meter fields, the tables touched, the next expiry, and refusals
with their sites. LLP 3000.009 §11.1 already lists these fields as the
outcome every host must agree on, so the comparison uses the same list. A
differential test that compares only returned rows would miss most of what
Snapback 4 promises.

**Authority is part of the meaning.** Rules decide what a principal can read
and write, and disclosure is the property the simulator's exhaustive test
checks against a model. The semantics must include principals and rules.
That makes disclosure and write authority axioms, not test fixtures.

**The value model is specific.** Integers are i53 and overflow refuses.
There is one total order over values (text compares by code point, not by
UTF-16 unit), one canonical JSON, and one digest. Each of these produced a
reproduced cross-host bug. The Lean model must state them exactly.

**There is more than one Rust executor.** The reference is compared with the
engine over SQLite, the engine over Postgres (on a schedule, as
`scripts/test-postgres.sh` runs today), and the device interpreter over its
memory store. Prefetch on and off, and a meter cut forced at each charge
position, are part of the same comparison (§11.1).

**CI follows this repository's verification law.** The blocking suite
(`scripts/test.sh`) has a 60-second budget. LLP 3000.009 §11 says no new
blocking gate follows from these obligations, and LLP 0189 puts the complete
battery on a schedule, with reds becoming fix-forward tickets. Large-scale
random runs therefore go on a schedule. At most a small fixed-seed smoke
check joins the fast path, and only if it fits the budget. "Update CI"
becomes "schedule the large runs and file what they find."

**Disagreements need a ruling, not a winner.** When Lean and Rust disagree,
the semantics is not automatically right, because it is new and unreviewed.
Each disagreement is ticketed. The ruling (Rust bug, semantics bug, or an
underspecified point the language must decide) is recorded in the governing
LLP.

**Existing assets seed the corpus.** The repository already has the
conformance corpus (`snapback4/corpus/`, 327 JSON cases), the guide's cards
(every card's Quarry is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs`),
the apps under `snapback4/apps/`, the simulator's rules model, and Kani
proofs of the key encoding. The large test corpus starts from these.

## 3. The adapted prompt (full scope; not adopted)

This is the faithful adaptation of §1. It is kept as the record of what was
considered. §4 explains why it was not adopted, and §5 replaces it. It has
one known flaw beyond its scope. It tells the agent to read the binder and
the interpreter before writing the semantics, which invites a model that
copies the code's mistakes (§4).

> Write an axiomatic and an operational semantics for Quarry, Snapback 4's
> data language, in Lean 4. Put them in a Lake project at `snapback4/lean/`
> with a pinned `lean-toolchain`. Avoid Mathlib unless a proof needs it,
> because build time matters for the scheduled runs. Read `snapback4/AGENTS.md`,
> LLP 3000, LLP 3000.003, LLP 3000.007 and LLP 3000.009 §11 first. Then read
> the language card (`snapback4 guide language`), the binder
> (`crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/`), the IR (`crates/snapback4-core/src/ir.rs`),
> the value model (`crates/snapback4-core/src/value.rs`) and the interpreter
> (`crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/`).
>
> **1. Semantics.** Define the meaning of a checked Quarry program: the
> parsed and type-checked surface program, before the binder lowers it to
> IR. Cover:
>
> - the value model exactly as `value.rs` states it: i53 integers with
>   refusing overflow, nominal ids, bytes, rows, arrays and maps, the one
>   total order (text by code point), the canonical JSON and the digest;
> - expressions, including short-circuit evaluation, null assertions and
>   pure helper calls with independent evaluation of each call;
> - schema, keys and key order, and point reads, scans, counts, bounded
>   reads and pages, with completeness and cursors;
> - writes, operations, jobs and their at-most-once identity;
> - principals and rules: what a principal may read, what it may write, and
>   which refusal it receives otherwise;
> - the meter: what each step charges, where a budget cuts a read, and what
>   a cut reports;
> - expiry, ephemeral tables and maintained columns, at the level of
>   observable outcomes.
>
> The operational semantics must be executable: a Lean function from
> (schema, store state, principal, program, arguments, meter budget, clock)
> to an outcome record with the fields LLP 3000.009 §11.1 lists. Those are
> data, completeness, cut sites, cursors, every meter field, tables touched,
> next expiry, refusals and their sites. The axiomatic semantics gives
> Hoare-style rules for statements and the system's invariants as theorems
> over the operational semantics. At minimum, prove:
>
> - determinism;
> - that the value order is total and agrees with the key encoding;
> - that no read discloses a row its rules hide from the principal;
> - that no write succeeds without write authority;
> - that `complete: true` is never reported for a read that omitted a
>   qualifying row.
>
> Where the language is underspecified, do not guess silently. List each
> point in an `OPEN.md` beside the Lean project, with the Rust engine's
> current behaviour.
>
> **2. Backend.** Add a Lean emitter to `snapback4-lang` beside the
> TypeScript and Rust emitters (`src/emit.rs`, `src/emit/rust.rs`). Expose
> it through `snapback4 check` (for example `--emit-lean <dir>`). For any
> app's `snapback/*.q`, it emits a Lean module that embeds the schema, the
> rules and every query, operation and job as terms of the §1 semantics. The
> emitted module must elaborate with no `sorry`. Optionally, also emit the
> bound IR as Lean terms, with an IR-level evaluator, so that a disagreement
> can be localized to the binder or the interpreter. The surface semantics
> remains the reference.
>
> **3. Differential random testing.** Build a harness (a crate, for example
> `snapback4-difftest`, plus a compiled Lean executable that reads cases and
> writes outcomes as canonical JSON) that:
>
> - generates well-typed Quarry programs, schemas with rules, store states,
>   principals, arguments, meter budgets and clocks from a seed;
> - runs each case through the Lean reference and through the Rust engine on
>   SQLite and on the device's memory store, and on Postgres when one is
>   available;
> - runs the Rust side with prefetch on and off, and with a meter cut forced
>   at every charge position;
> - compares every outcome field after the documented normalization (none
>   for data and refusals);
> - shrinks each disagreement to a minimal Quarry source file plus store
>   state, and writes it as a replayable case that names its seed.
>
> A named unsupported case never counts as a pass. Report counts of
> supported, unsupported and disagreeing cases separately.
>
> **4. Tests and scheduled runs.** Add a large corpus of differential cases:
>
> - every case in `snapback4/corpus/` that the semantics covers;
> - every guide card's Quarry;
> - the apps under `snapback4/apps/`;
> - each reproduced disagreement in LLP 3000.003 as a named regression case;
> - shrunk counterexamples as they are found.
>
> Add a fixed-seed smoke run to `scripts/test.sh` only if it fits that
> suite's 60-second budget, and leave the Lean build outside it if the build
> does not fit. Put the large randomized runs on a schedule, in the same way
> as the Postgres suite and the drift backstops. Large means at least 10^6
> cases per run across a seed range that rotates. File each new
> disagreement signature as a ticket under `issues/` without duplicating an
> open one. Do not add a blocking gate (LLP 3000.009 §11, LLP 0189).
> Document every command in `snapback4/AGENTS.md`.
>
# Rules — E_RULE

`insert` and `insert ... if absent` return E_RULE when the insert rule denies
the viewer. A unique clash with a hidden row returns E_RULE for either insert
form. For a readable secondary unique clash without a custom refusal, ordinary
insert returns E_UNIQUE naming the declared key, while `insert ... if absent`
succeeds with null. If the update rule denies an update or upsert-patch, it
returns E_RULE for a readable target and NOT_FOUND for an unreadable target. A
valid update or upsert-patch admitted by the update rule can change a hidden
row; if the resulting row remains hidden, it returns null. Without a custom
unique refusal, an update or upsert-patch with a secondary unique clash returns
NOT_FOUND if its target is unreadable, E_RULE if only the colliding row is
unreadable, and E_UNIQUE naming the declared key if both are readable. For rule
denial, an update condition or a secondary unique clash on an unreadable target,
NOT_FOUND carries no row, index, column or branch detail. A missing update
target or an incomplete upsert insert patch returns NOT_FOUND. An update
condition on an unreadable target returns NOT_FOUND even if it would match; on a
readable target, a false condition returns E_CONFLICT with a read-rule
`current`. Delete returns NOT_FOUND for a missing id or an unreadable refused target, and E_RULE for a readable row whose delete rule refuses. Reading a
hidden row returns null. Use a write admitted by its rule; NOT_FOUND on an
update or upsert-patch does not prove absence.

A read denied by a table's rule answers as an empty page, not a refusal,
and a client cannot tell it apart from an empty readable page — by design,
so that a hidden row cannot be probed. For access UI, use a membership query
the viewer may read, such as a conversation lookup readable by its members:
a member gets the conversation even when its message page is empty.
An explicit `require … else CODE` still refuses with the program's code.

A mutation's writes pass the table's rules as the caller; only jobs run as the
system. Under `dev`, `snapback4 why recent` prints each refusal and parked job:
operation, code, request/job ID, Unix time, and the refusing clause with its
site. Replays and uninstrumented paths say `cause not instrumented`. Records describe only
the named request/job.
Visibility filtering is not recorded. Inspect `snapback4 why TABLE` for rules
and residency. Unknown empty answers are silent and unrecorded by default;
`dev --trace-empty` records them locally without console notices.
`snapback4 why EMPTY_READ` gives rule, coverage and horizon investigation commands.

Console repeats print counted one-second windows; `guide refusals` explains.

With several live instances select `why recent --instance ID` (optionally
`--store ID`). Owner-only `.snapback4/dev-refusals/<storeId>/<instance>.json`
retains 32 records. Stopped/mismatched streams are rejected. The asynchronous
32-slot queue reports `dropped` on recovery; `why recent` refuses older
snapshots while newer refusal evidence is pending or dropped.
Query sites belong to the reader's generation; capture exhaustion points to
`read through`. No arguments or row values are recorded, no HTTP endpoint
exposes evidence, and production `serve` does not collect it. Caller bytes
remain unchanged.
//! Compiled examples first; diagnostic details are under `why`.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Types

Types bound columns, arguments, text, lists and uploads.

```quarry
table profiles:
  owner: principal
  name: text 1..80
  bio: text ?
  createdAt: time default now
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
mutation createProfile(name: text 1..80):
  return insert profiles { owner: viewer, name }
```

Reference contexts project required rows’ `.id`. Media: decimal KB/MB/GB.

Columns/arguments become nullable with `?`; omission becomes null. Table types are references; neither typing nor membership reads the target: use `require exists t[id] else CODE` or `r = t[id] ! else CODE`.
Defaults fill omissions; explicit values win. Formats: handle/url; email uses application validation or identity signup/login, not `format email`.
Money/decimal are scaled integers; fractions refuse E_INPUT. Identical admitted unique-key reads narrow after exists/non-null guards, including pure helper arguments; writes, rebinding and viewer changes invalidate proof.

## Limits

- Guard or assert nullable values before passing them to required parameters: E_QUARRY_TYPE.
- Values and arithmetic must fit the integer range: E_OVERFLOW.
- `/` and `%` take ints and floor: `-7 / 2` is -4, `-7 % 2` is 1. A divisor must be nonzero: E_DIV_ZERO.
- New required defaults must also fit the existing store: E_ADOPT.

## Details

Types describe the values a column or argument accepts.
They also set limits on text, lists and uploaded assets.

### Column types

```quarry
table everything:
  who: principal                      -- the authenticated subject; `viewer` is one
  tag: id                             -- an opaque identity
  parent: everything ?                -- a reference to a table's row: its id
  n: int                              -- an integer within ±(2^53 − 1); there are no floats
  at: time                            -- milliseconds since the epoch; `now` is one
  ok: bool
  price: money USD                    -- money CUR [scale]: integer minor units, scale 2 unless given
  ratio: decimal 4                    -- decimal S: an integer scaled by 10^S
  name: text 1..80                    -- text <=N, or text M..N
  description: text
  handle: text 3..20 format handle    -- format handle | url is checked on every write
  site: text <=200 format url ?
  kind: enum('a', 'b')                -- a text literal in this position is a variant
  meta: json <=4000 ?
  raw: bytes <=1024 ?
  photo: image <=10000000 once ?      -- an upload (guide assets); `once` serves one view per principal
  clip: video <=50000000 ?
  tags: [text <=20] <=8               -- [T] 1..N | [T] <=N (0..N); `?` on the whole list, never the element
  unique handle else HANDLE_TAKEN 'That handle is taken.'
  read <- .who = viewer
  insert <- .who = viewer
