pub enum RuleExpr {
    Public { reason: String },
    Allow,
    Deny,
    Eq(RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    Ne(RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    /// Ordered comparisons are confined to named update transitions and to
    /// insert rules over the row's own required int/time columns.
    Compare(crate::ir::BinaryOp, RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    And(Vec<RuleExpr>),
    Or(Vec<RuleExpr>),
    Not(Box<RuleExpr>),
    Exists {
        table: String, index: String, key: Vec<RuleTerm>, provenance: KeyProvenance,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
        equalities: Vec<(String, RuleTerm)>,
    },
    /// True only when the indexed target row was inserted earlier in this
    /// same transaction; insert rules only.
    Created {
        table: String, index: String, key: Vec<RuleTerm>, provenance: KeyProvenance,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
        equalities: Vec<(String, RuleTerm)>,
    },
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn is_public(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Public { .. })
    }

    pub fn is_uniformly_readable(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Public { .. } | Self::Allow)
    }

    /// Every `.field` the rule reads, on any probe key or comparison.
    pub fn fields(&self, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>| {
            if let RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::Reference { column: field, .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column: field, .. } = term {
                out.insert(field.clone());
            }
        };
        match self {
            Self::Public { .. } | Self::Allow | Self::Deny => {}
            Self::Eq(left, right) | Self::Ne(left, right) | Self::Compare(_, left, right) => {
                term(left, out);
                term(right, out);
            }
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => items.iter().for_each(|item| item.fields(out)),
            Self::Not(inner) => inner.fields(out),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => {
                key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_, term)| term)).for_each(|item| term(item, out));
            }
        }
    }

    /// The disjuncts of the rule: an `or` flattens, anything else is itself.
    pub fn disjuncts(&self) -> Vec<&RuleExpr> {
        match self {
            Self::Or(items) => items.iter().flat_map(|item| item.disjuncts()).collect(),
            other => vec![other],
        }
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.6 — service seats read on the server and never enlarge a device's audience.
    pub fn service_grant(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(principal)))
            | Self::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(principal)), RuleTerm::Viewer)
                if principal.strip_prefix("service:").is_some_and(|name| !name.is_empty()) => Some(principal),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Remove only entire service disjuncts; a conjunct, negation or ordinary
    /// principal equality must still pass the ordinary exact-audience check.
    pub fn device_audience_rule(&self) -> std::borrow::Cow<'_, RuleExpr> {
        if super::owner_equality::readable(catalog, schema, read, known) { return true; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable(); };
        let mut pinned = BTreeMap::new();
        // Unlike older advisory lints, proof cannot guess a non-id key: typed ids
        // may select byId even beside one same-width principal/composite key.
        let selected = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|index| &index.name == name));
        let inferred: Vec<_> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len()).collect();
        let positional_index = if read.index.is_some() { selected.filter(|index| index.is_plain()) }
            else { match inferred.as_slice() { [index] => Some(*index), _ => None } };
        if let Some(index) = positional_index {
            for (column, key) in index.columns.iter().zip(&read.keys) {
                pinned.insert(key.column.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| column.clone()), key.value.clone());
            }
        }
        // Extra named tests pin fields even when the positional prefix is unresolved.
        if selected.is_none_or(|index| index.computed.is_empty()) {
            for part in &read.keys { if let Some(column) = &part.column { pinned.insert(column.clone(), part.value.clone()); } }
        }
        if read.index.is_none() && read.keys.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_none()).count() == 1 && read.keys.first().is_some_and(|part| part.column.is_none() && is_viewer(&part.value)) {
            let principal_keys: Vec<_> = table.indexes.values().filter(|index| index.unique && index.columns().len() == 1
                && table.columns.get(&index.columns()[0]) == Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)).collect();
            if let [index] = principal_keys.as_slice() { pinned.insert(index.columns()[0].clone(), read.keys[0].value.clone()); }
        }
        let mut filters = Vec::new();
        if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { super::intent::split((**filter).clone(), &mut filters); }
        for filter in filters {
            if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = &filter && op == "="
                && let Some(column) = super::intent::column(left) { pinned.entry(column.to_owned()).or_insert_with(|| (**right).clone()); }
        }
        // A total self-membership key is witnessed by the probed row itself.
        if table.rules.read.disjuncts().iter().any(|rule| {
            let RuleExpr::Exists { table: target, index, key, equalities, .. } = rule else { return false };
            target == &read.table && equalities.is_empty() && table.index(index).is_some_and(|index|
                index.unique && index.columns().len() == key.len() && index.columns().iter().zip(key).all(|(column, value)| {
                    let required = column == "id" || table.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|kind| !matches!(kind, snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Optional(_)));
                    required && (matches!(value, RuleTerm::Field(field) if field == column)
                        || matches!(value, RuleTerm::Viewer) && pinned.get(column).is_some_and(is_viewer))
                }))
        }) { return true; }
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm| -> Option<String> {
            match term {
                RuleTerm::Viewer => Some("viewer".into()),
                RuleTerm::Literal(value) => Some(RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(value.clone())).quarry().trim_start_matches("viewer = ").to_owned()),
                RuleTerm::Field(column) => pinned.get(column).map(fact_text),
                _ => None,
            }
        };
        fn holds(rule: &RuleExpr, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> Option<String>, schema: &Schema, known: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
            match rule {
                RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => true,
                RuleExpr::Eq(a, b) => match (term(a), term(b)) {
                    (Some(a), Some(b)) => a == b || known.contains(&format!("({a} = {b})")) || known.contains(&format!("({b} = {a})")),
                    _ => false,
                _ => {}
            }
        }
    }
    fn lookup_fact(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, names: &[String], value: &Expr, known: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        let mut shadowed = false;
        let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if matches!(expr, Expr::Name { name, .. } if names.contains(name)) { shadowed = true; } });
        if shadowed { return; }
        let Expr::Assert { value, .. } = value else { return };
        let Expr::Read(read) = value.as_ref() else { return };
        if schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some() || !point_probe(catalog, read) { return; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        let mut indexes: Vec<_> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) } else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() }).collect();
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && read.keys.len() == 1 && is_viewer(&read.keys[0].value) {
            indexes.retain(|index| info.columns.get(&index.columns[0]).is_some_and(|kind| kind.kind == TypeKind::Principal));
        }
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && super::owner_equality::typed_id(catalog, read) { indexes.retain(|index| index.name == "byId"); }
        let [index] = indexes.as_slice() else { return };
        let keys = index.columns.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(at, column)| {
            let key = if named { read.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)) } else { read.keys.get(at) }?;
            Some(format!("{column} = {}", fact_text(&key.value)))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        // A successful ruled row lookup proves the indexed row exists. Extra
        // projected-field tests are not raw rule evidence and are deliberately omitted.
        known.insert(format!("exists {}[{keys}]", read.table));
    }
    fn point_probe(catalog: &Catalog, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
        if read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() { return false; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return false };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        info.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) }
                else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() })
    }
    fn expression_guard_node(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, expr: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, observe_prefix: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        if !observe_prefix {
            for input in guarded_inputs(catalog, expr) {
                input_guards(catalog, schema, program, &input, known, global, system, visiting, notes);
            }
        }
        if let Expr::Exists { read, site } = expr && (observe_prefix || point_probe(catalog, read)) {
            guard(catalog, schema, program, &Expr::Exists { read: read.clone(), site: site.clone() }, known, system, notes);
        }
        if let Expr::If { condition, .. } = expr {
            guard(catalog, schema, program, condition, known, system, notes);
            expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, condition, known, global, system, true, visiting, notes);
        }
        for (name, shape) in definitions(catalog, expr) {
            let key = format!("{shape}:{name}");
            if !visiting.insert(key.clone()) { continue; }
            if let Some(inner) = helper_body(catalog, &name, shape) {
                // Parameters and focal rows belong to the definition's scope. Only
                // viewer/literal facts about the same world cross that boundary.
                let entry = if let Expr::Call { args, .. } = expr && !shape { call_facts(catalog, &name, args, known, global) } else { known.intersection(global).cloned().collect() };
                body(catalog, schema, program, &inner, entry, global, &guard_bindings(catalog, &inner), system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
            }
            visiting.remove(&key);
                let mut values: Vec<Expr> = [&merge.matching, &merge.filter, &merge.cursor].into_iter().filter_map(|value| value.as_deref().cloned()).collect();
                for lane in &merge.lanes {
                    values.extend(lane.admission.as_deref().cloned()); values.extend(lane.distinct.clone());
                    values.extend(lane.prefix.iter().cloned()); values.extend(lane.loop_.as_ref().map(|(_, value)| value.clone()));
                    for read in lane.planned.iter().chain(lane.nulls.as_ref().map(|(_, read)| read)) { values.push(Expr::Read(*read.clone())); }
                    if let Some(fields) = &lane.project { values.extend(fields.iter().filter_map(|field| if let ShapeField::Field { value, .. } = field { Some(value.clone()) } else { None })); }
                }
                values.iter().flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted)).collect()
            },
            Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Focal { .. } | Expr::Literal { .. } => vec![],
        }
    }
    // Reverse dependency closure identifies query values consumed by guards,
    // across aliases/objects and all possible rebinding paths. The observation
    // is checked at its original statement, with that statement's authority.
    fn has_guard(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt], visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
        if statements.iter().any(|stmt| matches!(stmt, Stmt::Require { .. } | Stmt::If { .. })) { return true; }
        let mut copy = analysis_body(statements); let mut found = false;
        super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
            if matches!(expr, Expr::If { .. }) { found = true; }
            for (name, shape) in definitions(catalog, expr) {
                let key = format!("{shape}:{name}");
                if !visiting.insert(key.clone()) { found = true; continue; }
                found |= helper_body(catalog, &name, shape).is_some_and(|body| has_guard(catalog, &body, visiting));
                visiting.remove(&key);
            }
        });
        // Explicit guards can also be nested in loops/viewer scopes.
        found || statements.iter().any(|stmt| match stmt { Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => has_guard(catalog, body, visiting), _ => false })
    }
    fn guarded_inputs(catalog: &Catalog, expr: &Expr) -> Vec<Expr> {
        let consumed = definitions(catalog, expr).iter().any(|(name, shape)| helper_body(catalog, name, *shape)
            .is_some_and(|body| has_guard(catalog, &body, &mut BTreeSet::from([format!("{shape}:{name}")]))));
        match expr {
            Expr::Call { args, .. } if consumed => args.clone(),
            Expr::ShapeUse { base, .. } if consumed => vec![(**base).clone()],
            Expr::Shape { base, fields, .. } => {
                let values: Vec<_> = fields.iter().filter_map(|field| if let ShapeField::Field { value, site, .. } = field { Some(Stmt::Expr { value: value.clone(), site: site.clone() }) } else { None }).collect();
                if consumed || has_guard(catalog, &values, &mut BTreeSet::new()) { vec![(**base).clone()] } else { vec![] }
            },
            _ => vec![],
        }
    }
    fn guard_bindings(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt]) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        fn add_names(expr: &Expr, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
            if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr { out.insert(name.clone()); }
            if let Expr::Object { fields, .. } | Expr::Write { fields, .. } = expr {
                out.extend(fields.iter().filter(|field| field.value.is_none() && !field.spread).map(|field| field.name.clone()));
            }
        }
        fn names(value: &Expr) -> BTreeSet<String> {
            let mut out = BTreeSet::new(); let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| add_names(expr, &mut out)); out
        }
        fn input_names(value: &Expr) -> BTreeSet<String> {
            let mut out = BTreeSet::new(); value_nodes(value, &mut |expr| add_names(expr, &mut out)); out
        }
        fn gather(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt], guards: &mut BTreeSet<String>, edges: &mut BTreeMap<String, BTreeSet<String>>) {
            let mut copy = analysis_body(statements);
            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
                if let Expr::If { condition, .. } = expr { guards.extend(names(condition)); }
                // A definition can consume its input as a guard even when the
                // call itself is not a conditional. Conservatively track all
                // argument/base dependencies of such definitions.
                for input in guarded_inputs(catalog, expr) { guards.extend(input_names(&input)); }
            });
            for statement in statements {
                match statement {
                    Stmt::Bind { names: bindings, value, .. } => for name in bindings { edges.entry(name.clone()).or_default().extend(names(value)); },
                    Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => guards.extend(names(condition)),
                    Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, .. } => { guards.extend(names(condition)); gather(catalog, then_body, guards, edges); gather(catalog, else_body, guards, edges); }
                    Stmt::For { name, domain, body, .. } => { edges.entry(name.clone()).or_default().extend(names(domain)); gather(catalog, body, guards, edges); }
                    Stmt::As { body, .. } => gather(catalog, body, guards, edges),
                    _ => {}
                }
            }
        }
        let mut guards = BTreeSet::new(); let mut edges = BTreeMap::new(); gather(catalog, statements, &mut guards, &mut edges);
        loop {
            let count = guards.len();
            let next: Vec<_> = guards.iter().filter_map(|name| edges.get(name)).flat_map(|values| values.iter().cloned()).collect();
            guards.extend(next); if count == guards.len() { return guards; }
        }
    }
    fn helper_body(catalog: &Catalog, name: &str, shape: bool) -> Option<Vec<Stmt>> {
        if shape {
            let decl = catalog.shapes.get(name)?;
            Some(decl.fields.iter().filter_map(|field| match field {
                ShapeField::Field { value, site, .. } => Some(Stmt::Expr { value: value.clone(), site: site.clone() }),
                ShapeField::NamedSpread { name, site } => Some(Stmt::Expr { value: Expr::ShapeUse { base: Box::new(Expr::Focal { site: site.clone() }), name: name.clone(), site: site.clone() }, site: site.clone() }),
                _ => None,
            }).collect())
        } else {
            let helper = catalog.functions.get(name)?;
            Some(match &helper.body {
                FunctionBody::Block(body) => body.clone(),
                FunctionBody::Expression(value) => vec![Stmt::Return { value: value.clone(), site: value.site().clone() }],
            })
        }
    }
    fn definitions(catalog: &Catalog, expr: &Expr) -> Vec<(String, bool)> {
        match expr {
            Expr::Call { name, .. } if catalog.functions.contains_key(name) => vec![(name.clone(), false)],
            Expr::ShapeUse { name, .. } => vec![(name.clone(), true)],
            Expr::Name { name, .. } if catalog.shapes.contains_key(name) => vec![(name.clone(), true)],
            Expr::Shape { fields, .. } => fields.iter().filter_map(|field| if let ShapeField::NamedSpread { name, .. } = field { Some((name.clone(), true)) } else { None }).collect(),
            _ => vec![],
        }
    }
    fn effects(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt], visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> (bool, bool) {
        let mut copy = analysis_body(statements);
        let mut writes = false;
        let mut binds = false;
        fn bindings(body: &[Stmt]) -> bool {
            body.iter().any(|stmt| match stmt {
                Stmt::Bind { .. } => true,
                Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => bindings(then_body) || bindings(else_body),
                Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => bindings(body),
                _ => false,
            })
        }
        binds |= bindings(statements);
        super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
            if matches!(expr, Expr::Write { .. }) { writes = true; }
            for (name, shape) in definitions(catalog, expr) {
                let key = format!("{shape}:{name}");
                if !visiting.insert(key.clone()) { writes = true; binds = true; continue; }
                if let Some(body) = helper_body(catalog, &name, shape) {
                    let (w, b) = effects(catalog, &body, visiting); writes |= w; binds |= b;
                } else { writes = true; binds = true; }
                visiting.remove(&key);
            }
        });
        (writes, binds)
    }
    fn effectful(catalog: &Catalog, value: &Expr) -> bool {
        effects(catalog, &[Stmt::Expr { value: value.clone(), site: value.site().clone() }], &mut BTreeSet::new()).0
    }
    fn ordered_call(catalog: &Catalog, value: &Expr) -> bool {
        matches!(value, Expr::Call { name, args, .. } if catalog.functions.contains_key(name) && args.iter().all(|arg| !effectful(catalog, arg)))
    }
    fn call_facts(catalog: &Catalog, name: &str, args: &[Expr], known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        let mut out: BTreeSet<_> = known.intersection(global).cloned().collect();
        let Some(helper) = catalog.functions.get(name) else { return out };
        let readonly = helper_body(catalog, name, false).is_some_and(|body| !effects(catalog, &body, &mut BTreeSet::new()).0);
        // Use fresh intermediate names so caller variables that happen to share
        // a formal's spelling cannot be mistaken for transferred evidence.
        for fact in known {
            let source = format!("query guardFact():\n  require {fact} else GUARD\n  return null\n");
            let (doc, errors) = crate::parser::parse_document("guard.q", &source);
            if !errors.is_empty() { continue; }
            let Some(Stmt::Require { condition, .. }) = doc.programs.first().and_then(|p| p.body.first()) else { continue };
            let mut condition = condition.clone();
            let mut used = BTreeSet::new();
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| { if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr { used.insert(name.clone()); } });
            used.extend(helper.args.iter().map(|param| param.name.clone()));
            for arg in args {
                let mut copy = analysis_expr(arg);
                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr { used.insert(name.clone()); } });
            }
            let mut substitutions = Vec::new();
            for (param, arg) in helper.args.iter().zip(args) {
                if is_viewer(arg) || (!readonly && !matches!(arg, Expr::Name { .. })) { continue; }
                let mut marker = "diagSnapshot".to_owned();
                while used.contains(&marker) { marker.push('_'); }
                used.insert(marker.clone());
                substitutions.push((fact_text(arg), marker, param.name.clone()));

//! Referential actions (LLP 3000.039): each reference between ordinary durable
//! tables says what deleting its target does — `on delete restrict` (the
//! default), `cascade`, `set null` or `keep`. This pass synthesizes the index
//! a reference is probed through, lowers the action into the schema, refuses
//! what a column cannot carry, checks that a delete's cascade fits its
//! program, and names every delete whose behavior the restrict default changed.
// @ref LLP 3000.039#2 — restrict is the default; cascade and set null need a cap on the reference.
use super::catalog::Catalog;
use super::intent_walk as walk;
use crate::{ast::*, diag::Diagnostic};
use snapback4_core::schema::{self as core, references::{self, OnDelete as Action}};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

fn action(declared: OnDelete) -> Action {
    match declared { OnDelete::Restrict => Action::Restrict, OnDelete::Cascade => Action::Cascade, OnDelete::SetNull => Action::SetNull, OnDelete::Keep => Action::Keep }
}

/// Before the schema is bound: every reference whose action probes its
/// children gets an index leading with the reference (then the child's
/// `expire at` column), unless one already exists. A `cap N by <column>` is
/// served by the same index.
pub(super) fn indexes(catalog: &mut Catalog) {
    let ordinary = |catalog: &Catalog, name: &str| catalog.tables.get(name).is_some_and(|info| info.maintained.is_none() && info.decl.ephemeral.is_none());
    let mut wanted = Vec::new();
    for (name, info) in &catalog.tables {
        if !ordinary(catalog, name) { continue; }
        for column in &info.decl.columns {
            let TypeKind::Ref { table: parent } = &column.type_.kind else { continue };
            if column.on_delete.as_ref().is_some_and(|(a, _)| *a == OnDelete::Keep) || column.written_by.is_some() || !ordinary(catalog, parent) { continue; }
            if info.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|through| through.reference == column.name) { continue; }
            // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.3 — the effective deadline: a read-through child's `$deadline`, else `expire at`.
            let mut leading = vec![column.name.clone()];
            if info.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.expiring) { leading.push(snapback4_core::schema::through::DEADLINE.to_owned()); }
            else if let Some((expire, _)) = &info.decl.expire { leading.push(expire.clone()); }
            if info.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.is_plain() && index.columns.starts_with(&leading)) { continue; }
            leading.push("id".into());
            wanted.push((name.clone(), IndexDecl { name: references::reference_index_name(&column.name), columns: leading, unique: false, refusal: None, words: None, computed: BTreeMap::new(), site: column.site.clone() }));
        }
    }
    for (table, index) in wanted {
        catalog.synthesized.insert((table.clone(), index.name.clone()));
        catalog.tables.get_mut(&table).expect("catalog table").indexes.push(index);
    }
}

/// After receipts and timelines are bound (they decide which tables are
/// engine bookkeeping): lower each action, refusing what a column cannot carry.
pub(super) fn bind(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &mut core::Schema, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    let mut lowered: Vec<(String, String, Action)> = Vec::new();
    let mut unbounded = BTreeMap::new();
    for (name, info) in &catalog.tables {
        if info.maintained.is_some() { continue; }
        for column in &info.decl.columns {
            let declared = column.on_delete.as_ref();
            let site = declared.map_or(&column.site, |(_, site)| site);
            let refuse = |diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>, message: String, rewrite: String| diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_REFERENCE_ACTION", message, rewrite).guide("writes"));
            let TypeKind::Ref { table: parent } = &column.type_.kind else {
                if declared.is_some() { refuse(diagnostics, format!("{name}.{} is not a reference; `on delete` belongs on a column whose type names a table", column.name), format!("{}: <table> on delete …, or remove `on delete`", column.name)); }
                continue;
            };
            if !schema.tables.contains_key(parent) || !schema.tables.contains_key(name) { continue; }
            // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.1 — a field-owned reference is private to its owner: probing it would disclose it.
            if column.written_by.is_some() {
                if declared.is_some() {
                    refuse(diagnostics, format!("{name}.{} is written by its field owner, so it carries no delete action: probing it would tell the parent's deleter about a private value", column.name), "remove `on delete`".into());
                }
                continue;
            }
            if !schema.actionable(name) || !schema.actionable(parent) {
                if declared.is_some() {
                    refuse(diagnostics, format!("{name}.{} -> {parent} carries no delete action: ephemeral tables, engine-maintained tables and receipt or timeline bookkeeping keep their own lifetimes, so the row stays when {parent} is deleted", column.name), "remove `on delete`".into());
                }
                continue;
            }
            let chosen = declared.map_or(Action::Restrict, |(a, _)| action(*a));
            let table = &schema.tables[name];
            let through = table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == column.name);
            if chosen.writes() && let Some(reason) = references::write_target_refusal(schema, name) {
                refuse(diagnostics, format!("{} on {name}.{}: {reason}; another row's delete may not delete or rewrite its rows", chosen.words(), column.name), if reason.contains("maintained sum") { format!("{}: {parent} on delete restrict   -- or keep. For a maintained sum source, unwind it with ordinary deletes in a system job: `job unwind():\n  for row in {name}:\n    delete {name}[row.id]`; each delete updates the sum. Mark the parent for cleanup, filter the job to those parents, and delete each parent only after its children are gone (guide writes)", column.name) } else { format!("{}: {parent} on delete restrict   -- or keep", column.name) });
                continue;
            }
            if chosen == Action::Keep && through {
                refuse(diagnostics, format!("on delete keep on read-through {name}.{}: a read-through child lives within its parent (LLP 3000.014 §3), so it cannot outlive it", column.name), format!("{}: {parent} on delete cascade, or leave it undeclared (restrict)", column.name));
                continue;
            }
            if chosen == Action::SetNull && let Some(reason) = references::set_null_refusal(schema, name, table, &column.name) {
                refuse(diagnostics, format!("on delete set null on {name}.{}: {reason}", column.name), format!("{}: {parent} on delete cascade, or {}: {parent} on delete restrict", column.name, column.name));
                continue;
            }
            if chosen == Action::Cascade && through && table.is_expiring() {
                refuse(diagnostics, format!("on delete cascade on read-through {name}.{} needs a child that never expires: an expired, unswept child still holds its parent (LLP 3000.014 §3.3)", column.name),
                    format!("keep `{}: {parent}` (restrict); delete live {name} rows, let the sweep take expired ones, then delete the {parent} row", column.name));
                continue;
            }
            if chosen.writes() && schema.reference_cap(name, &column.name).is_none() {
                unbounded.entry(parent.clone()).or_insert_with(|| site.clone());
            }
            // `keep` is the legacy meaning, so the schema spells it by absence: a
            // schema whose references all keep stays byte-identical and capability-free.
            if chosen != Action::Keep { lowered.push((name.clone(), column.name.clone(), chosen)); }
        }
    }
    for (name, column, chosen) in lowered {
        schema.tables.get_mut(&name).expect("bound table").on_delete.insert(column, chosen);
    }
    // Event prices depend on the actions just lowered and must precede bound checks.
    schema.prepare_write_reservations();
    // Diagnose an authored root's whole cascade once, including missing bounds
    // below an already-capped edge. Avoid contradictory parent/child recipes.
    let mut authored=BTreeSet::new();
    for doc in &catalog.documents {for program in &doc.programs {
        let mut body=program.prelude.clone();body.extend(program.body.clone());
        walk::statements(&mut body,&mut |expr| if let Expr::Write {op:WriteOp::Delete,table,..}=expr && schema.tables.contains_key(table) {authored.insert(table.clone());});
    }}
    let mut roots=BTreeMap::new();
    for (parent,site) in &unbounded {
        let found=authored.iter().filter(|root|super::cascade_repair::reaches(schema,root,parent)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if found.is_empty() {roots.insert(parent.clone(),site.clone());}
        else {for root in found {roots.entry(root.clone()).or_insert_with(||site.clone());}}
    }
    let names=roots.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>();
    roots.retain(|root,_|!names.iter().any(|other|other!=root && super::cascade_repair::reaches(schema,other,root) && !super::cascade_repair::reaches(schema,root,other)));
    for (parent,site) in roots {
        let missing=schema.tables.keys().filter(|table|super::cascade_repair::reaches(schema,&parent,table)).flat_map(|table|schema.referencing(table))
            .filter(|r|r.action.writes() && schema.reference_cap(r.child,r.column).is_none())
            .map(|r|format!("{}.{} needs `cap N by {}` or plain single-column `unique {}`",r.child,r.column,r.column,r.column)).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ");
        diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&site,"E_CASCADE_BOUND",format!("delete {parent}: {missing}; all children share one reservation"),super::cascade_repair::recipe(schema,&parent,false)).guide("writes"));
    }
    // A cascade must end: no table reaches itself through cascades.
    let mut reported = BTreeSet::new();
    for name in schema.tables.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>() {
        if !unbounded.is_empty() || schema.delete_reservation(&name).is_some() || !schema.is_referenced(&name) { continue; }
        let Some(r) = schema.referencing(&name).into_iter().find(|r| r.action == Action::Cascade) else { continue };
        if !reported.insert((r.child.to_owned(), r.column.to_owned())) { continue; }
        let site = catalog.tables.get(r.child).and_then(|info| info.decl.columns.iter().find(|c| c.name == r.column)).map(|c| c.on_delete.as_ref().map_or(&c.site, |(_, s)| s).clone()).unwrap_or_default();
        diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_REFERENCE_ACTION", format!("on delete cascade from {name} reaches {name} again: a cascade must end, so the cascading references cannot form a cycle"),
            format!("{}: {name} on delete restrict   -- or set null on an optional reference", r.column)).guide("writes"));
    }
    if let Some(table) = schema.deletion_cycle() && !diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_REFERENCE_ACTION") {
        let site = catalog.tables.get(&table).map(|info| info.decl.site.clone()).unwrap_or_default();
        diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_REFERENCE_ACTION", format!("{table} reaches itself through `on delete cascade` and `read through`: a cascade deletes children before parents, and a read-through child must go before its parent, so no order can delete those rows"),
            "make one of the cascading references `on delete restrict`").guide("writes"));
    }
    for issue in references::validate(schema) {
        let site = catalog.site_for(&issue.path);
        if diagnostics.iter().any(|d| matches!(d.code.as_str(), "E_REFERENCE_ACTION" | "E_CASCADE_BOUND")) { break; }
        diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_REFERENCE_ACTION", issue.message, "declare the reference's action the line names (guide writes)").guide("writes"));
    }
}

/// Every delete site: a cascade whose reservation cannot fit its program is
/// `E_CASCADE_BOUND`; an undeclared reference that can now refuse the delete
/// is `N_DELETE_RESTRICTED` (it replaces the 0.4 orphans note).
// @ref LLP 3000.039#7 — the note fires exactly where the restrict default changed a delete's behavior.
pub(super) fn sites(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &core::Schema, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    // parent -> the undeclared references that now refuse its delete.
    let mut undeclared: BTreeMap<&str, Vec<(&str, &str)>> = BTreeMap::new();
    for (name, info) in &catalog.tables {
        for column in info.decl.columns.iter().filter(|c| c.on_delete.is_none()) {
            let TypeKind::Ref { table: parent } = &column.type_.kind else { continue };
            // A read-through child refused its parent's delete before (E_PARENT_HAS_CHILDREN).
            if info.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == column.name) { continue; }
            if schema.tables.get(name).and_then(|t| t.on_delete.get(&column.name)) == Some(&Action::Restrict) {
                undeclared.entry(parent.as_str()).or_default().push((name.as_str(), column.name.as_str()));
            }
        }
    }
    // Guards that prove no child exists (an unfiltered count required zero,
    // a count-like sum, a status every child insert flips away from): the
    // default cannot change that delete.

//! Referential actions: what deleting a referenced row does to the rows that
//! name it (`on delete restrict | cascade | set null`).
// @ref LLP 3000.039#2 — every reference between ordinary durable tables carries one action; restrict is the default.
use super::*;
pub use reservations::Price;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub enum OnDelete {
    Restrict,
    Cascade,
    SetNull,
    /// The referencing row stays and names a deleted row (the pre-0.5 meaning),
    /// declared on purpose: history, audit trails, contributions that outlive
    /// their target.
    Keep,
}

impl OnDelete {
    pub fn words(self) -> &'static str {
        match self { Self::Restrict => "on delete restrict", Self::Cascade => "on delete cascade", Self::SetNull => "on delete set null", Self::Keep => "on delete keep" }
    }
    /// The actions that write the children, bounded by a cap on the reference.
    pub fn writes(self) -> bool { matches!(self, Self::Cascade | Self::SetNull) }
    /// The actions a delete must consult (every one but `keep`).
    pub fn acts(self) -> bool { self != Self::Keep }
}

/// One action-carrying reference `child.column -> parent`.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct Referencing<'a> {
    pub child: &'a str,
    pub table: &'a Table,
    pub column: &'a str,
    pub action: OnDelete,
}

/// The prefix of the reference index a live child of `parent` is found under.
pub const REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX: &str = "$reference:";
pub fn reference_index_name(column: &str) -> String { format!("{REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX}{column}") }

/// Heap a closure keeps per reached row: its table and id (an id is at most
/// 144 canonical bytes) and the set and queue entries holding them.
pub const CLOSURE_IDENTITY_BYTES: u64 = 384;
/// Child rows a cascade or set-null walk reads per seek (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
pub const REFERENCE_PAGE: usize = 4;

impl Schema {
    /// Whether rows of `name` take part in referential actions, as child or
    /// parent: an ordinary durable application table. Ephemeral rows, engine
    /// derivations and receipt/timeline bookkeeping keep their own lifetimes.
    pub fn actionable(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        let Some(table) = self.table(name) else { return false };
        table.ephemeral.is_none() && self.maintain_for(name).is_none() && self.private_owner(name).is_none()
            && !self.is_recount_work(name) && !self.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.owns(name))
            && !self.receipts.iter().any(|f| [&f.scope, &f.members, &f.runs].iter().any(|t| t.as_str() == name))
    }

    /// Every reference into `parent` a delete consults (all but `keep`), in
    /// (child, column) order.
    pub fn referencing<'a>(&'a self, parent: &str) -> Vec<Referencing<'a>> {
        // A plain loop into a vector: iterator adapters monomorphize per call site on the web device.
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for (child, table) in &self.tables {
            for (column, action) in &table.on_delete {
                if action.acts() && matches!(table.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: target }) if target == parent) {
                    out.push(Referencing { child, table, column, action: *action });
                }
            }
        }
        out
    }

    /// A delete of a `parent` row consults its referencing rows, so it needs
    /// read authority on that row (LLP 3000.039 §3 step 1).
    pub fn is_referenced(&self, parent: &str) -> bool { !self.referencing(parent).is_empty() }

    /// The index a reference is probed through: its column first, then the
    /// child's deadline column when the child can expire, so one seek skips an
    /// expired backlog. A `read through` reference uses its child index.
    pub fn reference_index(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        let table = self.table(child)?;
        if table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == column) {
            return table.indexes.contains_key(through::CHILDREN).then_some(through::CHILDREN);
        }
        // The effective deadline: a read-through child's `$deadline`, else `expire at`.
        let deadline = table.expiry_column();
        table.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| {
            let columns = index.columns();
            index.is_plain() && columns.first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
                && deadline.is_none_or(|deadline| columns.get(1).map(String::as_str) == Some(deadline))
        }).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str())
    }

    /// The cap bounding a reference's children: `cap N by <column>` exactly,
        // Kahn's order over the reached cascade subgraph; a leftover table is a cycle.
        let mut indegree = vec![0_usize; reach.len()];
        for &(_, child, _) in &edges { indegree[child] += 1; }
        let mut bounds = vec![0_u64; reach.len()];
        bounds[0] = 1;
        let mut ready: Vec<usize> = (0..reach.len()).filter(|&t| indegree[t] == 0).collect();
        let mut done = 0;
        while let Some(table) = ready.pop() {
            done += 1;
            for &(from, child, cap) in edges.iter().filter(|(from, _, _)| *from == table) {
                bounds[child] = bounds[child].saturating_add(bounds[from].saturating_mul(cap));
                indegree[child] -= 1;
                if indegree[child] == 0 { ready.push(child); }
            }
        }
        (done == reach.len()).then(|| reach.into_iter().zip(bounds).collect())
    }

    /// The fixed charge a delete of a `parent` row reserves before it probes,
    /// cascades or clears any row, over every meter: the closure's rows and
    /// their identities, every restrict probe (one seek per closure row, plus
    /// the closure rows it skips), every set-null row, and one engine write of
    /// each, priced from the source-write plan the engine already exports. A
    /// function of the schema only — caps, the reference graph, each table's
    /// indexes and row size, and the exported plans — so a meter, an `E_BOUND`
    /// abort or the physical allowance never depends on how many (hidden) rows
    /// exist, and every host charges alike: a device's projection keeps every
    /// table that can carry an action and every exported price. `None` when the
    /// closure is unbounded (a cycle or a missing cap) or a written table has no
    /// fixed write price. Heap is the closure's identity scratch plus the largest
    /// single row's transient work, plus event images retained until commit.
    // @ref LLP 3000.039#5.1 — the reservation is schema-fixed; LLP 3000.015 §2's law for work shaped by hidden rows.
    pub fn delete_reservation(&self, parent: &str) -> Option<Price> {
        let bounds = self.closure_bounds(parent)?;
        let mut price = [0_u64; 7];
        let mut heap = 0_u64;
        let mut identities = 0_u64;
        let mut gathered = 0_u64;
        let rows_of = |name: &str| bounds.iter().find(|(t, _)| t == name).map_or(0, |(_, n)| *n);
        for (table, n) in &bounds {
            let n = *n;
            if table != parent {
                // Fetched by the closure walk and again, current, before its delete.
                let unit = self.unit_write(table)?;
                heap = heap.max(unit[4]);
                add(&mut price, [unit[0], unit[1], unit[2] + 1, unit[3], 0, unit[5], unit[6]].map(|v| v.saturating_mul(n)));
                identities = identities.saturating_add(n);
            }
            for r in self.referencing(table) {
                // A capped walk holds one page of child images per seek.
                let row = crate::engine::row_copy_ceiling(r.table);
                heap = heap.max(row.saturating_mul(REFERENCE_PAGE as u64));
                let seeks = if r.table.expiry_column().is_some() && r.table.through.as_ref().is_none_or(|t| t.reference != r.column) { 2 } else { 1 };
                // A walk over a capped group: a seek per page, and a last, short page per range.
                let walk = |cap: u64| seeks * n + cap.saturating_mul(n) / REFERENCE_PAGE as u64;
                // Every reached row runs the lifetime hook for every through
                // child, including children the closure already cascaded away.
                let through = r.table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == r.column);
                if through && table != parent { add(&mut price, [3_u64, 0, 3, 0, 0, 0, 0].map(|v| v.saturating_mul(n))); }
                match r.action {
                    // A read-through child's probe: three seeks per closure row (LLP 3000.014 §6).
                    OnDelete::Restrict if through => {},
                    // One probe per closure row, a row per seek: it may skip the closure
                    // rows that name it (each names one target per reference), then stops
                    // at its first outside witness.
                    OnDelete::Restrict => {
                        let probe = seeks * n + rows_of(r.child) + n;
                        add(&mut price, [2 * probe, probe, seeks * n + rows_of(r.child) + n, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
                    }
                    OnDelete::Cascade => {
                        let pages = walk(u64::from(self.reference_cap(r.child, r.column)?));
                        add(&mut price, [2 * pages, pages, seeks * n, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
                    }
                    OnDelete::SetNull => {
                        let cap = u64::from(self.reference_cap(r.child, r.column)?);
                        gathered = gathered.max(cap);
                        let cleared = cap.saturating_mul(n);
                        let unit = self.unit_write(r.child)?;
                        heap = heap.max(unit[4]);
                        let pages = walk(cap);
                        add(&mut price, [2 * pages, pages, seeks * n, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
                        add(&mut price, [unit[0], unit[1], unit[2] + 1, unit[3], 0, unit[5], unit[6]].map(|v| v.saturating_mul(cleared)));
                    }
                    OnDelete::Keep => {}
                }
            }
        }
        // The root is read again, current, before its own removal.
        price[2] = price[2].saturating_add(1);
        // The closure's identities (table and id) are held until the last
        // delete, with the root's and one page of child ids.
        // A set-null group is gathered whole (at most its cap) before it is cleared.
        price[4] = heap.saturating_add(identities.saturating_add(1 + REFERENCE_PAGE as u64 + gathered).saturating_mul(CLOSURE_IDENTITY_BYTES));
        let mut receiver_steps = 0;
        for (table, _) in &bounds {
            for reference in self.referencing(table) {
                if reference.action.writes() { receiver_steps = receiver_steps.max(self.write_reservation(reference.child)?.action_events?.steps_per_write); }
            }
        }
        price[0] = price[0].saturating_add(price[3].saturating_mul(receiver_steps));
        price[4] = price[4].saturating_add(self.retained_action_heap(parent, price[3])?);
        Some(price)
    }

    /// Diagnostic recipe price: action work plus the root's ordinary write.
    /// Execution retains its existing separate charges for these two terms.
    pub fn delete_total_reservation(&self, parent: &str) -> Option<Price> {
        let mut total = self.delete_reservation(parent)?;
        add(&mut total, self.unit_write(parent)?);
        Some(total)
    }

    /// Event images remain live until commit, after action scratch is released.
    pub fn delete_event_heap(&self, parent: &str) -> Option<u64> {
        self.retained_action_heap(parent, self.delete_reservation(parent)?[3])
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.039#5.1 — two event images per priced row write, plus bounded keys and audience transitions, retained for the request lifetime.
    fn retained_action_heap(&self, parent: &str, writes: u64) -> Option<u64> {
        let mut per_write = 0;
        for (table, _) in self.closure_bounds(parent)? {
            for reference in self.referencing(&table) {
                if reference.action.writes() {
                    let events = self.write_reservation(reference.child)?.action_events?;
                    per_write = per_write.max(events.heap_per_write);
                }
            }
    // default cannot change that delete.
    let guards = super::delete_guards::Guards::new(catalog);
    let mut notes = Vec::new();
    for doc in &catalog.documents {
        for program in doc.programs.iter().filter(|p| !matches!(p.kind, ProgramKind::Query) && !p.native) {
            let original = program;
            let program = program.clone();
            // Each delete with the (child, reference) pairs a dominating zero-count
            // guard proves childless, and every sum view the program reads.
            let (deletes, summed) = guarded_deletes(&program, &guards);
            let word = if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job { "job" } else { "mutation" };
            let mut noted: BTreeSet<String> = BTreeSet::new();
            let mut priced: BTreeSet<String> = BTreeSet::new();
            let mut read_noted: BTreeSet<String> = BTreeSet::new();
            for (parent, site, counted) in deletes {
                if noted.contains(&parent) { continue; }
                if priced.insert(parent.clone()) && let Some(price) = schema.delete_reservation(&parent) && let Some(over) = over_ceiling(&price, program.kind == ProgramKind::Job) {
                    let chain = schema.referencing(&parent).into_iter().filter(|r| r.action.writes())
                        .map(|r| format!("{}.{} ({}, cap {})", r.child, r.column, r.action.words(), schema.reference_cap(r.child, r.column).unwrap_or(0))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                    diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_CASCADE_BOUND",
                        format!("{word} {}: `delete {parent}[…]` reserves {over} for what it deletes or clears through {chain}, whether or not those rows exist, so the {word} can never commit", program.name),
                        super::cascade_repair::recipe(schema, &parent, program.kind == ProgramKind::Job)).guide("writes"));
                }
                // The delete reaches every table its cascades reach; their undeclared
                // references refuse it too.
                let mut reached = vec![parent.clone()];
                let mut at = 0;
                while at < reached.len() {
                    let next: Vec<String> = schema.referencing(&reached[at]).into_iter().filter(|r| r.action == Action::Cascade).map(|r| r.child.to_owned()).collect();
                    for table in next { if !reached.contains(&table) { reached.push(table); } }
                    at += 1;
                }
                let kept: Vec<(&str, &str, &str)> = reached.iter().flat_map(|table| undeclared.get(table.as_str()).into_iter().flatten().map(move |(c, col)| (table.as_str(), *c, *col)))
                    .filter(|(target, c, col)| !(*target == parent.as_str() && (counted.contains(&((*c).to_owned(), (*col).to_owned())) || guards.status_guarded(original, &site, &parent, c, col)))).collect();
                // @ref LLP 3000.039#7 — the read prerequisite is the other behavior change: name it where a deleter may not be a reader.
                if program.kind != ProgramKind::Job && read_noted.insert(parent.clone()) && schema.is_referenced(&parent) && !deleters_read(schema, &parent) && !schema.children(&parent).any(|_| true) {
                    notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "N_DELETE_NEEDS_READ",
                        format!("mutation {}: `delete {parent}[…]` now also needs read authority on the row: other rows reference {parent} ({}), so a caller the delete rule admits but the read rule does not gets NOT_FOUND", program.name,
                            schema.referencing(&parent).into_iter().map(|r| format!("{}.{}", r.child, r.column)).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
                        format!("make every {parent} deleter a reader (`delete <- <the read rule> and …`), or mark the reference `on delete keep` if deleting {parent} should not consult those rows")).guide("writes"));
                }
                let Some(&(target, child, column)) = kept.first() else { continue };
                noted.insert(parent.clone());
                let named = kept.iter().map(|(_, child, column)| format!("{child}.{column}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                let through = if target == parent { String::new() } else { format!(" (through its cascade to {target})") };
                let sum = summed.iter().find(|(_, c, by, _)| target == parent && c == child && by == column).map(|(view, _, _, summed)| guards.sum_reason(view, child, column, summed)).unwrap_or_default();
                notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "N_DELETE_RESTRICTED",
                    format!("{word} {}: `delete {parent}[…]`{through} now refuses E_REFERENCED while a live row references it ({named}): an undeclared reference is `on delete restrict`; before 0.4.24 the referencing rows stayed and named a deleted row{sum}", program.name),
                    format!("declare what deleting a {target} row does to each: `{column}: {target} on delete cascade` deletes the {child} rows (with `cap N by {column}`), `{column}: {target} ? on delete set null` clears an optional reference, `on delete keep` keeps them as before, and `on delete restrict` preserves intended audit history and keeps this refusal; any declaration silences this note. Anyone whose insert rule admits a {child} row naming a {target} can hold it under restrict")).guide("writes"));
            }
        }
    }
    notes
}

/// The meter a reservation exceeds for a mutation (or a job), if any.
pub(super) fn over_ceiling(price: &references::Price, job: bool) -> Option<String> {
    let c = if job { snapback4_core::Ceilings::job() } else { snapback4_core::Ceilings::default() };
    let limits = [(price[3], c.rows_written, "rows written"), (price[2], c.examined, "rows examined"), (price[1], c.index_entries, "index entries"), (price[0], c.steps, "steps"), (price[4], c.heap_bytes, "heap bytes"), (price[5], c.output_bytes, "output bytes"), (price[6], c.rule_probes, "rule probes")];
    limits.iter().find(|(used, limit, _)| used > limit).map(|(used, limit, kind)| format!("{used} {kind} (a {} may use {limit})", if job { "job chunk" } else { "mutation" }))
}

use snapback4_lang::compile;
fn checked(source: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Bound { compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]) }
const REFS: &str = "table parents:\n  read <- allow\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents ? on delete keep\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\n";
#[test]
fn optional_reference_checks_join_the_null_edge() {
    for condition in ["parent != null", "not (parent = null)", "null != parent"] {
        let b = checked(&format!("{REFS}mutation add(parent: parents ?):\n  if {condition}:\n    row = parents[parent] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  insert kids {{ parent }}\n"));
        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
        assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED"), "{:?}", b.notes);
    }
    for body in ["  if parent != null:\n    unused = true\n", "  if parent != null and skip:\n    row = parents[parent] ! else NOT_FOUND\n"] {
        let b = checked(&format!("{REFS}mutation add(parent: parents ?, other: parents ?, skip: bool):\n{body}  insert kids {{ parent }}\n"));
        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
        assert!(b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED"), "{:?}", b.notes);
    }
}
#[test]
fn syntax_repairs_name_the_actual_form() {
    let b = checked("mutation add(body: text <=20 = 'hi'):\n  return body\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.rewrite.contains("body ?? 'hi'") && d.rewrite.contains("text <=20 ?")), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let b = checked("mutation add(a: bool, b: bool):\n  require (a or\n    b)\n  else NOPE\n  return true\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.rewrite.contains("closing line") && d.rewrite.contains("else NOPE")), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let b = checked("table parents:\n  public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep ?\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.rewrite == "parent: parents ? on delete keep"), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn whole_cascade_recipe_fits_once_across_children() {
    let source="table roots:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  delete <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable votes:\n  root: roots on delete cascade\n  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable comments:\n  root: roots on delete cascade\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=10000\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\nmutation remove(root: roots):\n  delete roots[root]\n";
    for source in [source.to_owned(),source.replace("  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable comments:","  owner: principal\n  cap 500 by root\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable comments:").replace("  body: text <=10000\n","  body: text <=10000\n  cap 500 by root\n")] {
        let b=checked(&source);
        let d=b.diagnostics.iter().find(|d|d.code=="E_CASCADE_BOUND").unwrap_or_else(||panic!("{:?}",b.diagnostics));
        assert!(d.rewrite.contains("heap bytes") && d.rewrite.contains("3 ×"),"{}",d.rewrite);
        let caps: Vec<_>=d.rewrite.split('`').filter(|s|s.starts_with("cap ")).collect();
        assert_eq!(caps.len(),2,"{}",d.rewrite);
        let edited=source.lines().filter(|line|!line.trim().starts_with("cap 500")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n");
        let edited=edited.replace("  root: roots on delete cascade",&format!("  root: roots on delete cascade\n  {}",caps[0]));
        let fixed=checked(&(edited+"\n"));
        assert!(fixed.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{}\n{:?}",d.rewrite,fixed.diagnostics);
        let price=fixed.schema.delete_total_reservation("roots").unwrap();
        let ceiling=snapback4_core::Ceilings::default();
        assert!(price[3]<=ceiling.rows_written && price[4]<=ceiling.heap_bytes,"{price:?}");
    }
}
const COERCION: &str="table parents:\n  name: text <=20\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\nfn label(p: parents): p.name\nquery parentName(p: parents):\n  return parents[p] { name }\nmutation add(id: id):\n  p = insert parents { id, name: 'new' }\n  insert kids { id: 'child', parent: p }\n  return { label: label(p), key: parents[p] }\n";
#[test]
fn contextual_row_ids_preserve_helper_snapshots_and_explicit_ir() {
    let implicit=checked(COERCION);
    assert!(implicit.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",implicit.diagnostics);
    let explicit=checked(&COERCION.replace("parent: p }","parent: p.id }").replace("key: parents[p]","key: parents[p.id]"));
    assert!(explicit.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",explicit.diagnostics);
    fn erase_sites(v:&mut serde_json::Value) { match v { serde_json::Value::Object(o)=> { o.remove("site"); for v in o.values_mut(){erase_sites(v);} }, serde_json::Value::Array(a)=>for v in a{erase_sites(v);},_=>{} } }
    let programs=|b:&snapback4_lang::Bound| {let mut v=serde_json::to_value(b.programs.iter().map(|p|&p.program).collect::<Vec<_>>()).unwrap();erase_sites(&mut v);v};
    assert_eq!(programs(&implicit),programs(&explicit));
    for source in [COERCION.replace("parent: p }","parent: (parents[id]) }"),COERCION.replace("id: 'child', parent: p","id: p, parent: p"),COERCION.replace("table kids:","table strangers:\n  public\ntable kids:").replace("parent: parents on delete keep","parent: strangers on delete keep")] {
        let refused=checked(&source); assert!(!refused.diagnostics.is_empty(),"unexpectedly accepted: {source}");
    }
}
#[test]
fn media_units_and_precise_type_repairs() {
    let b=checked("table pictures:\n  photo: image <=5MB\n  video: video <=2GB ?\n  public\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
    assert!(checked("table pictures:\n  photo: image <=5GB\n").diagnostics.iter().any(|d|d.message.contains("4,294,967,295")));
    assert!(checked("table pictures:\n  photo: image\n").diagnostics.iter().any(|d|d.rewrite.contains("image <=5MB")));
