//! Point reads own authorization and provisional expiry until their answer emits.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — the ordinary point read is `authorized_point`: row and
// field authorization precede result pricing, under the request's one physical
// allowance. 0.4.2's inherited-aggregate, parent-key and event-slot branches
// (LLP 3000.014 §5, LLP 3000.016 §2.2) sit above that boundary and share it.
use super::{runner::Runner, rules, read::PrefixProbe};
use crate::{error::{RuntimeError, MeterKind}, ir::Read, value::Value};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

fn unavailable_aggregate() -> Value { crate::value::object([("value", Value::Null)]) }

impl Runner<'_> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — logical guard facts do not promise that rule
    // expiry traversal short-circuits. Null points have no witness on any
    // replica; reject before occupancy, coverage or lifetime work. Maintained
    // null groups remain real groups, and population equality is unchanged.
    pub(super) fn null_unique_point(&self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool {
        key.contains(&Value::Null) && self.schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()
            && self.schema.table(table).and_then(|t| t.index(index)).is_some_and(|i| i.unique)
    }

    pub(super) fn created(&self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool {
        if self.null_unique_point(table, index, key) { return false; }
        self.created.contains_key(&(table.to_owned(), index.to_owned(), crate::value::canonical_json(&key)))
    }

    /// A point read: the row when it exists and the viewer may read it, else
    /// `null`. Hidden and absent are the same word on purpose.
    pub(super) fn get(&mut self, read: &Read) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        if self.decoration_cache.is_some() { return self.decoration_get(read); }
        let value = self.get_uncached(read)?;
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if self.following_witnesses.is_some() && let Value::Row(fields) = &value {
            let bytes = 256 + read.table.len() as u64 + fields.keys().map(|f| f.len() as u64 + 32).sum::<u64>() + value.field("id")?.as_str()?.len() as u64;
            self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
            self.following_witnesses.as_mut().unwrap().push(super::following_history::projection::Witness { table:read.table.clone(),id:value.field("id")?.as_str()?.into(),fields:fields.keys().cloned().collect() });
        }
        Ok(value)
    }

    pub(super) fn get_uncached(&mut self, read: &Read) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let mut emitted = false;
        self.schema.refuse_private(&read.table)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.21 — an unfinished maintained value is unavailable, never an absent group coerced to zero.
        let maintained = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some();
        if maintained { self.meter.require_uncapped()?; }
        let mut value = self.get_inner(read, &mut emitted)?;
        if maintained { self.meter.require_uncapped()?; }
        if emitted && self.meter.capped_at.is_none() { self.stamp_source(&read.table, &mut value)?; expiry.emit(); }
        Ok(value)
    }

    fn get_inner(&mut self, read: &Read, emitted: &mut bool) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        let key = read.key.iter().map(|value| self.expr(value)).collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
        if self.null_unique_point(&read.table, &read.index, &key) { return Ok(Value::Null); }
        self.observe_sequence_selection(&read.table, &read.index, &key)?;
        let table = self.schema.table(&read.table).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {}", read.table)))?;
        self.read_tables.insert(read.table.clone());
        self.rule_dependencies(&table.rules.read);
        self.meter.at(&read.site);
        self.aggregate_read_coverage(&read.table, &read.index, &key, &read.site)?;
        let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table) else {
            let (value, price) = self.get_prepared(read, &key, emitted)?;
            self.meter.heap(price)?;
            return Ok(value);
        };
        // @ref LLP 3000.028#3.4 — a generation-wide acquired flag cannot lend another caller an empty owner partition.
        if self.device && maintain.own_keys.is_some() && !self.tx.current_group(&read.table, &read.index, &key)? {
            self.unknown_fact(&read.table, &read.index, &key, &read.site);
            self.components.note(|frame| frame.speculative = true);
            return Ok(Value::Null);
        }
        if let Some(message) = maintain.expiring_read_refusal(self.schema) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_MAINTAIN_READ_EXPIRE", message });
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a point hit, miss or denial reserves the same reusable row scratch before discovering occupancy.
        let scratch = self.aggregate_point_scratch(maintain, &key);
        self.meter.heap(scratch)?;
        let result = (|| {
            self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            self.meter.require_uncapped()?;
            self.get_prepared(read, &key, emitted)
        })();
        // Transfer only the sealed result to retained ownership. Keep the
        // high-water mark, work counters and any cut when releasing scratch.
        self.meter.used.heap_bytes = self.meter.used.heap_bytes.saturating_sub(scratch);
        let (value, price) = result?;
        self.meter.heap(price)?;
        Ok(value)
    }

    /// The answer and the sealed price of its retained image, charged by the caller
    /// once its scratch is released.
    fn get_prepared(&mut self, read: &Read, key: &[Value], emitted: &mut bool) -> Result<(Value, u64), RuntimeError> {
        let table = self.schema.table(&read.table).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {}", read.table)))?;
        let index = table.index(&read.index).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing index {}.{}", read.table, read.index)))?;
        let maintained = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table);
        let inherited = maintained.filter(|m| self.schema.tables[&m.source].through.is_some());
        let mut key_admitted = false;
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — only key-determined inputs may authorize a synthetic aggregate row.
        if let Some(m) = inherited.filter(|m| m.parent_key.is_some() || rules::deciding_fields(&read.table, table, &table.rules.read).iter().all(|field| index.columns().iter().take(key.len()).any(|column| column == field) || (field == "id" && m.group_fields().iter().all(|f| index.columns().iter().take(key.len()).any(|column| column == f))))) {
            let visible = (|| Ok(if let Some(parent) = &m.parent_key {
                let Some(at) = index.columns().iter().position(|f| f == &parent.reference) else { return Err(RuntimeError::Structural {code:"E_READ_THROUGH_SCAN",message:format!("{} must be read by its full parent key; use {}[parent] (guide maintains)",read.table,read.table)}); };
                let parent_id = key.get(at).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("read through aggregate needs its full parent key".into()))?;
                match self.parent_verdict(&m.source,parent,parent_id)? {
                    super::through::ParentVerdict::Readable => true,
                    super::through::ParentVerdict::Unknown => { self.unknown_fact(&read.table,&read.index,key,&read.site); false },
                    _ => false,
                }
            } else {
                let mut fields: BTreeMap<_, _> = crate::small_map::collect(index.columns().into_iter().zip(key.iter().cloned()));
                let group_fields = m.group_fields();
                if group_fields.iter().all(|f| fields.contains_key(f)) {
                    let group: Vec<_> = group_fields.iter().map(|f| fields[f].clone()).collect();
                    fields.insert("id".into(), Value::Id(crate::schema::maintained_id(&group)));
                }
                let row = Value::Row(fields.into());
                self.meter.charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))?;
                let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
                if self.device { self.authorize_read(&read.table, &row, true)? }
                else if self.context.is_system() { true }
                else {
                    // The key rule's probes were charged above, possibly to a cut; its
                    // witnesses still run under the request's physical allowance and
                    // the cut stops the read before any aggregate state is fetched.
                    self.prepaid_witnesses(|runner, allowance| rules::evaluate(&table.rules.read,&viewer,None,&row,false,&mut PrefixProbe { runner, allowance }))?
                }
            }))();
            if !visible? || self.read_capped() { return Ok((unavailable_aggregate(), 0)); }
            key_admitted = true;
            if self.device && (table.residency.online_only || (m.parent_key.is_none() && !self.captured_aggregate_coverage(m, index, key)?)) {
                self.unknown_fact(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
                return Ok((unavailable_aggregate(), 0));
            }
            if self.device && m.parent_key.is_some() {
                // A locally synthesized total has no settled row to freeze.
                // @ref LLP 3000.022#14.3 — never recount provisional receipt inputs.
                let group = self.receipt_read_group(m, &read.index, key)?;
                if self.beyond_horizon || self.receipt_maintain_pending(m, group.as_deref())? { return Ok((unavailable_aggregate(), 0)); }
                // The parent-key view is derived locally from fully held children;
                // its generated table need not have an invertible sync audience.
                let fields = crate::small_map::collect(index.columns().into_iter().zip(key.iter().cloned()));
                let row = self.live_aggregate_inner(m, Value::Row(fields.into()))?.unwrap_or(Value::Null);
                *emitted = true;
                // Key-authorized and computed from the caller's key and the disclosed total: sealed without a lookup.
                let sealed = self.authorized_synthetic(table, row);
                let price = sealed.heap_bytes() + sealed.projection_heap() + sealed.materialization_heap();
                return Ok((sealed.into_value(), price));
            }
        }
        if self.filter_depth > 0 && !self.read_charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, 1)? { return Ok((Value::Null, 0)); }
        if self.device && table.sync.is_none() {
            self.unknown_fact(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
            self.components.note(|frame| frame.speculative = true);
            self.withhold_unheld_card(&read.table);
        }
        if key.iter().any(|value| *value == Value::Null) && !index.columns().iter().zip(key).all(|(column, value)| *value != Value::Null || table.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|kind| kind.is_optional())) {
            // A null key on a required column addresses nothing.
            return Ok((Value::Null, 0));
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — reserve authorization before knowing hit, miss or hidden; an exhausted probe is not a false verdict to roll back.
        let trusted = key_admitted || self.context.is_system() || self.trust_synced_read(table);
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — prediction keeps the server's reservation even for
        // delivered rows; a key-admitted aggregate has already paid its rule.
        let prepaid = !self.context.is_system() && !key_admitted;
        if prepaid && !self.read_charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))? { return Ok((Value::Null, 0)); }
        // @ref LLP 3000.013#14.7 — the requested key authorizes a
        // missing aggregate before its logical zero is disclosed. A hidden hit
        // and hidden miss are the same unavailable value.
        let authorize_missing_group = maintained.is_some() && !key_admitted;
        let tables_mark = self.read_tables.clone();
        let mut absent = false;
        let found = self.authorized_point(read, index, key, prepaid, trusted, maintained.is_some(), &mut absent)?;
        let Some(row) = found else {
            if absent && authorize_missing_group {
                // The same reservation owns a live row or a logical zero.
                let mut fields: BTreeMap<_, _> = crate::small_map::collect(index.columns().into_iter().zip(key.iter().cloned()));
                let m = maintained.expect("maintained aggregate");
                if !m.by.iter().all(|field| fields.contains_key(field)) { return Ok((unavailable_aggregate(), 0)); }
                let group: Vec<_> = m.by.iter().map(|field| fields[field].clone()).collect();
                fields.insert("id".into(), Value::Id(crate::schema::maintained_id(&group)));
                fields.insert("value".into(), Value::Int(0));
                let zero = Value::Row(fields.into());
                // The rule was prepaid above; its witnesses run on the restoring
                // scope. This is a synthetic image, so a device must evaluate its
                // key rule rather than trust delivery of a row it never received.
                let authorized = self.authorized_scratch(0, &[], |runner, _| {
                    if runner.context.is_system() || runner.authorize_read(&read.table, &zero, true)? {
                        Ok(Some(runner.authorized_synthetic(table, zero)))
                    } else { Ok(None) }
                })?;
                if self.read_capped() { return Ok((Value::Null, 0)); }
                return Ok(match authorized {
                    Some(row) => { self.next_expiry.inherit(row.expiry()); *emitted = true; (Value::Null, 0) }
                    None => { self.read_tables = tables_mark; (unavailable_aggregate(), 0) }
                });
            }
            if absent {
                // An authorized missing aggregate emits its logical zero: null here,
                // which the compiled read coerces to 0 (LLP 3000.007 §20.21).
                *emitted = key_admitted;
                return Ok((Value::Null, 0));
            }
            self.read_tables = tables_mark;
            return Ok((if maintained.is_some() { unavailable_aggregate() } else { Value::Null }, 0));
        };
        let price = row.heap_bytes() + row.projection_heap() + row.materialization_heap();
        let mut value = row.into_value();
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5 — retained history stays marked through projections; ordinary retained
        // point reads keep their established component composition (the row-level fallback is read-through's).
        if self.device && table.through.is_some() && self.context.row_provenance && self.retained_row(&read.table, &value)? {
            if let Value::Row(fields) = &mut value { fields.mark_retained(); }
        }
        let value = self.sequence_read_result(&read.table, value)?;
        *emitted = true;
        Ok((if matches!(self.timeline_authority, Some(super::timeline_authority::Authority::Transition(_))) { value }
            else { super::recorded::public_image(table, value) }, price))
    }
}
            let allowance = [(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))];
            if self.meter.cut == crate::meter::Cut::Abort { self.authorized_scan(&allowance, |_, _| Ok(()))?; }
            else { self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &allowance, |_, _| Ok(()))?; }
            return Ok(true);
        }
        Ok(false)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — source invalidation is schema-derived, including empty and denied groups.
    pub(super) fn aggregate_source_dependency(&mut self, table: &str) {
        if let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            && self.schema.tables[&maintain.source].is_expiring() {
            self.read_tables.insert(maintain.source.clone());
        }
    }

    fn prefix_verdict(&mut self, name: &str, table: &Table, index: &Index, prefix: &[Value], rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr, uniform: bool) -> Result<(bool, super::expiry::Pending), RuntimeError> {
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        if let Some(through) = &table.through && let Some(id) = index.columns().iter().zip(prefix).find_map(|(name,v)| (name == &through.reference).then_some(v)) {
            let verdict = self.parent_verdict(name,through,id)?;
            if verdict == super::through::ParentVerdict::Unknown { self.unknown_fact(name, crate::schema::through::CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], "read through parent"); }
            let admitted = verdict == super::through::ParentVerdict::Readable;
            return Ok((admitted, if admitted { expiry.defer() } else { Default::default() },
            ));
        }
        let probe_row = prefix_row(index, prefix);
        let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — one prepaid, restoring scope: a partial conjunction denial costs the same static allowance as a uniform denial.
        let verdict = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(rule), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))], |runner, allowance| {
            rules::evaluate(rule, &viewer, None, &probe_row, false, &mut PrefixProbe { runner, allowance })
        });
        self.trace_authorization(&verdict, name, Effect::Read, "read rule");
        let verdict = verdict?;
        if !verdict { self.filtered_clause(name, "read rule"); }
        let admitted = verdict && !self.read_capped();
        Ok((admitted, if uniform && admitted { expiry.defer() } else { Default::default() },
        ))
    }

    /// A page guard, judged once for the caller (`rules::page_guard_decision`):
    /// a row-independent grant, or a disjunct whose row columns the prefix pins,
    /// evaluated with the prefix's values. It charges the read rule's static
    /// probe count on either verdict, and records no filtered clause: a false
    /// guard leaves the per-row walk.
    fn grant_verdict(&mut self, table: &Table, index: &Index, prefix: &[Value], rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> Result<(bool, super::expiry::Pending), RuntimeError> {
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let probe_row = prefix_row(index, prefix);
        let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
        let verdict = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(rule), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))], |runner, allowance| {
            rules::evaluate(rule, &viewer, None, &probe_row, false, &mut PrefixProbe { runner, allowance })
        })?;
        let admitted = verdict && !self.read_capped();
        Ok((admitted, if admitted { expiry.defer() } else { Default::default() },
        ))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9 — coverage applies before a missing candidate or empty page can return zero/complete.
    pub(super) fn aggregate_read_coverage(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], site: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        {
            return self.fetch(allowance, table_name, id);
        }
        self.schema.refuse_private(table_name)?;
        let table = self
            .schema
            .table(table_name)
            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        if self.device {
            self.unknown_fact(
                table_name,
                "byId",
                &[Value::Id(id.to_owned())],
                "scheduled write target",
            );
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let found = self.authorized_scratch(
            crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table),
            &[],
            |runner, _| {
                runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id).map(|found| {
                    found.map(|mut row| {
                        materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                        row
                    })
                })
            },
        )?;
        Ok(found.filter(|row| !self.expired(table, row)))
    }

    /// The scan's prefix values, evaluated. A word term is not a value a
    /// store can seek to; `scan_words` takes those scans apart first.
    pub(super) fn prefix_values(&mut self, scan: &Scan) -> Result<Vec<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        scan.prefix
            .iter()
            .map(|key| match key {
                IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(expr) => self.expr(expr),
                IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(_) => Err(RuntimeError::Schema("a matching term is read through its words(..) index, never as a prefix value".into())),
            })
            .collect()
    }

    /// A `matching` read: the query's first term selects every word of the
    /// index it is a prefix of (one seek each, bounded by the meter for a
    /// uniform scope, otherwise at most `MAX_WORD_LANES`),
    /// and those words are merged as ordered lanes into one page, newest
    /// first when the index says so; every further term must also be a
    /// prefix of some word of the row (a filter over the page, never a
    /// second index). An empty term list matches nothing: its page is empty,
    /// not everything.
    fn scan_words(&mut self, scan: &Scan) -> Result<Vec<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.1 — a uniform matching scan is always lazy: the merge position appends
        // the row id when the ordering suffix lacks it.
        if rules::scan_visibility(self.schema, scan).uniform { return self.merge_lazy(None, Some(scan)); }
//! Development doors beside the operation routes: `POST /seed`, `POST /dev/reset`, and the
//! `--no-watch` hint that names a schema edited on disk after adoption.
use super::{Shared, lock, refusal_json, respond_timed};
use crate::project::{Project, SourceStamp};
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
use snapback4_core::error::Refusal;
use snapback4_core::wire;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::sync::Mutex;
use tiny_http::Request;

/// The source stamp each `--no-watch` dev host adopted, by project root.
static PINNED: Mutex<BTreeMap<PathBuf, SourceStamp>> = Mutex::new(BTreeMap::new());

pub(super) fn pin(project: &Path, stamp: SourceStamp) {
    PINNED.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).insert(project.to_owned(), stamp);
}

/// A `--no-watch` host serves the schema it started with. When a request
/// names an operation or argument that host does not know and the `.q` files
/// changed on disk since, say so: the edit is real, it was just never adopted.
pub(super) fn stale(shared: &Shared, refusal: Refusal) -> Refusal {
    let Some(adopted) = PINNED.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).get(&shared.project).copied() else { return refusal };
    if (Project { root: shared.project.clone() }).sources_stamp() == adopted { return refusal; }
    let message = format!("{}; this dev host runs with --no-watch and snapback/ changed on disk after it adopted its schema, so the edit is not served", refusal.message);
    Refusal { message, rewrite: Some("restart `snapback4 dev` (or drop --no-watch so saves re-adopt), then retry".into()), ..refusal }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — persona names are not their principals; dev only.
pub(super) fn persona_argument(shared: &Shared, kinds: &BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::ir::ArgType>, args: &Json) -> Option<Refusal> {
    if !shared.personas { return None; }
    let bound = lock(&shared.bound);
    crate::persona_principals::arguments(&bound.personas,kinds,args)
}
// Rows are written as the system principal; each names its id because seed is an upsert by id.
pub(super) fn seed(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
    if !shared.personas || !request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|a| a.ip().is_loopback()) {
        respond_timed(request, shared, 403, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "seeding is a development door")) }));
        return;
    }
    let refuse = |request, refusal: Refusal| respond_timed(request, shared, 400, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&refusal.guide("personas")) }));
    let rows: Vec<Json> = match serde_json::from_str(body) {
        Ok(Json::Array(rows)) => rows,
        _ => return refuse(request, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "seed takes a JSON array of {table, row}")),
    };
    let typed = match typed_rows(shared, &rows) {
        Ok(typed) => typed,
        Err(refusal) => return refuse(request, refusal),
    };
    let outcome = lock(&shared.engine).seed_at(typed, shared.now());
    match outcome {
        Ok(count) => {
            shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine));
            respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "seeded": count }));
        }
        Err(error) => respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&seed_refusal(error)) })),
    }
}

/// `/seed` rows checked against the served schema and typed, or the first problem.
fn typed_rows(shared: &Shared, rows: &[Json]) -> Result<Vec<(String, snapback4_core::Value)>, Refusal> {
    let schema = lock(&shared.bound).schema.clone();
    let mut typed = Vec::new();
    for (index, entry) in rows.iter().enumerate() {
        let (Some(table), Some(row)) = (entry.get("table").and_then(Json::as_str), entry.get("row")) else {
            return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "each seed entry is {table, row}"));
        };
        let Some(definition) = schema.table(table) else {
            return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("unknown table {table}")));
        };
        if row.is_object() && row.get("id").is_none_or(|id| !id.is_string()) {
            return Err(missing_id(index, table));
        }
### 5.2 Stage 2: a small Lean core

PAGE2 extends this operation fragment under [LLP 3000.036.001 §15](./3000.036.001-merged-pages-for-owner-or-x-lists.rfc.md#15-page2-ordinary-exact-memberships-row-ids-and-parent-owners) and [LLP 3000.036.003 §7](./3000.036.003-time-audiences-and-timed-disclosure.rfc.md#7-formal-and-generated-semantics): exact membership/byId classification and pinned-clock lower read lifetime, including physical write occupancy. Durable opening publication and clock-floor protocol remain runtime/simulator/device obligations, not Lean proof claims.

**Scope.** It starts after Stage 1's generator covers the core fragment.
The core is the value model, expressions, point reads and scans in key
order, the four rules, and the completeness flag. It is a Lake project at
`snapback4/lean/` with a pinned toolchain and no Mathlib unless a proof
needs it.

**2026-10-13 — FLAKE oracle refresh.** Every default-path `lean::refresh`
holds one blocking file lock under this checkout's ignored `.lake` directory
until `lake build quarry-oracle` exits. The lock covers both threads and
processes and is retained, never unlinked after release. Concurrent cold
refreshes previously rewrote the same generated C/object files: the default-
thread test suite saw null-filled C input, missing objects and compiler crashes.
Each refresh still checks current sources; there is no once-per-process cache
that could conceal a model edit. Explicit `SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE` retains its
caller-vouched bypass. A stale/corrupt local build is repaired before validating
the fix; no model or engine semantics change.

**The proofs:**

- determinism;
- the value order is total and agrees with the key encoding;
- no read discloses a row its rules hide;
- no write succeeds without write authority;
- `complete: true` is never reported for a read that omitted a qualifying
  row.

**Independence.** The model's author works from the language card
(`snapback4 guide language`), LLP 3000.007 and the value-model LLPs, and
does not read the binder or the interpreter. Underspecified points go to an
`OPEN.md` beside the model and are resolved there, with the engine's
behaviour stated, before they are encoded.

**The emitter and the comparison.** A Lean emitter in `snapback4-lang`
(beside `emit/rust.rs`) embeds programs in the core fragment, and reports
any other program as unsupported. The Stage 1 harness gains the Lean model
as a further oracle on that fragment. The model is not normative: when it
and the engine disagree, the disagreement is classified as a Rust defect, a
model defect, or an open language question, and an open question comes to
Charlie with both behaviours stated.

**Widening.** The model grows to a further area (the meter, expiry,
ephemeral tables, maintained columns or residency) only when that area has
been stable long enough for the model to keep up. This record leaves "long
enough" to a later revision, which should measure it from the area's own
commit history.

**Status (2026-10-08): the model and its proofs are built.** The model is a
Lake project at `snapback4/lean/`, pinned to Lean 4.34.1, with no Mathlib.
`./check.sh` runs `lake build` (about 15 s cold). It refuses any `sorry`,
`admit`, `native_decide` or `axiom`, audits 38 headline theorems for
standard axioms only, and runs 30 hand-written examples from the guide
cards. All 30 agree.

The model was written under the independence rule. It is an executable
big-step semantics of the core fragment:
- `next` on an exactly filled page (C2);
- the precedence among refusal codes (W1–W4).

**Status (2026-10-08): the emitter and the Lean oracle are built.**

- **The emitter** (`snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean.rs`) translates a checked
  program's surface tree, not the binder's IR, so the binder stays under
  test. It takes the few types it needs (literal typing beside ids,
  positional keys) from the declarations the checker accepted. Anything
  outside the fragment is a named unsupported reason, never a partial
  translation. `snapback4 check --emit-lean <file.q>` prints one file.
- **The runner** is `quarry-oracle` (`snapback4/lean/Oracle.lean`): one JSON
  case per line in, one tagged outcome per line out, one process per worker.
  The model's semantics were not changed for I/O.
- **The oracle** is `snapback4-difftest run-lean`. It compares the engine
  (SQLite, default ceilings) with the model on kind, refusal code, tagged
  data, `complete` and `next` presence. Each side then continues from its own
  cursor, and a mutation's `observe` reread runs on each side's resulting

/// Expression translation in one lexical scope.
struct Scope<'a> {
    module: &'a LeanModule,
    helpers: &'a [String],
    vars: BTreeMap<String, Ty>,
    /// The table a `.col` names (in a `where` or an update condition).
    focal: Option<String>,
}

impl<'a> Scope<'a> {
    fn new(module: &'a LeanModule, helpers: &'a [String]) -> Self {
        Self { module, helpers, vars: BTreeMap::new(), focal: None }
    }

    fn table(&self, name: &str) -> R<&'a Table> {
        match self.module.tables.iter().find(|t| t.name == name) {
            Some(t) => Ok(t),
            None => no("table-unknown", name),
        }
    }

    fn with_focal(&self, table: &str) -> Scope<'a> {
        Scope { module: self.module, helpers: self.helpers, vars: self.vars.clone(), focal: Some(table.to_owned()) }
    }

    /// The static type of `e`, as far as the fragment's literal typing and
    /// key resolution need it.
    fn ty(&self, e: &Expr) -> Ty {
        match e {
            Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer" => Ty::Scalar(Kind::Id(IdSort::Principal)),
            Expr::Name { name, .. } => self.vars.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown),
            Expr::Literal { value, .. } => match literal_scalar(value) {
                Ok((_, Some(k))) => Ty::Scalar(k),
                _ => Ty::Unknown,
            },
            Expr::Member { base, name, .. } => {
                let table = match base.as_ref() {
                    Expr::Focal { .. } => self.focal.clone(),
                    other => match self.ty(other) {
                        Ty::Row(t) | Ty::Scalar(Kind::Id(IdSort::Ref(t))) => Some(t),
                        _ => None,
                    },
                };
                table
                    .and_then(|t| self.module.tables.iter().find(|x| x.name == t).and_then(|x| x.col(name)))
                    .map(Ty::Scalar)
                    .unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown)
            }
            Expr::Read(read) if read.range.is_none() && read.filter.is_none() && !read.keys.is_empty() => Ty::Row(read.table.clone()),
            Expr::Call { name, .. } if name == "count" || CALENDAR.contains(&name.as_str()) => Ty::Scalar(Kind::Int),
            Expr::Exists { .. } => Ty::Scalar(Kind::Bool),
            Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } => if op == "not" { Ty::Scalar(Kind::Bool) } else { self.ty(value) },
            Expr::Binary { op, left, .. } => match op.as_str() {
                "+" | "-" | "*" | "/" | "%" | "??" => self.ty(left),
                _ => Ty::Scalar(Kind::Bool),
            },
            Expr::Assert { value, .. } => self.ty(value),
            Expr::If { then, .. } => self.ty(then),
            _ => Ty::Unknown,
        }
    }

    fn kind(&self, e: &Expr) -> Option<Kind> {
        match self.ty(e) {
            Ty::Scalar(k) => Some(k),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// `e` as a value of kind `want`: a text literal for an id is an id.
    fn expr_as(&self, e: &Expr, want: Option<&Kind>) -> R<Json> {
        if matches!(want,Some(Kind::Id(IdSort::Ref(_)))) && self.ty(e)==Ty::Unknown {
            return no("reference-expression-type",crate::print::expression(e));
        let kinds: Vec<Option<Kind>> = keys.iter().map(|k| self.kind(&k.value)).collect();
        let columns = resolve_key(table, keys, &kinds)?;
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for (c, k) in columns.iter().zip(keys) {
            out.push(json!([c, self.expr_as(&k.value, table.col(c).as_ref())?]));
        }
        Ok(out)
    }

    /// A `where` domain as pins and a residual filter (`Syntax.lean`): each
    /// `.col = e` conjunct whose `e` does not read the row is a pin.
    fn domain(&self, read: &ReadExpr, keyed: bool) -> R<(Vec<Json>, Json)> {
        if read.index.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.matching.is_some() || !read.computed.is_empty() || read.cursor_prefix.is_some() {
            return no("read-form", crate::print::read_expr(read));
        }
        let table = self.table(&read.table)?;
        let mut pins = if read.keys.is_empty() {
            Vec::new()
        } else if keyed && read.filter.is_none() {
            self.key(table, &read.keys)?
        } else {
            return no("keyed-range", crate::print::read_expr(read));
        };
        let focal = self.with_focal(&read.table);
        let mut residual = Vec::new();
        let mut conjuncts = Vec::new();
        if let Some(f) = &read.filter {
            flatten_and(f, &mut conjuncts);
        }
        for c in conjuncts {
            if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = c {
                if op == "=" {
                    let pin = match (column_of(left), column_of(right)) {
                        (Some(col), None) if !reads_row(right) => Some((col, right)),
                        (None, Some(col)) if !reads_row(left) => Some((col, left)),
                        _ => None,
                    };
                    if let Some((col, value)) = pin {
                        let Some(kind) = table.col(col) else { return no("focal-column", col.to_owned()) };
                        pins.push(json!([col, self.expr_as(value, Some(&kind))?]));
                        continue;
                    }
                }
            }
            residual.push(focal.expr(c)?);
        }
        let filter = residual.into_iter().reduce(|a, b| json!({"and": [a, b]})).unwrap_or(Json::Null);
        Ok((pins, filter))
    }

    fn read(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> R<Json> {
        let print = || crate::print::read_expr(read);
        if let Some(page) = pages::implicit_page(self.module, read) { return self.read(&page); }
        if let Some(reason) = pages::static_key_issue(read) { return no(reason, print()); }
        if let Some(reason) = pages::membership_physical_issue(self.module, read) {
            return no(reason, print());
        }
        if let Some(reason) = pages::compound_physical_issue(self.module, read) { return no(reason, print()); }
        match &read.range {
            None if read.filter.is_none() && !read.keys.is_empty() => {
                if read.index.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.matching.is_some() || !read.computed.is_empty() {
                    return no("read-form", print());
                }
                let table = self.table(&read.table)?;
                Ok(json!({"point": {"table": read.table, "key": self.key(table, &read.keys)?}}))
            }
            None => no("read-unbounded", print()),
            Some(range) => {
                if !read.keys.is_empty() {
                    return no("keyed-range", print());
                }
                let (pins, filter) = self.domain(read, false)?;
                let table = self.table(&read.table)?;
                for c in &read.order_keys {
                    if c != "id" && table.col(c).is_none() {
                        return no("order-column", c.clone());
                    }
                }
                // `by .x, .id` is `by .x`: `id` always breaks ties.
                let mut order = read.order_keys.clone();
                if order.last().is_some_and(|c| c == "id") {
                    order.pop();
                }
                if order.iter().any(|c| c == "id") {
                    return no("order-id-inside", print());
                }
                let after = match &range.cursor {
                    Some(c) => self.expr(c)?,
                    None => Json::Null,
                };
                Ok(json!({"page": {"table": read.table, "pins": pins, "filter": filter, "order": order,
                    "desc": range.order == Order::Desc, "take": range.take, "after": after}}))
            }
        }
    }

    fn write(&self, e: &Expr) -> R<Json> {
        let Expr::Write { op, table, keys, fields, if_absent, condition, .. } = e else { unreachable!() };
        if *op != WriteOp::Insert && keys.is_empty() { return no("row-variable-write", crate::print::expression(e)); }
        let t = self.table(table)?;
        if !t.caps.is_empty() && *op != WriteOp::Delete {
            return no("table-cap", format!("{} writes capped table {table}; the model does not enforce caps", crate::print::expression(e)));
        }
        Ok(match op {
            WriteOp::Insert => {
                if !keys.is_empty() || condition.is_some() {
                    return no("insert-form", crate::print::expression(e));
                }
                json!({"insert": {"table": table, "fields": self.fields(fields, Some(t))?, "ifAbsent": if_absent}})
            }
            WriteOp::Upsert => {
                if *if_absent || condition.is_some() {
                    return no("upsert-form", crate::print::expression(e));
                }
                json!({"upsert": {"table": table, "key": self.key(t, keys)?, "patch": self.fields(fields, Some(t))?}})
            }
            WriteOp::Update => {
                if *if_absent {
                    return no("update-form", crate::print::expression(e));
                }
                let cond = match condition {
                    Some(c) => self.with_focal(table).expr(c)?,
                    None => Json::Null,
                };
                json!({"update": {"table": table, "key": self.key(t, keys)?, "patch": self.fields(fields, Some(t))?, "cond": cond}})
            }
            WriteOp::Delete => {
                if *if_absent || condition.is_some() || !fields.is_empty() {
                    return no("delete-form", crate::print::expression(e));
                }
                json!({"delete": {"table": table, "key": self.key(t, keys)?}})
            }
        })
    }

    fn stmts(&mut self, body: &[Stmt]) -> R<Vec<Json>> {
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for s in body {
            match s {
                Stmt::Bind { names, value, .. } => {
                    let [name] = names.as_slice() else { return no("bind-destructure", names.join(", ")) };
                    if matches!(value, Expr::Write { .. }) {
                        out.push(json!({"write": {"bind": name, "op": self.write(value)?}}));
                        let ty = match value {
                            Expr::Write { table, .. } => Ty::Row(table.clone()),
                            _ => Ty::Unknown,
                        };
            index.is_plain() && columns.first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
                && deadline.is_none_or(|deadline| columns.get(1).map(String::as_str) == Some(deadline))
        }).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str())
    }

    /// The cap bounding a reference's children: `cap N by <column>` exactly,
    /// or one when the reference itself is a plain unique key. A nullable
    /// reference may have many nulls, but a parent delete probes one non-null
    /// parent id, whose unique group still has at most one row.
    pub fn reference_cap(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<u32> {
        let table = self.table(child)?;
        let declared = table.constraints.iter().find_map(|c| match c {
            Constraint::Cap { by, max } if by.len() == 1 && by[0] == column => Some(*max),
            _ => None,
        });
        let unique = table.indexes.values().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns() == [column]).then_some(1);
        declared.into_iter().chain(unique).min()
    }

    /// Whether `cap` on `table` skips a group whose reference is null: only a
    /// `set null` reference's own cap does (a null reference has no parent).
    pub fn cap_skips_null(&self, table: &Table, by: &[String], group: &[Value]) -> bool {
        by.iter().zip(group).any(|(column, value)| *value == Value::Null && table.on_delete.get(column) == Some(&OnDelete::SetNull))
    }

    /// The tables a delete of a `parent` row may write through its writing
    /// actions, and the tables its actions read. A function of the schema, so

//! A table-typed argument is an id with a stronger name. Storing it in a
//! reference column still does not prove that the target row exists.

use super::catalog::Catalog;
use crate::{
    ast::{Expr, FunctionBody, ProgramKind, ReadExpr, Stmt, TypeKind, WriteOp},
    diag::{Diagnostic, Site},
};
use snapback4_core::schema::{Constraint, RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Schema};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

#[derive(Clone)]
struct Candidate {
    argument: String,
    target: String,
    child: String,
    column: String,
    op: WriteOp,
    site: Site,
    checked: bool,
}

#[derive(Clone)]
struct Flow {
    /// Arguments whose name still denotes the mutation input, rather than a
    /// later binding with the same spelling.
    inputs: BTreeSet<String>,
    checked: BTreeSet<String>,
}

/// Name a likely missing existence check. Known helpers count only when their
/// body actually looks up the argument; passing an id through does not.
// @ref LLP 3000.039#7 — reference types validate id shape, never target
// existence; `N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED` points to the explicit lookup form.
pub(super) fn notes(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    let mut notes = Vec::new();
    for doc in &catalog.documents {
        for program in doc
            .programs
            .iter()
            .filter(|program| program.kind == ProgramKind::Mutation && !program.native)
        {
            let arguments: BTreeMap<String, String> = program
                .args
                .iter()
                .filter_map(|arg| match &arg.type_.kind {
                    TypeKind::Ref { table } => Some((arg.name.clone(), table.clone())),
                    _ => None,
                })
                .collect();
            if arguments.is_empty() {
                continue;
            }

            let mut candidates = Vec::new();
            let flow = Flow {
                inputs: arguments.keys().cloned().collect(),
                checked: BTreeSet::new(),
            };
            let body: Vec<_> = program
                .prelude
                .iter()
                .chain(&program.body)
                .cloned()
                .collect();
            flow_statements(catalog, schema, &arguments, &body, flow, &mut candidates);

            let mut emitted = BTreeSet::new();
            for candidate in candidates {
                if candidate.checked
                    || write_contract_checks(schema, &candidate)
                    || !emitted.insert(candidate.argument.clone())
                {
                    continue;
                }
                notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&candidate.site, "N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED",
                    format!("mutation {} stores argument {} directly in {}.{}, but never looks up {}[{}]: a reference accepts any well-formed id even when the row is absent", program.name, candidate.argument, candidate.child, candidate.column, candidate.target, candidate.argument),
                    format!("bind `parent = {}[{}] ! else NOT_FOUND` before the write, then store `parent.id`; keep the direct id only when dangling references are intentional", candidate.target, candidate.argument)).guide("types"));
            }
        }
    }
    notes
}

/// Follow only paths that can reach a write. A lookup fact joins by
/// intersection, so one conditional arm, a short-circuited operand, or a
/// possibly-empty loop can never authorize a later candidate.
fn flow_statements(
    catalog: &Catalog,
    schema: &Schema,
    arguments: &BTreeMap<String, String>,
    body: &[Stmt],
    mut flow: Flow,
    candidates: &mut Vec<Candidate>,
) -> Option<Flow> {
            }
            Stmt::Break { .. } | Stmt::Continue { .. } => return None,
        }
    }
    Some(flow)
}

fn join_flows(left: Option<Flow>, right: Option<Flow>) -> Option<Flow> {
    match (left, right) {
        (Some(mut left), Some(right)) => {
            left.inputs.retain(|name| right.inputs.contains(name));
            left.checked.retain(|name| right.checked.contains(name));
            Some(left)
        }
        (Some(flow), None) | (None, Some(flow)) => Some(flow),
        (None, None) => None,
    }
}

fn flow_expression(
    catalog: &Catalog,
    schema: &Schema,
    arguments: &BTreeMap<String, String>,
    expression: &Expr,
    flow: &mut Flow,
    candidates: &mut Vec<Candidate>,
) {
    let child = |value: &Expr, flow: &mut Flow, candidates: &mut Vec<Candidate>| {
        flow_expression(catalog, schema, arguments, value, flow, candidates)
    };
    match expression {
        Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } => {
            flow_read(catalog, schema, arguments, read, flow, candidates);
            for (name, target) in arguments {
                if flow.inputs.contains(name)
                    && target == &read.table
                    && read_checks_target_id(schema, read, name, target)
                {
                    flow.checked.insert(name.clone());
                }
            }
        }
        Expr::Created { read, .. } => flow_read(catalog, schema, arguments, read, flow, candidates),
        Expr::Unary { value, .. }
        | Expr::Member { base: value, .. }
        | Expr::Assert { value, .. }
        | Expr::ShapeUse { base: value, .. }
        | Expr::Walk { seed: value, .. } => child(value, flow, candidates),
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. }
            if matches!(op.as_str(), "and" | "or" | "??") => {
            child(left, flow, candidates);
            if lazy_right_must_run(op, left) {
                child(right, flow, candidates);
            } else {
                flow_conditional_expression(catalog, schema, arguments, right, flow, candidates);
            }
        }
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => {
            child(left, flow, candidates);
            child(right, flow, candidates);
        }
        Expr::Array { items, .. } => {
            for value in items {
                child(value, flow, candidates);
            }
        }
        Expr::Call { name, args, .. } => {
            for value in args {
                child(value, flow, candidates);
            }
            for (position, argument) in args.iter().enumerate() {
                let Expr::Name { name: argument, .. } = argument else {
                    continue;
                };
                let Some(target) = arguments
                    .get(argument)
                    .filter(|_| flow.inputs.contains(argument))
                else {
                    continue;
                };
                if helper_looks_up(
                    catalog,
                    schema,
                    name,
                    position,
                    target,
                    &mut BTreeSet::new(),
                ) {
                    flow.checked.insert(argument.clone());
                }
            }
        }
        Expr::Object { fields, .. } => {
            for value in fields.iter().filter_map(|field| field.value.as_ref()) {
                child(value, flow, candidates);
            }
        }
        Expr::Shape { base, fields, .. } => {
            child(base, flow, candidates);
            for field in fields {
                if let crate::ast::ShapeField::Field { value, .. } = field {
                    flow_conditional_expression(catalog, schema, arguments, value, flow, candidates);
                }
            }
        }
        Expr::If {
            condition,
            then,
            otherwise,
            ..
        } => {
            let (mut then_flow, mut else_flow) = flow_condition(
                catalog,
                schema,
            helper_expression(catalog, schema, value, &mut state, target, stack);
            for name in names { state.aliases.remove(name); }
            if alias { state.aliases.insert(names[0].clone()); }
            normal(state)
        }
        Stmt::Expr { value, .. } => {
            helper_expression(catalog, schema, value, &mut state, target, stack);
            normal(state)
        }
        Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => {
            let (yes, _) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, condition, state, target, stack);
            normal(yes)
        }
        Stmt::Return { value, .. } => {
            helper_expression(catalog, schema, value, &mut state, target, stack);
            HelperFlow { returned: Some(state), ..HelperFlow::default() }
        }
        Stmt::Emit { payload, once, .. } => {
            helper_expression(catalog, schema, payload, &mut state, target, stack);
            if let Some(once) = once { helper_expression(catalog, schema, once, &mut state, target, stack); }
            normal(state)
        }
        Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, .. } => {
            let (then_entry, else_entry) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, condition, state, target, stack);
            join_helper_flows(
                helper_statements(catalog, schema, then_body, then_entry, target, stack),
                helper_statements(catalog, schema, else_body, else_entry, target, stack),
            )
        }
        Stmt::For { name, domain, body, .. } => {
            helper_for(catalog, schema, name, domain, body, state, target, stack)
        }
        Stmt::As { principal, body, .. } => {
            helper_expression(catalog, schema, principal, &mut state, target, stack);
            helper_statements(catalog, schema, body, state, target, stack)
        }
        Stmt::Break { .. } => HelperFlow { broken: Some(state), ..HelperFlow::default() },
        Stmt::Continue { .. } => HelperFlow { continued: Some(state), ..HelperFlow::default() },
    }
}

fn helper_for(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, name: &str, domain: &Expr, body: &[Stmt], mut state: HelperState, target: &str, stack: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> HelperFlow {
    helper_expression(catalog, schema, domain, &mut state, target, stack);
    if matches!(domain, Expr::Array { items, .. } if items.is_empty()) {
        return HelperFlow { normal: Some(state), ..HelperFlow::default() };
    }
    let outer_alias = state.aliases.contains(name);
    let mut first = state.clone();
    first.aliases.remove(name);
    let mut header = first.clone();
    let mut returned = None;
    let mut broken = None;
    let mut iteration_exit = None;
    loop {
        let body_flow = helper_statements(catalog, schema, body, header.clone(), target, stack);
        returned = join_helper_states(returned, body_flow.returned);
        broken = join_helper_states(broken, body_flow.broken);
        let back = join_helper_states(body_flow.normal, body_flow.continued);
        iteration_exit = join_helper_states(iteration_exit, back.clone());
        let Some(mut back) = back else { break };
        // Every iteration introduces a fresh loop binding. Assigning that name
        // cannot restore or mutate the shadowed outer formal.
        back.aliases.remove(name);
        let next = join_helper_states(Some(first.clone()), Some(back)).unwrap();
        if next == header { break }
        header = next;
    }
    let restore = |state: Option<HelperState>| state.map(|mut state| {
        if outer_alias { state.aliases.insert(name.to_owned()); } else { state.aliases.remove(name); }
        state
    });
    let normal = join_helper_states(
        Some(state),
        join_helper_states(restore(iteration_exit), restore(broken)),
    );
    HelperFlow { normal, returned, ..HelperFlow::default() }
}

fn helper_condition(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, expression: &Expr, state: HelperState, target: &str, stack: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> (HelperState, HelperState) {
    match expression {
        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "not" => {
            let (yes, no) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, value, state, target, stack);
            (no, yes)
        }
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" => {
            let (left_yes, left_no) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, left, state, target, stack);
            let (right_yes, right_no) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, right, left_yes, target, stack);
            (right_yes, join_helper_states(Some(left_no), Some(right_no)).unwrap())
        }
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => {
            let (left_yes, left_no) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, left, state, target, stack);
            let (right_yes, right_no) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, right, left_no, target, stack);
            (join_helper_states(Some(left_yes), Some(right_yes)).unwrap(), right_no)
        }
        Expr::Binary { op,left,right,.. } if op=="=" || op=="!=" => {
            let mut evaluated=state;
            helper_expression(catalog,schema,expression,&mut evaluated,target,stack);
            let mut yes=evaluated.clone();let mut no=evaluated;
            let alias=match (left.as_ref(),right.as_ref()) {
                (Expr::Name {name,..},Expr::Literal {value:crate::ast::Literal::Null,..})|
                (Expr::Literal {value:crate::ast::Literal::Null,..},Expr::Name {name,..})=>Some(name),_=>None,
            };
            if alias.is_some_and(|name|yes.aliases.contains(name)) {if op=="=" {yes.checked=true;} else {no.checked=true;}}
            (yes,no)
        }
        _ => {
            let mut evaluated = state;
            helper_expression(catalog, schema, expression, &mut evaluated, target, stack);
            (evaluated.clone(), evaluated)
        }
    }
}

//! The online-only mutation route and a wake state independent of the writer.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::engine::ephemeral::{Hub, Snapshot};

pub(super) struct Wake {
    pub seq: u64,
    pub store_id: Option<String>,
    pub generation: u64,
    pub eph: Snapshot,
}
impl Wake {
    pub fn new(engine: &Engine) -> Self {
        Self {
            seq: engine.seq().unwrap_or(0),
            store_id: engine.store().meta("store_id").unwrap_or(None),
            generation: engine.generation(),
            eph: engine.ephemeral_hub().snapshot().expect("new hub"),
        }
    }
}
#[derive(Default)]
pub(super) struct Rate {
    buckets: Mutex<BTreeMap<String, (Instant, f64)>>,
    #[cfg(test)]
    fixed_now: Mutex<Option<Instant>>,
}
impl Rate {
    fn now(&self) -> Instant {
        #[cfg(test)]
        if let Some(now) = *lock(&self.fixed_now) {
            return now;
        }
        Instant::now()
    }
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub(super) fn set_now(&self, now: Instant) {
        *lock(&self.fixed_now) = Some(now);
    }
    pub fn admit(&self, key: String, now: Instant) -> bool {
        let mut buckets = lock(&self.buckets);
        buckets.retain(|_, (at, _)| now.saturating_duration_since(*at) < Duration::from_secs(60));
        if !buckets.contains_key(&key) && buckets.len() >= 4096 {
            return false;
        }
        let (at, tokens) = buckets.entry(key).or_insert((now, 40.0));
        *tokens = (*tokens + now.saturating_duration_since(*at).as_secs_f64() * 20.0).min(40.0);
        *at = now;
        if *tokens < 1.0 {
            false
        } else {
            *tokens -= 1.0;
            true
        }
    }
}
impl Shared {
    // @ref LLP 3000.017#3.3 — lock order engine → hub → wake.
    pub(super) fn install_ephemeral_wake(self: &Arc<Self>) {
        let owner = Arc::downgrade(self);
        self.ephemeral.set_publisher(move |snapshot| {
            if let Some(shared) = owner.upgrade() {
                let mut wake = lock(&shared.wake);
                wake.store_id = Some(snapshot.identity.clone());
                wake.generation = snapshot.generation;
                wake.eph = snapshot;
                drop(wake);
                shared.polls.notify_all();
            }
        }).expect("new hub publisher");
    }
    pub(super) fn durable_committed(&self, engine: &Engine) {
        self.publish_durable(engine, true);
    }
    pub(super) fn publish_engine(&self, engine: &Engine) {
        self.publish_durable(engine, false);
    }
    fn publish_durable(&self, engine: &Engine, committed: bool) {
        if let Ok(seq) = engine.seq() {
            let store_id = engine.store().meta("store_id").unwrap_or(None);
            let generation = engine.generation();
            let mut current = lock(&self.seq);
            let mut wake = lock(&self.wake);
            let changed = wake.seq != seq || wake.store_id != store_id || wake.generation != generation;
            *current = seq;
            wake.seq = seq;
            wake.store_id = store_id;
            wake.generation = generation;
            // The hub publisher alone owns wake.eph. Never wait for a running
            // ephemeral program while holding the serving engine here.
            if committed || changed {
                self.polls.notify_all();
                self.committed.notify_all();
            }
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn failed(request: Request, shared: &Shared, id: Option<&str>, why: Refusal) {
    let wake = lock(&shared.wake);
    let (store_id, head) = (wake.store_id.clone(), wake.seq);
    drop(wake);
    respond_timed(
        request,
        shared,
        200,
        super::mutation_reply(json!({"state":"failed","id":id,"why":refusal_json(&why),"store_id":store_id,"head":head})),
    );
}

pub(super) fn serve(request: Request, shared: &Shared, op: &str, body: &str) {
    let payload = serde_json::from_str::<Json>(if body.is_empty() { "{}" } else { body });
    let id = payload
        .as_ref()
        .ok()
        .and_then(|p| p.get("id"))
        .and_then(Json::as_str)
        .map(str::to_owned);
    let failed = |request, why| failed(request, shared, id.as_deref(), why);
    let payload = match payload {
        Ok(p) => p,
        Err(e) => {
            return failed(
                request,
                Refusal::new(
                    "E_INPUT",
                    "input",
                    format!("the request body is not JSON: {e}"),
                ),
            );
        }
    };
    let principal = match principal_of(&request, shared) {
        Ok(p) => p.0,
        Err(e) => return failed(request, e),
    };
    // A name may have been durable in a previous generation. Receipt custody
    // precedes signature decoding, rate admission and ephemeral execution.
    // @ref LLP 3000.022#6 — a stored success never executes a replacement body.
    let fingerprint = match delivery_fingerprint(&payload) {
        Ok(value) => value,
        Err(why) => return failed(request, why),
    };
    {
        let mut engine = producing_engine(shared);
        let preflight = mutation_authority(&request, shared, &engine, &principal)
            .and_then(|()| intent_store(&engine, &payload))
            .and_then(|()| {
                let args = payload.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or(json!({}));
                id.as_deref().map(|id| engine.replay_mutation(&principal, id, op, &args))
                    .transpose().map(Option::flatten).map_err(|e| e.refusal())
            });
        match preflight {
            Ok(Some(out)) => {
                let (store_id, head) = match response_head(&mut engine) {
                    Ok(value) => value,
                    Err(why) => return respond_timed(request, shared, 503, json!({"denied":why})),
                };
                let mut reply = out.reply(id.as_deref());
                reply["store_id"] = json!(store_id);
                reply["head"] = json!(head);
                return respond_timed(request, shared, 200, reply);
            }
            Err(why) => return failed(request, why),
            Ok(None) => {
                if let Err(error)=engine.require_non_media_ephemeral(&principal,id.as_deref()) { return failed(request,error.refusal()); }
                // @ref LLP 3000#http-overload-diagnostics — an ID alone does not deduplicate ephemeral writes, including after schema adoption.
                if payload.get("retry_write").and_then(Json::as_bool) == Some(true) {
                    return failed(request, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "retry_write requires a durable mutation; refresh the operation signature"));
                }
                if let Err(error) = engine.schema().check_delivery(op, fingerprint.as_deref()) {
                    return failed(request, error.refusal());
                }
            }
        }
    }
    // Bearer digests identify sessions without retaining secrets. Dev personas
    // have no session token and each principal is their development session.
    let key = crate::auth::bearer_token(&request)
        .map(|token| snapback4_core::value::digest(&token))
        .unwrap_or_else(|_| format!("persona:{principal}"));
    if !shared.ephemeral_rate.admit(key, shared.ephemeral_rate.now()) {
        let mut why = Refusal::new(
            "E_RATE_LIMIT",
            "auth",
            "ephemeral writes allow 20 requests per second, burst 40; retry later",
        )
        .guide("ephemeral");
        why.retryable = true;
        return failed(request, why);
    }
    let signature = lock(&shared.bound)
        .programs
        .iter()
        .find(|p| p.program.name == op)
        .map(|p| p.program.args.clone());
    let Some(signature) = signature else {
        return failed(
            request,
            Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("unknown operation {op}")),
        );
    };
    let empty = json!({});
    let args_json = payload.get("args").unwrap_or(&empty);
    let Some(fields) = args_json.as_object() else {
        return failed(
            request,
            Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "args must be an object"),

   293	pub(super) fn delivery_fingerprint(payload: &Json) -> Result<Option<String>, Refusal> {
   294	    let Some(delivery) = payload.get("delivery") else { return Ok(None) };
   295	    delivery.get("fingerprint").and_then(Json::as_str).filter(|s| !s.is_empty())
   296	        .map(|s| Some(s.to_owned())).ok_or_else(|| {

   298	            why.site = Some("delivery.fingerprint".into());
   299	            why.rewrite = Some("send delivery: { fingerprint } from the manifest or compiled binding".into());

    let session = &claim.session;
    json!({ "id": run.id, "action": run.action, "args": wire::map_to_json(&run.args), "principal": run.principal, "attempt": run.attempt, "at": run.at, "contract": run.contract, "deadline": run.deadline, "now": claim.now, "session": { "principal": session.principal, "kind": session.kind, "token": session.token, "expiresAt": session.expires_at } })
}

fn arguments(program: &snapback4_core::Program, json: &Json) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, snapback4_core::Value>, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
    let fields = json.as_object().ok_or_else(|| snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Input("action args must be an object".into()))?;
    fields.iter().map(|(name, value)| {
        let kind = program.args.get(name).ok_or_else(|| snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Input(format!("undeclared action argument {name}")))?;
        wire::from_json(kind, value).map(|value| (name.clone(), value)).map_err(snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Input)
    }).collect()
}

pub(super) fn call(request: Request, shared: &Shared, name: &str, body: &str) {
    let principal = match principal_of(&request, shared) { Ok(p) => p.0, Err(why) => return respond(request, 401, json!({ "denied": why })) };
    let payload: Json = match serde_json::from_str(body) { Ok(Json::Object(p)) => Json::Object(p), _ => return respond(request, 400, json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "an action takes { id, args }") })) };
    let Some(id) = payload.get("id").and_then(Json::as_str) else { return respond(request, 400, json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "mint an id on the client and reuse it when retrying this action") })); };
    let admitted = {
        let mut engine = producing_engine(shared);
        if let Err(why) = intent_store(&engine, &payload) {
            let metadata = response_head(&mut engine);
            drop(engine);
            return respond(request, 200, match metadata {
                Ok((store_id, head)) => json!({ "denied": why, "store_id": store_id, "head": head }),
                Err(why) => json!({ "denied": why }),
            });
        }
        let raw_args = payload.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({}));
        match engine.replay_action(id, &principal, name, &raw_args, shared.now()) {
            Ok(Some(run)) => Ok((run, true)),
            Err(error) => Err(error),
            Ok(None) => {
                let args = engine.program(name).filter(|p| p.kind == ProgramKind::Action).ok_or_else(|| snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_ACTION_UNKNOWN", message: format!("no action {name} is declared") })
                    .and_then(|p| arguments(p, &raw_args));
                args.and_then(|args| {
                    let mut context = Context::new(&principal, args).with_wire_args(raw_args).at(shared.now());
                    if let Ok(token) = crate::auth::bearer_token(&request) { context = context.with_session(token); }
                    engine.request_action(name, id, context)
                })
            }
        }
    };
    let (mut run, replayed) = match admitted { Ok(run) => run, Err(error) => { let why = failure(&error); return respond(request, if why.retryable { 503 } else { 200 }, json!({ "denied": why })); } };
    shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine));
    let wait = query_params(request.url()).get("wait").and_then(|s| s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(25).min(25);
    let deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(wait);
    while !matches!(run.state.as_str(), "done" | "failed" | "parked") && Instant::now() < deadline {
        {
            let seq = lock(&shared.seq);
            let _ = shared.committed.wait_timeout(seq, deadline.saturating_duration_since(Instant::now()).min(Duration::from_millis(250))).unwrap_or_else(|p| p.into_inner());
        }

codex
I found two gaps in the HTTP routing: ephemeral mutations bypass both the new persona-argument check and the service-seat formatter. I’m confirming the call paths and line evidence; the new runtime witness calls `Engine::mutate`, which doesn’t exercise that HTTP route.

            epoch,
            incarnation: hub.version.incarnation.clone(),
            tables,
        };
        hub.publish();
        drop(hub);
        drop(tx);
        Ok(Outcome {
            result,
            ephemeral,
            metered,
            seq,

/// A static type, as far as the fragment needs one.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum Ty {
    Scalar(Kind),
    Row(String),
    Unknown,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
struct Col {
        let kinds: Vec<Option<Kind>> = terms.iter().map(|t| t.1.clone()).collect();
        let columns = resolve_key(target, &read.keys, &kinds)?;
        let point = resolve_probe_point(target, &read.keys, &columns)?;
        let mut key = Vec::new();
        for (c, (mut t, _)) in columns.iter().zip(terms) {
            if target.col(c).as_ref().is_some_and(Kind::is_id) {
                id_literal(&mut t);
            }
            key.push(json!([c, t]));
        }
        Ok(json!({"probe": {"table": read.table, "point": point, "key": key}}))
    }
}

/// Retag a text literal term or expression as an id.
fn id_literal(j: &mut Json) {
    if let Some(lit) = j.get_mut("lit") {
        if let Some(text) = lit.get("text").cloned() {
            *lit = json!({"id": text});
        }
    }
}

fn literal_scalar(value: &Literal) -> R<(Json, Option<Kind>)> {
    Ok(match value {
        Literal::Str(s) => (json!({"text": s}), Some(Kind::Text(None))),
        Literal::Number(n) => match n.parse::<i64>() {
            Ok(v) => (json!({"int": v.to_string()}), Some(Kind::Int)),
            Err(_) => return no("number-literal", n.clone()),
        },
        Literal::Bool(b) => (json!({"bool": b}), Some(Kind::Bool)),
        Literal::Null => (json!({"null": null}), None),
    })
}

/// Whether a column of kind `col` accepts an argument of kind `arg` in a
/// positional key (indexes card: "exactly one declared unique key must
/// accept the argument types in order").
fn accepts(col: &Kind, arg: &Kind) -> bool {
    match (col, arg) {
        (Kind::Int, Kind::Int) | (Kind::Bool, Kind::Bool) => true,
        (Kind::Text(_), Kind::Text(_)) => true,
        (Kind::Id(a), Kind::Id(b)) => a == b || matches!((a, b), (IdSort::Plain, IdSort::Ref(_)) | (IdSort::Ref(_), IdSort::Plain)),
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// Named columns for a key: named parts as written; a single `id`- or
/// `t`-typed positional part is `id`; otherwise the one declared unique key
/// of the same arity whose column types accept the arguments in order.
fn resolve_key(table: &Table, keys: &[KeyPart], kinds: &[Option<Kind>]) -> R<Vec<String>> {
    if keys.iter().all(|k| k.column.is_some()) {
        let names: Vec<String> = keys.iter().map(|k| k.column.clone().unwrap()).collect();
        for n in &names {
            if n.contains('.') || table.col(n).is_none() {
                return no("key-column", format!("{}[{n}]", table.name));
            }
        }
        return Ok(names);
    }
    if keys.iter().any(|k| k.column.is_some()) {
        return no("key-mixed", format!("{}[..]", table.name));
    }
    if kinds.iter().any(Option::is_none) {
        return no("key-untyped", format!("{}[..]", table.name));
    }
    let kinds: Vec<Kind> = kinds.iter().map(|k| k.clone().unwrap()).collect();
    if let [Kind::Id(sort)] = kinds.as_slice() {
        if matches!(sort, IdSort::Plain) || matches!(sort, IdSort::Ref(t) if *t == table.name) {
            return Ok(vec!["id".into()]);
        }
    }
    let fits: Vec<&Vec<String>> = table
        .uniques
        .iter()
        .filter(|u| u.len() == kinds.len() && u.iter().zip(&kinds).all(|(c, k)| table.col(c).is_some_and(|ck| accepts(&ck, k))))
        .collect();
    match fits.as_slice() {
        [one] => Ok((*one).clone()),
        _ => no("key-unresolved", format!("{}[..] matches {} unique keys", table.name, fits.len())),
    }
}

/// The unique key selected by a rule probe. Named equalities may contain
/// residual predicates beyond the point key; the binder selects the shortest
/// covered total unique, then the first declared candidate. Positional probes
/// keep their declared-only, type-directed resolution above.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#14 — nullable residual membership equalities are distinct from the selected point key.
fn resolve_probe_point(table: &Table, keys: &[KeyPart], columns: &[String]) -> R<Vec<String>> {
    if keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_none()) {
        return Ok(columns.to_vec());
    }
    if columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id") {
        return Ok(vec!["id".into()]);
    }
    let total = |unique: &[String]| {
        unique.iter().all(|column| {
            column == "id"
                || table
                    .cols
                    .iter()
                    .find(|candidate| candidate.name == *column)
                    .is_some_and(|candidate| !candidate.nullable)
        })

**NOT READY**

1. **Ephemeral HTTP refusals bypass the service-seat formatter.** The formatter is applied by [Engine::mutate](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/mod.rs:864), but the actual HTTP route calls `mutate_ephemeral` directly and converts errors with `e.refusal()` in [serve_ephemeral.rs](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve_ephemeral.rs:249), also at line 269. Service callers therefore receive E_RULE without §8’s required static clause. Route both error conversions through the shared formatter and add an HTTP regression.

2. **The development persona guard misses ephemeral mutations and actions.** [serve.rs](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve.rs:1175) dispatches ephemeral mutations before the guard at line 1210; their [argument decoder](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve_ephemeral.rs:203) performs no persona check. Actions likewise [decode directly](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/actions.rs:76). A declared bare persona can consequently execute or enqueue these operations instead of receiving §8’s E_INPUT guidance. Apply the existing dev-only guard to both routes and cover them with dev/production HTTP regressions.
